# Full-domain two-channel spiral guarantees collapse to Huber

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL CANDIDATE, UNVERIFIED.
Worker max2, assigned analytical round52. TASK.md and originating
master_guidance71ea82aa7acc7e5a authorize this one round, with all
computational and formal gates closed. Plan:ea2096c2f303efcc.
No fact_submit is authorized or made, and no new accepted fact is claimed.

This document defines a NEW scalar functional L_wide. It preserves the
old name L_sp and its restricted domain unchanged. All new algebra needed
below, including the reused hierarchy and mass-deletion ingredients, is
proved jointly here. The sole accepted mathematical input is
fact04219554e41d9750, for the original benchmark ray and saved enclosure.
The unverified geometric construction and independent analytical audit
are provenance, never correctness premises in this scalar proof.

The conclusion is an upper bound for two explicit lower-guarantee
formulas, not for the actual worst-case error W. It does not bound the
stronger exact closest-radius formula(6) of the geometric source, nor
the source-format realization after a closest-minimizer improvement.

## 1. Exact enlarged scalar definition

The immutable geometric source is finding c8fcf4ced5a34046 at
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/c8fcf4ced5a34046.md,
SHA2564d1e99db853eba3751a3f4761fc2c400c68eacdc9c7912b17c0b0c82d6975d2e.
Its formulas(1),(2),(7),(8) define the following numbers, without needing
any assertion about a function realizing them.

Take independently

    0<epsilon<=1/2, kappa>0.

For every such pair set

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    C=c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp),
    d_sp=1-kappa^2,
    T_sp=(exp(d_sp*theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp, 0<kappa<1,
    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                   kappa>=1,
    H_sp=C*[1+(1+kappa)*T_sp]-1,
    A=A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon,
    a_sp=2*A/C.                                           (1)

C and A are scoped abbreviations, not the accepted benchmark C_n or a
primitive-certificate capacity. The definition includes kappa=1 using
the second branch, with no division by d_sp there. Define exactly

    qmax_sp=1,                                       kappa<=epsilon,
    qmax_sp=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
              *exp(kappa*[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]), kappa>epsilon,
    N_sp=max{0,kappa*qmax_sp-epsilon},
    B_sp=1+max{epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon}.                  (2)

No simplification of the general-kappa horizontal-normal price N_sp is
assumed. In particular, this definition does not silently substitute the
smaller old threshold when kappa>1.

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any finite nonnegative word at its original
physical length n. A nonempty subset of its original physical indices,
listed in increasing order, induces the actual decomposition

    h=(block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),

where k>=1 and s_i is the ordinary sum of block_i. Empty blocks and
zero physical entries are permitted. One parameter pair is fixed for
the entire selection. Define the two distinct scalar channels

    Phi_M=1/(1+2*s_k)*product_(i=0)^(k-1)
             [(b_i-1)/(C*s_i+max{epsilon*b_i,A})]^2,
             eligible when every b_i>=B_sp;                (3)

    Phi_S=1/[2*(1+s_k)]*product_(i=0)^(k-1)
             [b_i/(C*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b_i)]^2,
             eligible when every b_i>=2*(1+epsilon^(-2)).  (4)

Equation(4) uses C*(a_sp+s_i)=2*A+C*s_i exactly. Its numerator is b_i,
not b_i-1. Its terminal denominator is 2*(1+s_k), not 1+2*s_k.

Write H_base(h)=1/(1+2*sum h). Define

    L_wide(h)=max{H_base(h),
                 sup Phi_M over all pairs and eligible nonempty subsets,
                 sup Phi_S over all pairs and eligible nonempty subsets}. (5)

The supremum of an empty family in(5) means0. The supremum notation does
not presume parameter attainment. No product mixes factors from different
channels. The Huber term is included explicitly; hence the equality
proved below is attained by that term. All constants and denominators in
(1)--(5) are positive on their stated domains.

The source describes(4) as an exact payoff normalized by its specified
minimizer0 and initial distance1. We retain that convention. Interpreting
(3) or(4) as lower bounds for actual W remains conditional on establishing
the full geometry, which is not part of this scalar candidate.

## 2. Word family and the quantitative claims

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2). For a fixed real tau>0 and integer m>tau define

    beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda*(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    H_(m,tau)=H_m, n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.                (6)

Every child keeps the SAME enclosing parameters(m,tau). A level is the
index j of a bridge 1+a*c^j, not the physical index of one occurrence.
Only tau=1 is the original diagnostic family; the theorem uses the
different words specified by small tau in(6).

The joint claims to be proved are as follows. For EVERY real
0<tau<=1/4, EVERY integer m>=1536, EVERY parameter pair in(1), and EVERY
nonempty subset of the physical word H_(m,tau), one has

    Phi_M<=1/(1+2*S_m),
    Phi_S<=5/[16*(1+S_m)].                               (7)

In(7) the formulas can even be evaluated on ineligible subsets; the
proof bounds that larger comparison class. Therefore(7) applies, in
particular, to each channel's own actual threshold, including equality.
Consequently

    L_wide(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2*S_m).                       (8)

Retain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark, defined for integers n>=0 by

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2,
    G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=i<n) Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=i<n) G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                      (9)

Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives the positive finite limit

    B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1.                         (10)

For every fixed0<tau<=1/4 the further claim is

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))
                         =Z_1/(2*exp(tau)).            (11)

In fact, the strict benchmark deficits already hold at EVERY m>=1536:

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,1/4))<237/250<1,
    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))<79/80 for1/5<=tau<=1/4.  (11a)

The corresponding limits are strictly below the same constants. These
claims use only the accepted benchmark enclosure, rederived from its
saved endpoint in Section8. No new benchmark calculation is performed.

## 3. Joint hierarchy identities and the selection mechanism

For fixed(m,tau), put

    r=c*(c-2), v=a/(c-2)=beta/r,
    M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a*c^j, b_j=1+d_j,
    u_j=d_j/M_j, M_max=M_(m-1).                         (12)

Writing x=tau/m and gamma=4+2*sqrt(2), direct expansion gives

    r=1+gamma*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta>0.                 (13)

Thus0<v<1 and c>lambda>2. The sum recurrence is
M_(j+1)=2*M_j+a*c^j with M_0=1. Substitution, using a=v*(c-2), proves
by induction that

    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)*2^j, 0<=j<=m.                     (14)

It follows that

    1<=M_j<=c^j,
    0<u_j<=c-2,
    M_j<=M_max for0<=j<m.                              (15)

For the first two inequalities use c>2 and M_j>=v*c^j. For the last,
the sum recurrence has positive added terms and M_j>0.

Choose ANY currently selected checkpoint having minimum selected level
j. Its immediate complete H_j children contain only levels<j, so neither
child contains a selected checkpoint. If s and t are the actual ordinary
sums immediately before and after this checkpoint, then

    s>=S_j, t>=S_j.                                   (16)

This includes a first or last selected checkpoint: its adjacent ordinary
block is then the true initial or terminal block. Tied minimum levels
cause no problem because each child has strictly smaller levels.

Deleting a checkpoint below means deleting it from the selected subset,
not from the physical word. Its full physical step b is reclassified as
ordinary, so the two ordinary blocks merge to the sum s+b+t. All other
physical indices, step values, and the one parameter pair are retained.
Eligibility of any remaining checkpoint is unchanged.

We prove one minimum-level deletion scheme for both channels under the
same whole-word alternatives

    (I)  C>=r=c*(c-2),
    (II) A>=2*C*M_max,                                (17)

assuming C>=1, A>=2*C and2<c<=5/2. Section7 proves these assumptions
and the dichotomy for the ENTIRE enlarged parameter domain. The two
channel comparisons below are separate; neither is inferred from a
pointwise ordering of(3) and(4).

## 4. Mass channel: exact interior and terminal comparisons

Let b=b_j=1+d be a selected minimum-level checkpoint, with
M=M_j, S=M-1, u=d/M. Then0<u<=1/2 and s,t>=S.
For this channel write Q_M(b)=max{epsilon*b,A}.

If a next selected checkpoint b' exists, put R=Q_M(b'). Cancelling its
positive numerator b'-1 and all unchanged factors shows that deletion
does not lower Phi_M exactly when

    d*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q_M(b))*(C*t+R).           (18)

The right side minus the left is increasing in Q_M(b). Its derivatives
with respect to R,s,t are, respectively,

    C*s+Q_M(b)-d,
    C*(C*t+R-d),
    C*(C*s+Q_M(b)-d).

They are positive throughout s,t>=M-1 and Q_M(b),R>=A: indeed
C*(M-1)+A>=C*M>=M>=2*d. Thus its minimum is at
s=t=M-1 and Q_M(b)=R=A. Put z_M=(A-C)/M>=0. Dividing that minimum by
M^2 gives the exact expression

    E_M=C^2-C*u*(2+u)+z_M*(2*C-u)+z_M^2.              (19)

Under(I), u*(2+u)<=c*(c-2)<=C, so every grouped term in(19) is
nonnegative. Under(II),

    z_M=(A-C)/M>=C*(2*M_max-1)/M>=C,

because M<=M_max and M_max>=1. Therefore

    E_M>=C^2-(5/4)*C+C*(2*C-1/2)+C^2
        =4*C^2-(7/4)*C>0.                            (20)

Here u*(2+u)<=5/4 and2*C-u>0 justify each replacement. This proves
the interior deletion comparison in either branch of(17).

If b is the LAST selected checkpoint, the exact comparison is instead

    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]
      <=(C*s+Q_M(b))^2*(1+2*t).                      (21)

Since C*s+Q_M(b)>=s+1, it suffices to bound

    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]/[(s+1)^2*(1+2*t)].              (22)

For fixed s this decreases in t, because the fraction without d^2 is
[1+2*(s+b)/(1+2*t)]/(s+1)^2. Its s derivative after suppressing the
positive constant1+2*t is -2*(s+2*b+2*t)/(s+1)^3<0. Hence(22) is
largest at s=t=M-1. There it equals

    u^2*[(4+2*u)*M-1]/(2*M-1)
      <=u^2*(3+2*u)<=1.                              (23)

For the first inequality, the difference of the cross-multiplied
numerators is2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. For the second, u^2*(3+2*u) increases
on[0,1/2] and equals1 at1/2. This terminal comparison needs no
additional choice between(I) and(II).

Repeatedly deleting a currently selected minimum level strictly reduces
the number of selections. The comparisons keep Phi_M nondecreasing
until the empty-selection value1/(1+2*S_m) is reached. This proves
the first inequality in(7) under(17). It is a proof for an arbitrary
finite subset, not an executed recurrence or enumeration.

## 5. Source-format channel: the numerator shift is paid explicitly

Keep b=1+d, M=M_j, u=d/M, and s,t>=M-1 from Section4. Now define

    Q_S(b)=2*A+epsilon*b.

Its numerator is b itself. If a next selected checkpoint b' exists,
write R=Q_S(b'). The exact interior deletion comparison, after cancelling
the unchanged positive b', is

    b*[C*(s+b+t)+R] <= (C*s+Q_S(b))*(C*t+R).           (24)

The difference is increasing in Q_S(b), and its R,s,t derivatives are

    C*s+Q_S(b)-b,
    C*(C*t+R-b),
    C*(C*s+Q_S(b)-b),

respectively. All are positive on the larger comparison region
s,t>=M-1, Q_S(b),R>=2*A. In fact,

    C*(M-1)+2*A-b
      >=C*M+3*C-(M/2+1)>0,                           (25)

using A>=2*C, C>=1, M>=1 and d<=M/2. Therefore the smallest difference
in(24) occurs at s=t=M-1 and Q_S(b)=R=2*A. Define

    q=2*A-C>=3*C.

The minimum is EXACTLY

    F_S=(C*M+q)^2-(d+1)*[C*(2*M+d)+q]
       =M^2*[C^2-C*u*(2+u)]
         +M*[q*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)]+q^2-q.               (26)

For the first equality, note that the merged physical sum is
2*(M-1)+(d+1)=2*M+d-1, so adding R=2*A yields
C*(2*M+d)+q. The extra1 in b=d+1 is retained in every term of(26).

Under(I), the M^2 coefficient in(26) is nonnegative. Also

    q*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)
      >=3*C*(2*C-u)-C*(2+u)
       =C*(6*C-4*u-2)>=2*C>0,                       (27)

and q^2-q>=0 because q>=3*C>=3. Thus(24) holds in branch(I).

Under(II), use the first expression in(26). Put z_S=q/M. Since

    q=2*A-C>=C*(4*M_max-1)>=3*C*M,

one has z_S>=3*C. Moreover(d+1)/M<=3/2 and(2*M+d)/M<=5/2. Consequently

    F_S/M^2
      >=(C+z_S)^2-(3/2)*[(5/2)*C+z_S]
       =C^2-(15/4)*C+z_S*(2*C-3/2)+z_S^2
      >=16*C^2-(33/4)*C>0.                          (28)

The last step uses C>=1, so2*C-3/2>0 and substitution z_S>=3*C has
the claimed direction. Its final expression is positive for C>=1.
This proves the interior source-format comparison for both branches.

For a LAST selected checkpoint, deletion replaces the terminal factor
1/[2*(1+t)] by1/[2*(1+s+b+t)]. The exact ratio of the selected
local factor to the deleted local factor is

    R_tail=b^2*(1+s+b+t)/[(C*s+Q_S(b))^2*(1+t)].       (29)

Because Q_S(b)>=2*A>=4*C and C>=1, its denominator satisfies
C*s+Q_S(b)>=s+4. The resulting upper bound for(29) is

    b^2*(1+s+b+t)/[(s+4)^2*(1+t)].                   (30)

For fixed s, the fraction without b^2 equals
[1+(s+b)/(1+t)]/(s+4)^2 and decreases with t. Its derivative in s,
with the positive constant1+t suppressed, is

    (2-s-2*b-2*t)/(s+4)^3<0,

since b=1+d>1. Thus(30) is largest at s=t=M-1, where

    R_tail
      <=(d+1)^2*(2*M+d)/[(M+3)^2*M]
      <=(5/2)*[(M/2+1)/(M+3)]^2
      <=5/8.                                       (31)

For the last two inequalities use d<=M/2 and
M/2+1<=(M+3)/2. This is a uniform quantitative terminal contraction.
It has not been replaced by the mass-channel terminal comparison.

## 6. Finite pruning and the two different empty-selection values

For the source channel only, define the empty-product bookkeeping value
to be1/[2*(1+S_m)]. This is the value of its own displayed formula after
all checkpoints are removed from the subset. It is a comparison sink;
the functional(5) retains its actual Huber baseline1/(1+2*S_m).

Start with any nonempty selected subset, and repeatedly choose a minimum
currently selected level. At an interior deletion use(24); at a terminal
deletion use(31). Every remaining subset still has the complete children
property(16). At least one deletion is terminal, since the last remaining
checkpoint is terminal. All factors outside the local comparison are
unchanged and positive. Multiplying the comparisons therefore gives

    Phi_S<=(5/8)*1/[2*(1+S_m)]
         =5/[16*(1+S_m)].                            (32)

If additional deletions are terminal, each contributes another factor
at most5/8; only one such factor was needed in(32). The mass pruning in
Section4 gives its own bound1/(1+2*S_m). Finally

    5/[16*(1+S_m)]<1/(1+2*S_m),                      (33)

because cross multiplication reduces to5*(1+2*S_m)<16*(1+S_m).
Thus under the hypotheses and dichotomy(17), both channels are bounded
by the Huber baseline, and that baseline attains L_wide.

No pointwise comparison between Phi_S and Phi_M was used. The extra1
in the source numerator is absorbed in the q-dependent terms(26)--(28),
and the terminal factor is handled by(29)--(31). In particular, merely
copying the old b-1 proof would not prove the source-channel assertion.

The proof also covers level0, whose children are empty and M_0=1,
and arbitrary ties between selected levels. The bounds were proved on
the larger class of all nonempty subsets of this positive word, so there
is no lost threshold case, including B_sp at kappa>1 or equality at
either actual eligibility threshold. No ordinary block is replaced by
an envelope, alternative ordering, or child with a new parameter m.

## 7. The deletion dichotomy holds on the entire enlarged domain

Assume0<tau<=1/4 and m>=1536. First2<c<5/2. To give rational bounds,
sqrt(2)<10/7 implies lambda<17/7; for m>=9,

    c<=lambda*(1+1/(4*m))
      <(17/7)*(37/36)=629/252<5/2.                   (34)

Also gamma=4+2*sqrt(2)<7 and lambda^2=3+2*sqrt(2)<6, so m>=2 and(13)
give

    r-1<7/(4*m)+3/(8*m^2)
       <=31/(16*m)<2/m.                              (35)

For EVERY pair0<epsilon<=1/2,kappa>0, one has C>1. Both branches of
T_sp in(1) are positive. Therefore, without any restriction kappa<1,

    A=epsilon+(C/epsilon)*[1+(1+kappa)*T_sp]
      >=C/epsilon>=2*C.                              (36)

This includes kappa=1 and every kappa>1. The source's piecewise T_sp
and threshold price remain unchanged.

If C>=r, alternative(I) applies immediately. Consider ANY remaining
pair with C<r. Since C>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2), equations(35) and m>=2 imply

    epsilon^2<=C^2-1<r^2-1
      <4/m+4/m^2<=6/m<=1/256.                       (37)

Thus epsilon<1/16. Using pi>3 and arctan(epsilon)<epsilon gives

    theta_sp=pi/2-arctan(epsilon)>23/16>1.            (38)

For completeness pi>3 follows by comparing the unit circle circumference
with its inscribed regular hexagon, and arctan(epsilon)<epsilon follows
by integrating1/(1+t^2)<1 for positive t. Neither uses a decimal value.

The SAME assumption C<r yields, directly from the formula for C,

    kappa*theta_sp<=log C<log r<r-1<2/m.              (39)

Since theta_sp>1 and m>=1536, this implies

    kappa<2/m<=1/768<1/16.

In particular kappa<1 has now been proved in precisely the branch
where the exponential expression for T_sp will be used. Put

    X=(1-kappa^2)*theta_sp/kappa.

From(38)--(39),

    X>((1-kappa^2)*theta_sp^2/2)*m
      >(255/256)*(529/256)*(m/2)
       =(134895/131072)*m>m.                         (40)

For0<kappa<1, the formula in(1) simplifies exactly to

    1+(1+kappa)*T_sp=(exp(X)-kappa)/(1-kappa)
                    >=exp(X).                       (41)

Indeed subtracting exp(X) gives
kappa*(exp(X)-1)/(1-kappa)>=0. Combining(37),(40),(41) yields

    A>=C*exp(X)/epsilon>16*C*exp(m).                  (42)

The positive exponential series gives exp(1)>1+1+1/2=5/2>c.
Together with(15),

    M_max<=c^(m-1)<exp(m),
    A>16*C*exp(m)>2*C*M_max.                         (43)

This is alternative(II). Hence every parameter pair is covered by
(I) or(II), and Sections4--6 prove(7)--(8).

This is a finite uniform argument: no limits or optimized constants
K=m*kappa and E=m*epsilon were assumed. It includes every horizon-
dependent scaling, oscillatory choices, fixed positive curvature, both
sides of kappa=epsilon, both sides of kappa=1, and epsilon=1/2. Within
the stated small-tau family there is no remaining parameter region.

The precise changes from the old-domain proof are now visible. Its
small-C branch used kappa<=epsilon<1/16 to bound1-kappa^2. Here(39)
forces kappa<1/16 directly. It also used its exponential formula for A
throughout the old domain. Here the universal bound(36) replaces that
use outside the small-C branch, and(39) independently justifies the
exponential branch wherever(41) is used. No ordering of kappa and epsilon
enters any deletion comparison. The new source-channel comparisons in
Section5 additionally pay its different numerator and terminal factor.

## 8. Original benchmark: saved endpoint, finite deficit, and exact limit

Only the following part of accepted fact04219554e41d9750 is used:

    lambda^(-j)*B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
      <=lambda^(-j)*(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2, j>=0.     (44)

Its conditional four-step seed construction is not a premise. The
relevant statement and Sections7--8 of that accepted fact were read.
Its Section8 fixes the saved table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
The hash and saved rows1023,1024 were read back; neither the generator
nor any benchmark or exact-check program was executed.

With D80=1208925819614629174706176, its row1024 gives

                    <15910*D80
                     =19234009790068750169575260160. (45)

where(3363*99+2378*140)-6725*99=82>0. Apply(44) at j=10. Using(45),

All terms in the second summand are nonnegative. At tau=1/4, the first

       for EVERY1/5<=tau<=1/4 and integer m>=1536.    (53)

## 10. Final theorem of the joint candidate

THEOREM (UNVERIFIED analytical candidate). Let L_wide be exactly(1)--(5),
including the piecewise T_sp, the mass threshold B_sp with its full N_sp
price, and the separate source threshold2*(1+epsilon^(-2)). Let H_(m,tau)
be exactly(6), with unchanged enclosing(m,tau) in every child. For every
real0<tau<=1/4 and integer m>=1536, simultaneously for all kappa>0,
0<epsilon<=1/2 and all respective eligible nonempty chronological
checkpoint subsets,

    Phi_M<=1/(1+2*S_m),
    Phi_S<=5/[16*(1+S_m)],
    L_wide(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2*S_m).

For the original all-split benchmark(9) at the actual physical length
n_m=2^m-1, every such m satisfies

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,1/4))<237/250<1.

Uniformly for all1/5<=tau<=1/4 and the same m>=1536 one also has

    B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))<79/80<1.

For each fixed0<tau<=1/4 the exact limit is

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*L_wide(H_(m,tau))
                      =Z_1/(2*exp(tau)),

where Z_1 is the accepted positive benchmark ray limit from
fact04219554e41d9750. These are statements only about the defined scalar

PROOF. The same minimum-level selection deletion mechanism in

# Signed clipping energy: exact one-sided profile and a complete large-ratio domain

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL REPORT.** Project
`gd-exact-profile-20260926`, worker max3, invocation76 after baseline75,
completed ceiling76 / attempted ceiling77, controller cap1. Every new
mathematical statement below is part of this single unverified draft.
No fact_submit or other formal call was authorized or made.

The new scoped conclusion is an exact all-amplitude profile on an
unbounded, explicit clipping-ratio domain. For each SAME enclosing
integer m>=1536, with the parameters and full physical word defined
below, put

    Gamma_m = sqrt(2*a*d_(m-1)*D_m).

For every two positive clipping levels u,v satisfying

    max(u,v)/min(u,v) >= Gamma_m,                     (T)

and the ONE static unit-core function with derivative

    g(x)=clip(x,[-v,u]),

the candidate theorem proved jointly here is

    sup_(s!=0) 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 = 1/D_m.             (P)

The supremum includes both initial signs and EVERY real amplitude.
Both saturated rays attain it at s=D_m*u and s=-D_m*v. Condition(T)
allows arbitrary dependence of the ratio on m, including divergence
at any rate above Gamma_m and its reflected reciprocal tending to0.
The proof does not assume a bounded number of sign changes, discard
returns, truncate either child, or drop the final physical leaf.

A second part of the same threshold-conditioned argument evaluates the
one-sided clipping function g_infty(x)=max(x,-v), which still has unit
curvature on its entire positive ray and unique minimum0. Its exact
all-amplitude profile on H_m is also1/D_m. This is NOT a flat-curvature
case. An exact asymmetric capture rectangle and a two-endpoint plateau
are derived for it. They also give a finite-clipping, arbitrary-amplitude
result whenever u>=d_(m-1)*v and the initial state s<=u (all negative
starts included).

Mechanism1, a signed primitive-energy envelope, fails on an explicit
actual root-crossing trajectory: |x_n+S_m*g_n|>|s|. The full energy
expression and all guards are given in Section4. Its terminal objective
is below1/D_m, so this is an obstruction to that stronger envelope, not
a counterexample to(P). Mechanism2 retains exact threshold-conditioned
histories and produces(T).

The unrestricted finite-ratio family remains OPEN. In particular, this
report does not evaluate every ratio between the earlier uniform band
and Gamma_m. It does not exclude all scalar functions, activate two
independent core curvatures, or prove an upper bound on the full W.
The original every-n/every-h minimax target remains OPEN.

## 1. Original scope and provenance

For a predetermined finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))
and fixed L>0, the original iteration is

    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)*grad F(x_i),
    W_n(h)=sup 2*(F(x_n)-F(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2).

The supremum is over every finite Euclidean dimension, every finite
convex differentiable globally L-smooth F with an attained minimum,
and every permitted initial point and minimizer. Write R_n(h)=W_n(h)/2.
The target is W_n^star/U_n^ZJ ->1 or a rigorous negative resolution,
with W_n^star=inf_(h>=0) W_n(h) and the original all-integer-split
benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. No original quantifier is modified here.

Accepted fact_id `931b9653ce0f7201` was read in full. It supplies the
unconditional comparison B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50 for the SAME hierarchy
below. Its proposed full-class bound17/(16D_m) is explicitly unproved.
Only its unconditional benchmark comparison is used in Section10.
No other accepted input is needed for the new mathematical proofs.
The project glossary was inspected; hierarchy a,c,H_j,A_j and local
energy functions below are scoped notation, not replacements of
unrelated project symbols with the same letters.

The complete prior joint report `05423f48b823e3bf` was read as awareness.
Its ratio-band theorem is not imported as an accepted premise. Every
premise needed for(T) and(P) is proved jointly below. In particular,
no ratio-free extension of its weighted capture or common-radius
plateau is assumed. TASK.md and guidance `4455d6e36806d677` govern this
invocation; the subsequent `231e15a7ee479cc6` leaves this allocation
unchanged. Latest guidance `5fee1ea5f63fd8fc` was also read and explicitly
continues max3 invocation76 under its existing cap. The recorded plan is
`a50f743debae0e74`.

## 2. Same-enclosing-m word and all parameter guards

Fix any integer m>=1536 ONCE. Define

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_j=1+2*S_j=2*A_j-1.

All children retain this exact m,epsilon,beta,c,a. H_j has2^j-1 steps.
The largest PHYSICAL bridge parameter is d_*=d_(m-1); d_m is only
an auxiliary ruler. Let ell=1+a be the first and last physical step,
and b=a*beta. Every step is greater than1, and every bridge of level
at least1 is immediately preceded and followed by a leaf ell. This
last property follows inductively from the literal concatenation;
there are no adjacent nonleaf bridges.

Here epsilon<=1/6144<1/100, beta>99/100, and

    2<c<(49/20)*(101/100)<5/2,
    1/3<99/250<a<1/lambda<1/2,
    0<b<1/2, c*b=beta^2<1.

The c bound uses sqrt(2)<29/20. Set eta=a/(c-2). Since
lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda, one has c*(c-2)>1 and0<eta<1.
Summing the literal word gives

    A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,
    d_j/A_j <= c-2 <1/2,                            (2.1)
    A_j<c^j for j>=1.

The closed expression has initial value1 and the correct update because
2*eta+a=c*eta. The ratio in(2.1) is strictly increasing in j: its
reciprocal is

    A_j/d_j = 1/(c-2)+((1-eta)/a)*(2/c)^j.            (2.2)

Two placement guards will be used repeatedly. First,

    A_j-d_j>ell for every j>=1.                      (2.3)

Indeed A_j-d_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*(3-c)*c^j. Both coefficients
are positive and both powers increase with j. At j=1 its value is
2+a-beta>1+a=ell. In particular a*d_j<A_j-ell.

Second, for every2<=j<=m,

    d_j>A_(j-1),
    (c-1)*d_(j-1)-A_(j-1)<0.                        (2.4)

At j=2,

    c*(d_2-A_1)=2*epsilon-lambda^2*epsilon^2*(1+epsilon)>0,

since lambda^2<6 and6*epsilon*(1+epsilon)<2. If d_j>A_(j-1),
then d_(j+1)-A_j=(c-1/c)*d_j-2*A_(j-1)>0, because
c-1/c>lambda-1/lambda=2. The second inequality in(2.4) follows
from(2.1) and(c-1)*(c-2)<(3/2)*(1/2)<1.

At the final level,

    d_m^2>D_m.                                      (2.5)

To see this, a>1/3 and A_m<c^m imply d_m^2>c^(2m)/9, while
D_m<2*c^m. Since c^m>2^m>=32>18 for m>=5, the first quantity
exceeds the second. All advertised m satisfy this.

Finally Gamma_m>d_*>1. The latter follows from d_2=beta*c>1 and
m-1>=2. With A=A_(m-1), d=d_*, one has d<A/2 and
D_m=4*A+2*d-1>4*A, since d>1. Thus
2*a*D_m>(8/3)*A>d, proving Gamma_m^2>d^2. These estimates justify
every use of an inactive positive clipping threshold under(T).

## 3. Static functions, complete queries, minima, and every row

For u,v>0 let

    g(x)=clip(x,[-v,u]), f(x)=x*g(x)-g(x)^2/2.

Equivalently f is x^2/2 on[-v,u], is u*x-u^2/2 on x>=u,
and is -v*x-v^2/2 on x<=-v. Its derivative g is continuous,
nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. Partitioning an interval at the two
thresholds proves the Lipschitz assertion directly. Consequently f
is finite, convex, differentiable, globally1-smooth and has the
complete minimum set{0}, with f(0)=0. For general L set F=L*f.
The original updates with steps h_i/L then coincide with

    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i),
    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), 0<=i<=n.                  (3.1)

These specify ALL physical queries and the original terminal on one
fixed function. The initial radius about the complete minimum is
exactly |s|. All theorem statements cover this full literal trajectory,
not an auxiliary trajectory with freely chosen gradients.

Let y_i=x_i-g_i. Direct substitution of the displayed f gives every
ordered interpolation residual, including terminal and optimum,

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2
        =y_j*(g_i-g_j)<=0.                          (3.2)

If y_j>0, then g_j=u and g_i<=u. If y_j<0, then g_j=-v and
g_i>=-v. If y_j=0 the residual is zero. This proves both orders,
all remote rows, threshold equalities and repeated points. At the
optimum x=g=f=0 the same formula applies. The complete Gram matrix
of the initial displacement and all gradients is an ordinary scalar
Euclidean Gram: every quadratic form is the square of a linear
combination of these real numbers, hence is nonnegative. Scaling by
L gives the original L-smooth residuals without changing the score.

The one-sided comparison function is

    g_infty(x)=max(x,-v),
    f_infty(x)=x^2/2 for x>=-v,
               -v*x-v^2/2 for x<=-v.                (3.3)

It has the same regularity, complete minimum{0}, true radius |s|,
physical query definition(3.1), and all residuals(3.2). For this
function y_j is either0 or negative, so the proof of(3.2) is unchanged.
Its positive curvature is1, not0. All its definitions are explicit;
no limit interchange u->infinity is invoked.

For either function f(x)<=x^2/2 everywhere. On a clipped ray the
difference is (|x|-b_side)^2/2. Reflection swaps u and v, changes the
sign of every iterate, and preserves the score. We may therefore
assume u>=v when proving(T), and reflect the complete conclusion.

For reference retain BOTH signed endpoints. For a finite-clipping
step1+d, the exact image of[-p,q], p,q>=0, is[-p',q'], with

    p'=max(d*min(q,u), p-(1+d)*v),
    q'=max(d*min(p,v), q-(1+d)*u).                   (3.4)

The first terms are nonnegative. The opposite-sign extremum occurs
at the included threshold or endpoint on each ray; the remaining
same-sign extremum occurs at the outer endpoint. Continuity fills
the interval. Thus this formula includes all threshold equalities.
If Psi_j denotes the whole H_j map and Phi_j its signed interval
map, then Phi_0 is the identity and

    Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j o E_(d_j) o Phi_j.               (3.5)

Each Phi_j is coordinatewise nondecreasing by interval inclusion.
All histories in(3.5) are compatible images of one continuous map;
there is no independent choice of child maxima. For(3.3), the exact
one-step formula becomes

    p'=max(d*q,p-(1+d)*v), q'=d*min(p,v).             (3.6)

These formulas are used as symbolic identities only. No trajectory
or recurrence was run as a program.

## 4. Mechanism1: a genuine signed-energy obstruction

One tempting ratio-free primitive envelope is

    |Psi_m(s)+S_m*g(Psi_m(s))| <= |s|.               (4.1)

For this clipped function its squared left side equals the signed
objective/gradient expression

    x_n^2+2*S_m*f_n+S_m*(S_m+1)*g_n^2,

because f_n=x_n*g_n-g_n^2/2. Thus(4.1) retains the actual clipped
objective weight and is stronger than a bare radius claim. It would
imply the desired objective bound: on either ray, maximizing
2f(x)/(x+S_m*g(x))^2 gives1/D_m by completing the saturated-ray
square, while the core ratio is1/A_m^2<=1/D_m.

Nevertheless(4.1) is false for every advertised m. Put

    A=A_(m-1), d=d_(m-1), S=A-1,
    choose u>0, v=d*u/A, s=A*u.                     (4.2)

Here0<v<u since d/A<1/2. Every query of the first child is
x=u*(A-P), where P is its actual prefix mass, and that child ends
at u. The root sends u to-d*u=-A*v. Every query of the second
child is x=-v*(A-Q), where Q is its actual prefix mass. The ACTUAL
terminal after its last leaf is -v. All queried saturated guards hold
because both children end at their threshold and all earlier prefix
masses are smaller. The function, rows, complete minimum and radius
are those of Section3, with own radius A*u.

To check the strict violation, first prove d/A>1/lambda. At level2,

    lambda*d_2-A_2
      =epsilon*(1+4/[lambda*(1+epsilon)]-lambda^2*epsilon)>0.

Here lambda^2*epsilon<6/6144<1. The ratios d_j/A_j increase by(2.2),
so the same strict comparison holds at m-1>=2. If t=d/A, then

    |x_n+S_m*g_n|/|s|=A_m*d/A^2=t*(2+t)>1,           (4.3)

because (1/lambda)*(2+1/lambda)=1. This includes the small-side
clipping weight exactly. It is not a claim that the objective itself
increases beyond the target: this trajectory has score d^2/A^4, and

    D_m*d^2/A^4 < (5*A-1)/(4*A^2)<1                 (4.4)

since d/A<1/2, D_m<5*A-1 and A>2. The latter inequality follows
from4*A^2-5*A+1=(4*A-1)*(A-1)>0 for A>2.

A second direct energy-only closure also loses necessary history.
If one replaces a child's actual image by the entire sublevel
2f<=R^2/D_j, then on a saturated ray with threshold1 the permissible
radius is (R^2/D_j+1)/2. Passing this enlarged symmetric interval
through a bridge and another child objective bound yields, for large
R, a bound with leading growth R^4/(4*D_j^3). The desired parent
bound grows only as R^2/D_(j+1). For sufficiently large R the former
cannot prove the latter. This is an obstruction to that enlargement,
not an actual trajectory counterexample: the omitted affine prefix
history is precisely the missing information.

Mechanism1 is therefore not promoted to a uniform invariant. The next
mechanism keeps threshold-conditioned affine exterior branches and
exact signed interval capture, rather than assuming(4.1).

## 5. Mechanism2: exact capture for one-sided clipping

Throughout Sections5-6 use ONE fixed function(3.3), with v>0.
For j>=1 define the positive capacity

    Q_j=(A_j-ell)/a.

By(2.3), Q_j>0 and d_j<Q_j. For every j>=1 the following exact
interval identity holds:

    Psi_j([-A_j*v,Q_j*v])=[-v,a*v].                 (5.1)

The two exterior maps are also exact:

    Psi_j(-p)=-p+S_j*v,        p>=A_j*v,
    Psi_j( q)=-a*q+(S_j-ell)*v, q>=Q_j*v.           (5.2)

For the first branch every original query is negative saturated and
the terminal is at most-v. For the second, the first physical leaf
sends q to-a*q; the ENTIRE remaining physical suffix is negative
saturated and ends at most-v, because

    a*q >= (A_j-ell)*v=(1+S_j-ell)*v.

Thus no suffix query or final leaf is discarded. At equality the
terminal is exactly-v. These exterior formulas do not rely on(5.1).
The global positive output bound

    positive endpoint of Psi_j(I) <= a*v            (5.3)

holds for every interval I and j>=1, because the final leaf sends
any positive input negative and any negative input to at most a*v.

At j=1, A_1=2+a and Q_1=1/a. Formula(3.6) applied to the capacity
rectangle gives p'=max(a*Q_1*v,(A_1-ell)*v)=v and q'=a*v.
This proves(5.1) in the base case.

Assume(5.1) at j. For the parent capacity rectangle the exact first
child image, using(5.1),(5.2),(5.3), is

    [-(A_j+d_j+1)*v, a*v].

Indeed the two outer endpoints have this same negative magnitude,
since A_(j+1)-S_j=A_j+d_j+1 and
a*Q_(j+1)-(S_j-ell)=A_j+d_j+1. The central rectangle has image
[-v,a*v], and the two exterior pieces only extend the negative
endpoint. The bridge then has exact image

    [-A_j*v,d_j*v].

For its negative endpoint use a*d_j<A_j, from(2.3), and subtract
(1+d_j)*v from the previous negative magnitude. For its positive
endpoint use that the prior negative magnitude exceeds v. Since
d_j<Q_j, this bridge image lies inside the child capacity rectangle.
The second child therefore maps into[-v,a*v]. Both endpoint values
are actually attained: input -A_j*v produces-v, while input
-(A_j-ell)*v produces+a*v by remaining negative saturated until the
last query -v and then taking the final leaf. Both inputs belong to
[-A_j*v,d_j*v]. Continuity fills the interval. This proves(5.1)
with compatible full-child histories.

This is a different rectangle from the failed finite-ratio weighted
capture claim. Its upper capacity Q_j and upper output a*v are fixed
by the first/last leaf of this one-sided static function.

## 6. All-amplitude one-sided profile, including every intermediate scale

Write Phi_j^infty for the exact signed interval map of(3.3). For all
j>=1 and every integer r>=0 we claim the two coordinate bounds

    Phi_j^infty(d_j*v*b^r,d_j*v*b^r)
          <= (v*b^(r+1),v*b^(r+1)).                 (6.1)

The inequality is coordinatewise; the actual image need not be
symmetric. At j=1, d_1=beta<1. The whole input interval lies in the
quadratic region[-v,infinity), and the leaf is exactly x->-a*x.
Its endpoint magnitudes are b*v*b^r. This proves the base for ALL r.

For the unscaled case r=0 at j>=2, abbreviate A=A_(j-1),d=d_(j-1).
The input radius d_j*v is greater than A*v by(2.4), but is less
than Q_(j-1)*v, since

    a*d_j=beta*d < A-ell                              (6.2)

by(2.3). Thus(5.1)-(5.3) give the exact first-child signed endpoints

    p=(d_j-S_(j-1))*v>v, q=a*v.

The bridge maps these to

    p'=a*d*v, q'=d*v.

In fact the competing term in p' is
[d_j-A-d]*v=[(c-1)*d-A]*v<0 by(2.4). This exact asymmetric image
is contained in[-d*v,d*v]. The second child's r=0 induction bound
then gives both output magnitudes at most b*v.

For r>=1, the input radius satisfies

    d_j*v*b^r=(c*b)*d_(j-1)*v*b^(r-1)
               <=d_(j-1)*v*b^(r-1).

The first child's induction hypothesis and interval inclusion give
actual endpoints p,q<=v*b^r<v. The bridge is therefore linear on
the entire actual image, with endpoints d*q,d*p. These are at most
d_(j-1)*v*b^r. The second child's induction hypothesis at that same
r bounds both outputs by v*b^(r+1). This completes simultaneous
induction in j with ALL integers r retained. No optimized children
are identified or combined.

For ANY real0<R<=d_j*v, choose the unique applicable geometric cell

    d_j*v*b^(r+1)<R<=d_j*v*b^r, r>=0.

Exact upper boundaries are included in the right-hand inequality.
Interval inclusion and(6.1) give both endpoints at most
v*b^(r+1)<R/d_j. Therefore

    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/d_j, 0<|s|<=d_j*v.              (6.3)

If s is in[-A_j*v,Q_j*v] and |s|>d_j*v, identity(5.1) bounds
|Psi_j(s)| by v<|s|/d_j. Thus(6.3) holds on the WHOLE capacity
rectangle. This treats amplitudes crossing arbitrarily many levels;
it is not a finite-mesh argument. At s=0 the output is0.

At j=m, f_infty<=x^2/2 and(2.5) give the strict interior bound

    2*f_infty(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <=1/d_m^2<1/D_m.         (6.4)

For negative exterior s=-z*v, z>=A_m, formula(5.2) gives score

    (2*z-D_m)/z^2 <=1/D_m,

because z^2-D_m*(2*z-D_m)=(z-D_m)^2>=0. Equality is attained at
z=D_m, with every physical query negative saturated, terminal-A_m*v,
true radius D_m*v and gap D_m*v^2/2.

For positive exterior q>=Q_m*v, put z=q/v and M=D_m-2*ell>0.
The exact terminal is negative saturated by(5.2), and its score is

    (2*a*z-M)/z^2 <=a^2/M.                          (6.5)

This follows from a^2*z^2-M*(2*a*z-M)=(a*z-M)^2. Furthermore

    (1-a^2)*D_m >= (1-a^2)*(3+2*a)>2*(1+a)=2*ell,

since

    (1-a^2)*(3+2*a)-2*(1+a)=(1+a)^2*(1-2*a)>0.

Here D_m>=D_1=3+2*a. Consequently a^2/(D_m-2*ell)<1/D_m.
This proves the exact all-amplitude one-sided profile

    sup_(s!=0) 2*f_infty(Psi_m(s))/s^2=1/D_m.        (6.6)

Every positive or negative start, both capacity equalities and the
literal final leaf have been covered. The positive exterior formula
is a true affine saturated suffix, not a rescaled effective word.

## 7. Uniform whole-word bound for every single-crossing history

We will need an elementary bound for EVERY position of H_m. At index i
write P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t. If that step has level k,
so h_i=1+d_k, then

    d_k^2/[(1+P_i)^2*(1+2*T_i)] <=1/D_m.             (7.1)

This is an upper estimate of a scalar expression; it does not turn the
usual single-cut lower witness into an upper bound on unrestricted W.
Here is a direct proof with all occurrences covered.

Every level-k bridge has a complete H_k immediately before and after
it, so P_i,T_i>=S_k. Put A=A_k,d=d_k and
M=S_m-S_(k+1)>=0. There is some0<=x<=M for which
P_i=S_k+x and T_i=S_k+M-x. Their denominator is

    (A+x)^2*(2*A-1+2*M-2*x).

Its logarithm is concave on[0,M], because it is the sum of logarithms
of positive affine functions. Hence its minimum is at an endpoint.
The endpoint x=M minus the endpoint x=0 is

    M*[2*A*(A-1)+M*(2*A-1)]>=0.

The denominator is therefore at least A^2*(2*A-1+2*M). Also
D_m=4*A+2*d-1+2*M. It remains to prove

    d^2*(4*A+2*d-1) <= A^2*(2*A-1), d^2<=A^2.

The second follows from(2.1). At k=0 the first is
 a^2*(3+2*a)<1, since a<1/2. At k>=1, A=A_k>2 and d/A<1/2,
so

    (d/A)^2*(4*A+2*d-1)/(2*A-1)
      <(1/4)*(5*A-1)/(2*A-1) <=3/4<1,

where5*A-1<=3*(2*A-1) is equivalent to A>=2. Adding2*M*d^2
versus2*M*A^2 proves(7.1), including the first and last leaf.

Now take any finite u,v and a positive start s for which the physical
trajectory has a FIRST positive-to-negative crossing at step i and
NO subsequent return to positive. Zero states have zero final gap
and can be omitted from this discussion. Before step i every query
is positive saturated: an unsaturated positive query would cross at
its very next step because all h_t>1. Thus the incoming query is

    y=s-P_i*u>0,
    min(y,u)<=s/(1+P_i).

The latter inequality follows separately from y>=u, which implies
s>= (1+P_i)*u, and y<=u, which implies s=P_i*u+y>=(1+P_i)*y.
The crossing lands at-r, with

    0<r<=d_k*min(y,u)<=d_k*s/(1+P_i).                (7.2)

For y<=u the first inequality is equality r=d_k*y. For y>=u,
r=(1+d_k)*u-y<=d_k*u.

With no return, all subsequent negative queries must be saturated;
an unsaturated one would become positive at its next step. The
terminal magnitude is w=r-T_i*v>0. If w>=v, its energy is

    2*f(-w)=2*v*r-(2*T_i+1)*v^2<=r^2/(2*T_i+1).

If0<w<=v, then v>=r/(T_i+1), so w<=r/(T_i+1) and
2*f(-w)=w^2<=r^2/(T_i+1)^2<=r^2/(2*T_i+1). This includes T_i=0.
Together with(7.1)-(7.2) it gives

    2*f(x_n)/s^2 <=1/D_m.                            (7.3)

If a positive trajectory has no crossing at all, all nonterminal
queries are positive saturated and x_n=s-S_m*u>=0. A saturated
terminal has the usual squared-difference bound1/D_m. A core terminal
has score at most1/A_m^2<=1/D_m: writing y=x_n in[0,u], one has
s=S_m*u+y>=A_m*y. Thus ALL zero-crossing and single-crossing
histories are controlled, at every clipping ratio, without assuming
that such histories cover the whole family.

## 8. Complete finite-clipping transfer on the large-ratio domain

Assume u>=v and u/v>=Gamma_m. By Section2 this implies
u>=d_*v. There is a useful preliminary domain with the weaker
condition u>=d_*v:

    for every initial s<=u, the finite-clipping trajectory and
    objective agree exactly with the one-sided function(3.3).       (8.1)

This includes all negative starts of arbitrary magnitude. To prove it,
any negative-to-positive crossing produces a positive state at most
d_i*v<=d_*v<=u. Every positive query in[0,u] uses g=x and immediately
becomes nonpositive. A negative state that stays negative has the same
update for both functions. Starting either nonpositive or in[0,u]
therefore never visits x>u. Induction over the literal physical steps
proves(8.1), including the terminal. Section6 yields the target bound
for every such start. Equality at a negative saturated start remains
available. No continuity limit in the clipping ratio is needed.

It remains under(T) to cover positive starts s>u. The zero-state,
zero-crossing and single-crossing cases are covered in Section7.
Suppose instead that after its first positive-to-negative crossing
the trajectory returns to positive at least once. From its FIRST
such return onwards, every positive state is at most d_*v<=u,
and upper clipping is inactive forever, by exactly the argument for(8.1).

Every later positive-to-negative crossing has magnitude at most

    a*d_*v.                                         (8.2)

If its step is a leaf, multiply the positive query, at most d_*v,
by a. If its step is a nonleaf bridge, its preceding physical step
is a leaf. A positive query at that bridge cannot have arisen from
a positive core input at that leaf, because that would have become
negative; it must have arisen from a negative query. The positive
bridge query is therefore at most a*v. Multiplication by its own
d_i<=d_* proves(8.2). The initial return itself may occur at a
nonleaf bridge, but its next leaf is covered by the first case.
This proves the assertion for every subsequent crossing, with no
limit on their number or positions.

While a negative state remains negative it decreases in magnitude:
a negative core query would cross, while a negative saturated one
adds the positive increment h_i*v. Thus at a negative TERMINAL,
(8.2) gives |x_n|<=a*d_*v. Since f(-r)<=v*r for every r>=0,

    2*f(x_n)<=2*a*d_*v^2.                            (8.3)

If the terminal is positive, the actual last leaf produces it from
a negative query and hence x_n<=a*v. Its energy is then
x_n^2<=a^2*v^2<=2*a*d_*v^2, since d_*>=a. A zero terminal also
satisfies(8.3). This is where keeping the original final leaf is
essential: an intermediate positive bridge output can be much larger.

Finally s>u>=Gamma_m*v and Gamma_m^2=2*a*d_*D_m give

    2*f(x_n)/s^2 <=2*a*d_*v^2/s^2 <1/D_m.            (8.4)

Combining(8.1), Section7 and(8.4) proves the upper bound in(P) for
EVERY real nonzero start under(T). Reflection treats v>=u. The finite
ratio can be arbitrarily large; no constants depend on a bounded
number of returns or an amplitude cutoff.

For lower attainment choose s=D_m*u. Every physical query is
x_i=u*(D_m-P_i)>=u, including the terminal u*A_m. The own radius
is D_m*u and the terminal gap is D_m*u^2/2, so the score is1/D_m.
Likewise s=-D_m*v gives x_i=-v*(D_m-P_i), terminal-v*A_m and the
same score. These are actual instances on the same static function,
with all rows and the full Gram derived algebraically in Section3.
This completes the joint derivation of(P) on the complete domain(T).

Equivalently, the exact signed interval endpoints of Section3 satisfy

    2*f(-p_m(R,R))<=R^2/D_m,
    2*f( q_m(R,R))<=R^2/D_m                           (8.5)

for EVERY R>0 and every ratio in(T). Every endpoint is attained from
some |s|<=R by continuity; the already proved pointwise bound uses
that start's own radius, at most R. Conversely, (8.5) bounds every
individual start by taking R=|s| and using monotonicity of f away
from0 on each ray. This proves both of the requested signed-energy
inequalities on(T), not only a symbolic recurrence for them.

## 9. Boundary checks and the precise remaining interface

The all-saturated exterior formulas have been proved as part of the
argument, not treated as a new search mechanism. For finite clipping,
s>=A_m*u and s<=-A_m*v have exact score at most1/D_m, regardless
of clipping ratio. This follows from the actual monotone saturated
prefixes and the square identity in Section6.

For comparison, a function with one FLAT ray and active curvature
kappa in(0,1], clipped at level b0>0, has complete minimum half-line
and, for any nonnegative word of mass S, exact closest-radius profile

    kappa/(1+2*kappa*S).

Here is a joint boundary proof. Reflect so the positive ray is active.
An active start has closest radius s. If the trajectory enters the
minimum half-line its terminal gap is zero. Otherwise it stays positive
and decreases. A saturated terminal forces all earlier queries to be
saturated; completing the same square gives the displayed upper bound,
attained at s=b0*(2*S+1/kappa). If the terminal is core, monotonicity
of g along the decreasing positive states gives
s=x_n+sum h_i*g_i>=x_n*(1+kappa*S); its score is at most
kappa/(1+kappa*S)^2<=kappa/(1+2*kappa*S). Zero steps and threshold
equalities are included. Both-flat functions have zero gap. On H_m
this value is at most1/D_m. This boundary check is distinct from the
unit-curvature one-sided clipping theorem of Section6.

For finite ratios this report establishes:

* the FULL all-amplitude domain max(u,v)/min(u,v)>=Gamma_m;
* the all-amplitude one-sided clipping model(3.3), proved directly;
* for finite u>=d_*v, EVERY start s<=u, with reflected counterpart;
* every zero-crossing and single-crossing history at ANY finite ratio;
* every saturated exterior at ANY finite ratio.

The previous JOINT UNVERIFIED report05423f48b823e3bf additionally
claims the complete uniform ratio band max/min<=1/(c-2). It is
awareness only here: no result in Sections2-8 depends on that claim.
Combining drafts would require treating their proofs jointly or formally
verifying them; neither draft is an accepted fact.

The still-unresolved target-strength interface is exactly(8.5) for
all remaining positive u,v,R. In particular the method above does NOT
cover every intermediate ratio

    1/(c-2) < max(u,v)/min(u,v) < Gamma_m.

For u>=v and d_*<=u/v<Gamma_m, all s<=u are already covered by(8.1).
The unresolved case there can be narrowed further to s>u with at
least one negative-to-positive return and potentially

    u<s<Gamma_m*v.

At larger s, the same estimate(8.3) already suffices. Below u/v=d_*,
a return may exceed u and reactivate the large clipping level; the
inactive-positive-ray reduction itself then fails. This is a precise
surviving history constraint, not a claim that all those instances are
bad or that any crossing count can be freely prescribed.

Neither a root-only escape nor recurrence derivation is called a
counterexample to the objective profile. No monotonicity in clipping
ratio is asserted: the high-ratio proof is by an explicit trajectory
partition and exact guards. Independent positive core curvatures remain
unattempted. The original full W and the minimax problem remain OPEN.

## 10. Correct benchmark direction and attainment scope

Under(T) the restricted-family value equals1/D_m. Accepted
fact_id931b9653ce0f7201 gives

    B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50, n_m=2^m-1.

Thus this particular complete large-ratio clipping family cannot reach
the original benchmark on the diagnostic hierarchy: its supremum
relative to U_(n_m)^ZJ is strictly below47/50. The same is true for the
one-sided comparison family. This is an upper bound on these declared
function families only, NOT on W_(n_m)(H_m). It cannot establish the
conditional full-W premise17/(16D_m), minimax optimality, or a negative
resolution of the original problem. No benchmark lower comparison is
inferred from an upper bound on B_n/D_m.

## 11. Retrieval, scope checks and resource record

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving and
construct-counterexamples. The active program was signed clipping
energy on the same-m hierarchy; the missing mechanism was compatible
objective control under arbitrarily unequal clipping and later returns.
Search mode was repair. A Matlas query returned three inapplicable
results: batch-normalization local contraction(arXiv2108.01110), noisy
Armijo descent under strong convexity(arXiv1906.04648), and stochastic
ADMM convergence(arXiv2012.07401). None supplies this threshold map or
is used. Web fallback gave silver-stepsize and Huber background, including
arXiv2309.16530; no external theorem was imported. The exact known local
Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur arXiv1502.05666 text was read at Section2.4
and Lemma2, including both proof directions, for conjugacy context.
The static row calculation(3.2) supplies every needed row directly.
No new body of external machinery was opened or rejected without reading.

Some broad retrieval output was truncated. The actual current guidance,
prior report's full mathematical body, and the complete accepted931
proof were read in subsequent bounded/direct outputs; no claim uses
unseen truncated material. The administrative mkdir/append commands
returned successful empty output, which is not mathematical evidence.

Author's textual checks within this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft:

* Full child parameters are never reset; d_m is not a physical bridge.
* One-sided positive curvature is1, and the complete minimum is{0}.
* Positive capacity Q_j pays the first leaf and the WHOLE remaining suffix.
* Capture proof uses exact asymmetric images; the plateau uses only
  coordinatewise inclusion, with each threshold guard displayed.
* The geometric scaling induction quantifies over every r>=0 and every
  real input radius, including endpoints and arbitrarily many scales.
* The large-ratio proof covers both initial signs and every amplitude;
  at most one return is not assumed. Its small-side energy weight remains v.
* The last leaf, not an intermediate root, bounds positive terminal energy.
* Section7 bounds all original cut locations using their actual prefix
  and suffix masses, including the first and last leaf.
* Every actual primal family uses one globally smooth static function,
  all physical samples, all ordered rows, complete minimum and own radius.
* Every newly needed premise is derived in this joint report. No finding
  is treated as a fact. Only the accepted931 benchmark ratio is imported.

Resource account: ONE analytical invocation; at most TWO substantive
mechanisms. ZERO mathematical/numerical programs, arithmetic/trajectory/
recurrence replays, optimizers/solvers/benchmark DP, formal calls/fact_submit,
new accepted facts, installations, paid consults or subagents. Tools were
used only for textual retrieval, local artifact writing and administrative
integrity checks. All older grants remain closed. There is no outward
publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown, task overwrite, or
automatic renewal. Official post-return process/counter state is not
observable in this report. At the authorized cap, hand back this joint
draft and its self-contained summary; original target OPEN.
