# Paid optimum rows: exact budget and three scoped analytical screens

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No formal submission,
new fact, full-class objective bound, or original-target resolution is claimed.
This is worker max's single round after completed67, with ceiling68 and
attempted ceiling69, under TASK.md and guidance66aa167542a58193.

The three explicit policies below have nonnegative original multipliers and
exact value flow. Their new optimum-outgoing rows fit a genuine same-word
Huber budget. Nevertheless each fails a full original-coordinate PSD test.
For the two asymmetric policies, every PSD level even in the larger classes
specified below must satisfy q_cert*(1+2 sum H_m)>4/3 for m>=1536. For the
source-based policy, bounded whole-word slack cannot repair the fixed finite
array: the necessary relative slack exceeds 2^m/(30000m).

These are restrictions on certificate families, NOT lower bounds on W.
No unrestricted-dual infeasibility is asserted. General finite-row changes
that alter the displayed cross-block moments or inherited right-child
coefficients remain outside these screens.

## 1. Fixed words, original objective, and accepted inputs

Fix an enclosing integer m. Every child throughout this document uses

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta_word=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta_word/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

The physical word is h=H_m of length n=2^m-1. Put

    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,
    p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, 0<=i<=n.

All n updates are retained in order. The terminal gradient g_n is an original
interpolation variable, not an extra physical update. No child resets m.

Use accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290's convention: L=1,
x_star=g_star=f_star=0 and independent original formal vectors
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For all distinct
original indices, including star and terminal, define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The accepted interpolation convention gives Q_ij<=0 on actual trajectories.
A full certificate is

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij,             (1)

with every lambda_ij>=0, every value coefficient zero, and M(q) PSD on ALL
original formal vectors. It would imply W_n(h)<=q, with W=2R and the
original specified-minimizer radius. Zeros below are multiplier choices,
not deleted original inequalities.

Other accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750's original all-split benchmark,
ray limit, and full-coordinate primitive/objective identities, and
fact5fd396eba75651e7's scalar comparison for these exact words:

    B_n/D<237/250 for m>=1536,
    B_n/D -> Z_1/(2 exp(1/4)).                              (2)

Here U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Only the scalar part of fact5fd is used. Its special
witness-family optima do not bound W from above.

The complete prior drafts dd4045b39aa0c257, ee71972baf834490 and
83357ebfcaa1356b were read. They are provenance, not accepted proof premises.
Every prior coefficient identity needed below is reproduced and derived
within this joint draft. Numerical findings ad24079f80cd6a3a and
242f8d453ac26183 are not mathematical inputs.

All notation in this document is local. In particular, A in Section 5 is
a child STEP SUM; A_j in Section 7 is one plus a child sum. The physical
bridge d in Section 5 is distinguished from the bridge EXCESS d_j in
Section 7. The letters a_i below are multipliers, whereas unindexed a above
is the fixed physical hierarchy parameter.

## 2. Exact same-word Huber price, before choosing any policy

Let Hub(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1 and |x|-1/2 otherwise. It is convex,
differentiable and 1-smooth, with unique minimizer0. Start at x_0=D.
Every actual query is x_i=D-p_i>=S+1>1, so

    g_i=1, f_i=D-p_i-1/2, 2f_n=D.

For an empty word S=0 the same calculation uses the boundary point1;
our screened words have S>0. Direct substitution in the ORIGINAL rows gives

    Q_ij=0 for finite i!=j,
    Q_(i,star)=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-(2S-p_i).                                 (3)

Write b_i=lambda_(star,i), a_i=lambda_(i,star), and q=zeta/D with
eta=zeta-1. On this exact Huber transcript,

    M(q)=D eta-sum_(i=0)^n b_i(2S-p_i).                    (4)

Thus a necessary condition for PSD is

    sum_i b_i(2S-p_i)<=D eta,
    eta>=0, sum_i b_i<=D eta/S<=3eta when S>1.             (5)

The last inequality is strict when eta>0; at eta=0 all b_i are zero.

The row price varies from 2S to S, including the terminal price S. No
nonzero optimum-outgoing row is free on this transcript. Function values
and the specified-minimizer radius are unchanged.

The price is exact as an evaluation, but it is not a sufficient PSD test.
There is a second useful restriction that keeps the x_0 mixed terms. Let
K_ij=lambda_ij on finite pairs and let B=sum b_i. The complete finite flow is

    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+a_i-b_i=2*1_(i=n),
    sum_i a_i=2+B.                                        (6)

On the entire two-dimensional original subspace g_i=v for every finite i,
x_0=x, flow gives

    M(q)=q x^2-(2+B)xv+[D+sum_i b_i(1+p_i)]v^2.            (7)

Indeed finite position terms sum to
sum_i p_i(out_i^K-in_i^K)=2S-sum_i a_i p_i+sum_i b_i p_i;
finite gradient differences vanish, and the optimum terms supply the
remaining a_i p_i and (a_i+b_i)/2. Therefore PSD also requires

    zeta >= D(1+B/2)^2/[D+sum_i b_i(1+p_i)].              (8)

For example, spending the ENTIRE budget (4) on b_n=D eta/S, eta>0,
would give M(D,1,...,1)=0, but the x_0 derivative there equals
2eta-b_n=-eta/S!=0. A PSD quadratic vanishing at a vector annihilates
that vector, as follows by evaluating it on that vector plus t times an
arbitrary direction for both signs of t. Such saturated terminal spending
therefore cannot be PSD without changing the budget allocation. This is a
budget warning, not a fourth screened policy.

## 3. Full value flow and full correction in original coordinates

Put z_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, so x_i=x_0+z_i. Under (6), (1) is exactly

    q||x_0||^2-sum_i a_i<x_0,g_i>
     +sum_i a_i[-<g_i,z_i>+||g_i||^2/2]
     +sum_i b_i||g_i||^2/2
     +sum_(i!=j finite)K_ij[<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].   (9)

In particular its FULL matrix is

    [[q,-a^T/2],[-a/2,C]],                                (10)

on the original n+2 coordinates. Define h_n=0 and out_i=a_i+sum_j K_ij.
Every entry of the gradient block C is

    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(j>i)K_ij,
    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv
                         +h_i[a_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j.  (11)

All sums in (11) are over finite labels. Half the total mass incident to i
is out_i-1_(i=n) by (6), including BOTH optimum directions. A forward
position row based at i subtracts h_i K_ij from the diagonal. For i<j,
difference squares contribute -(K_ij+K_ji) to the cross coefficient;
position rows based at i contribute -h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv, and rows based at j
contribute h_i[a_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv]. This proves (11) without omitting b.

For q>0 the exact full square completion is

    M(q)=q||x_0-sum_i a_i g_i/(2q)||^2
                +sum_(i,j)[C_ij-a_i a_j/(4q)]<g_i,g_j>.   (12)

The terminal, every mixed term, and every original gradient remain.

More generally start with any value-flow base (K^0,a^0,b^0=0) at 1/D.
Permit a signed finite modification deltaK with final K^0+deltaK>=0.
Set r_i=sum_j deltaK_ij-sum_j deltaK_ji. Exact final flow forces

    a_i=a_i^0+b_i-r_i>=0, b_i>=0.                         (13)

The complete value-free correction to the base is

    eta||x_0||^2/D-sum_i(b_i-r_i)<x_0,g_i>
     +sum_i(b_i-r_i)sum_(t<i)h_t<g_i,g_t>
     +sum_i(b_i-r_i/2)||g_i||^2
     +sum_(i!=j)deltaK_ij[<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].   (14)

This follows by subtracting (9) for the base. It allows reductions of old
finite multipliers, provided their FINAL signs hold. The three policies
below use particular nonnegative additions and do not exhaust (13)-(14).

A balanced optimum cycle of weight rho_i adds rho_i to both a_i and b_i,
keeps K unchanged, and adds exactly

    rho_i C_i^opt,
    C_i^opt=Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i)
           =||g_i||^2-<x_0,g_i>+sum_(t<i)h_t<g_i,g_t>.    (15)

For the sum of such cycles, deltaC has diagonal rho_i and entries
2deltaC_ti=h_t rho_i for t<i. Its x_0-to-gradient entries are -rho_i/2.
These are not just positive diagonal corrections. For example a lone
cycle at i>0, with x_0=0, g_i=1 and an earlier g_t=-2/h_t, other gradients0,
has value -rho_i when h_t>0. Adding eta||x_0||^2/D does not change that
negative value. This illustrates why (12), rather than diagonal signs,
is the required test.

For any distinct finite i,j, a directed optimum triangle of weight t_ij
adds t_ij to the three ORIGINAL rows Q_(star,i), Q_(i,j), Q_(j,star).
Its flow is zero and its complete polynomial is

    t_ij T_ij^opt,
    T_ij^opt=||g_i||^2+||g_j||^2-<g_i,g_j>
                  +<g_i,z_j-z_i>-<g_j,x_0+z_j>.         (16)

Only a_j increases. Its new x_0 cross term is -t_ij<x_0,g_j>, and
its Huber price is t_ij(2S-p_i). Both facts matter below.

## 4. The three explicit policies and their actual budgets

Define positive density v_i=h_i for i<n and v_n=S+1, so sum_i v_i=D.
Take eta>=0 and q=(1+eta)/D.

Policy I: on the asymmetric array of Section 5, add balanced optimum cycles

    rho_i=eta v_i/D.                                     (17)

Policy II: use exactly the same cycle law (17), on the source-calibrated
finite array and its original objective completion in Section 7.

Policy III: on the asymmetric array, use

    rho_i=eta v_i/(2D),
    t_(n,L_k)=eta/2, all other added triangles zero.      (18)

Here L_k is the original physical root query of the last join. Thus this
policy changes a finite row too: it adds eta/2 to Q_(n,L_k), as well as
to Q_(star,n) and Q_(L_k,star).

Every addition in (17)-(18) is nonnegative and has zero value flow. For
Policies I and II, (3) gives their EXACT new Huber expenditure

    P_H=eta/D * sum_i v_i(2S-p_i).                        (19)

For Policy III it is

    P_H=eta/(2D)*sum_i v_i(2S-p_i)+eta S/2.               (20)

For eta>0, (19)<=2S eta<D eta. Similarly (20)<=3S eta/2<D eta.
Thus these are feasible budget allocations with genuine paid costs and
positive Huber remainder, not laws rejected merely for spending too much.
At eta=0 the costs vanish. Exact sums (19)-(20), not only their upper
bounds, specify the necessary cost of each policy.

The tests below grant STRICTLY LARGER classes: arbitrary nonnegative
balanced weights, and for Policy III arbitrary nonnegative triangles
star->R_i->L_l->star, with any original left destination. Their failure
therefore includes the explicit policies without fitting a weight profile.

## 5. Complete asymmetric base, with unrestricted left completion

Consider the final actual parent (H_j,d,H_j), j=m-1, k=2^j-1. In THIS
section d=1+a c^j is the physical bridge, A=sum H_j, t=A+d,
S=2A+d, D=1+2S. The left queries L_l=l, 0<=l<=k, include bridge L_k;
right queries R_i=k+1+i, 0<=i<=k, include actual terminal n=R_k.
Let p_i^c be the child chronological masses, with p_0^c=0,p_k^c=A.

Take any nonnegative finite reference array K^c and nonnegative mu_i with

    sum_v K^c_iv-sum_v K^c_vi+mu_i=2*1_(i=k).              (21)

In particular sum mu_i=2. Reference child PSD is NOT assumed. Set

    theta=(3A+d)/(2S), s=1-theta=t/(2S),
    kappa=0 or s/(2d), E=sum_i mu_i(A-p_i^c), 0<=E<=2A.

The prescribed original rows of the base are

    K_(R_i,R_v)=K^c_iv, a_(R_i)=theta mu_i,
    K_(R_i,L_0)=mu_i[s-kappa d(A-p_i^c)/A],
    K_(R_i,L_k)=mu_i kappa t(A-p_i^c)/A,
    K_(R_i,L_l)=0 for 0<l<k,
    sum_l K_(L_l,R_i)=kappa mu_i(A-p_i^c).               (22)

All b_i are initially zero. All left finite rows and optimum attachments
are otherwise arbitrary nonnegative values satisfying full original flow.
No PSD condition on the left child is presumed. Thus these screens grant
arbitrary position-dependent left corrections already present in the base.

All prescribed signs hold: s>0,theta>0, and at repaired kappa,
s-kappa d(A-p_i^c)/A=s(A+p_i^c)/(2A)>=s/2. Put
B_i=sum_l K_(R_i,L_l)=s mu_i+kappa mu_i(A-p_i^c), and
F_i=sum_l K_(L_l,R_i)=kappa mu_i(A-p_i^c). Right flow is (21) plus
theta mu_i+B_i-F_i, hence is 2*1_(i=k).

For a completely explicit base completion, send every F_i from L_k, set
all left finite rows0, and take

    a_(L_0)=2s-kappa dE/A, a_(L_k)=kappa dE/A,
    a_(L_l)=0 for 0<l<k.                                (23)

The first attachment is at least s; all are nonnegative. Incoming-minus-
outgoing cross flow at L_0,L_k is precisely (23); other left flows vanish.
Thus (21)-(23) give a complete value-flow array for j>=1. For the empty
word use a_0=2 separately; for H_1 use the actual one-step array
lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1, and apply (21)-(23)
at subsequent levels. For h=(b) that one-step base has

    M(1/(1+2b))=||x_0-(1+2b)(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/(1+2b)
                              +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4.

The actual b=1+a is below sqrt(2)<3/2. This checks its starting calibration;
no parent PSD is inferred. Subsequent definitions are coefficient formulas,
never executed recurrences. The proof next allows all larger left completions,
not just (23).

## 6. New full left-block test: Policies I and III fail even with free weights

On the ORIGINAL formal space choose

    g_(L_l)=v for EVERY left query, including L_k,
    g_(R_i)=0 for EVERY right query, including terminal,
    x_0=x.                                                (24)

The original positions are x_(L_l)=x-p_l^c v and x_(R_i)=x-tv.
This is a test subspace of the whole quadratic, not a quotient or a claim
that the formal assignment is interpolation-feasible.

Let B_l be total backward inflow into L_l and F_l total forward outflow.
Left finite flow is -a_(L_l)-F_l+B_l. Multiplying it by p_l^c and summing
shows that all left finite position terms contribute
sum_l p_l^c[-a_(L_l)-F_l+B_l]v^2. The optimum rows contribute
-a_L xv+sum_l a_(L_l)(p_l^c+1/2)v^2, where a_L=sum_l a_(L_l)=2s.
Backward rows contribute (sum_i B_i)v^2/2. Forward rows contribute
sum_l F_l[-t+p_l^c+1/2]v^2. All p_l^c a_(L_l) and p_l^c F_l terms cancel.
Consequently the full base (9), including every arbitrary left coefficient,
restricts to

    M_base(q)=q x^2-2s xv+Gamma_L v^2,
    Gamma_L=sum_i B_i+sum_l p_l^c B_l-t sum_l F_l
           =2s+kappa E.                                 (25)

The final equality uses sum_i B_i=2s+kappa E,
sum_l p_l^c B_l=kappa tE, and sum_l F_l=kappa E, directly from (22).
This exact cancellation is the restrictive cross-block MOMENT of the base.
It is not a general identity for asymmetric certificates.

A balanced cycle on a left label contributes
rho_(L_l)[(1+p_l^c)v^2-xv] on (24); a cycle on a right label is zero.
A backward optimum triangle star->R_i->L_l->star contributes precisely
t_(R_i,L_l)[(1+p_l^c)v^2-xv] by (16). These statements include their
x_0 mixed terms. Let U be the total weight of these left balanced cycles
and backward triangles, and let J be their sum weighted by (1+p_l^c).
Then 0<=J<=(A+1)U, and the complete modified restriction is

    M(q)=q x^2-(2s+U)xv+(Gamma_L+J)v^2.                 (26)

The right balanced weights may be arbitrary; they vanish on this test.
The triangle weights and destinations may be arbitrary as specified;
no Huber budget restriction is needed for the following obstruction.

Suppose A>=1024 and d/A>2/5. Since E<=2A and kappa<=s/(2d),

    Gamma_L<=s(2+A/d)<(9/2)s<(A+1)s.                    (27)

For q<=0, (26) cannot be PSD since its mixed coefficient is nonzero.
For q>0, set v=1 and x=(s+U/2)/q. Full PSD would require

    q >= (s+U/2)^2/(Gamma_L+J)
      > (s+U/2)^2/[(A+1)(s+U)] >= s/(A+1).             (28)

The last inequality follows by subtracting s(s+U) from (s+U/2)^2;
the difference is U^2/4>=0. This explicitly pays the mixed term: merely
raising the positive gradient diagonal would miss its simultaneous change.

Also Ds>t=A+d, so

    qD>Ds/(A+1)>(A+d)/(A+1)
                     >(7/5)A/(A+1)>4/3.               (29)

The final inequality follows from A>20. Thus the exact full-space assignment
just specified is negative at ANY proposed qD<=4/3, regardless of how the
balanced and backward-triangle weights are chosen. This proves the failure
of both explicit Policies I and III and their larger stated classes.

### The actual enclosing-m words satisfy the elementary conditions

For j=m-1, direct substitution in the physical sum identity gives

    A=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2), d=1+a c^j.                (30)

The identity follows from S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a c^j and S_0=0 by symbolic
induction. For m>=1536, 12/5<c<5/2 and a>3/10. For the upper bound on c,
use lambda<17/7 and epsilon<1/256 to get c<4369/1792<5/2.
Let z=(2/c)^j. Since (5/6)^4<1/2 and j>=24, z<1/64. Then

    A/d<1/(c-2)+z/a<17/7+5/96<5/2.

This proves d/A>2/5. Every child step exceeds1, so
A>2^j-1>1024. Therefore (29) holds at EVERY m>=1536, on the actual
unchanged word, not a newly parameterized child.

The proof is a short full-matrix screen. It does not spend the round on
an impossibility theorem for general duals, or assert that a feasible level
exists above this necessary lower restriction.

## 7. The full source-calibrated array for Policy II

In this section set S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, d_j=a c^j, so the physical
bridge at level j is 1+d_j and A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j. For any word H_j with
terminal index z define the ORIGINAL primitive history polynomial

    V_j=2sum_(i<z)h_i(f_i-f_z)
              +sum_(i<z)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-A_j(A_j-1)||g_z||^2.

Start with empty finite array Lambda^0=0. At each join let p be the
physical bridge query and z the parent terminal; i ranges over nonterminal
right-child queries. Use the following exact nonnegative weights:

    u_j=2lambda,
    v_j^w=[u_j d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,
    k_j=1+(u_j+v_j^w)/2,
    z_j^w=2A_j,
    p_j^w=4A_j+2d_j-u_j(A_j-1)
                         =2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda).     (31)

Embed the entire left finite array with weight1, the entire right finite
array with weight k_j, and add

    u_j sum_i h_i Q_(i,p)+v_j^w sum_i h_i Q_(i,z)
                             +p_j^w Q_(z,p)+z_j^w Q_(p,z).   (32)

There are no optimum rows in this finite recursion. Define P_j by
V_j+sum Lambda^j_ij Q_ij=P_j. This defines a full quadratic, without
assuming its positivity.

For a check of every coefficient, temporarily write A=A_j,d=d_j,
s=A-1,T=2A+d,K=A(A-1),K_+=T(T-1), u=u_j,v=v_j^w,k=k_j,
p^w=p_j^w,z^w=z_j^w, and r=sum_i h_i g_i over the right
nonterminal queries. For a nonempty right child, function coefficients
cancel precisely when

    u+v=2(k-1), us+p^w-z^w=2(A+d),                       (33)

which (31) satisfies. For the empty right child these equations remain
sufficient and are the chosen policy; no necessity is asserted there.
The four gradient parts of the unweighted terms
in (32) are respectively

    d<r,g_p>+||r||^2/2+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2+s||g_p||^2/2,
    -||r||^2/2+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2+s||g_z||^2/2-<r,g_z>,
    d<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2,
    -(d+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r>-<g_p,g_z>.

After adding the history differences, the correction R_j in
P_(j+1)=P_j^left+k_j P_j^right+R_j has coefficients

    R_j=(u-v)||r||^2/2+(ud-z^w)<r,g_p>+(p^w-v)<r,g_z>
                  +(p^w d-z^w)<g_p,g_z>+C_p||g_p||^2+C_z||g_z||^2,
    C_p=K-d-d^2+(us+p^w)/2-(d+1/2)z^w,
    C_z=-K_++kK+vs/2+(p^w+z^w)/2.

Substitute (31), put t_j=d_j/A_j, t_*=1/lambda,
Y=r-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z. The SAME complete correction is

    R_j=(t_*-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>
                           +A_j^2(2+t_*+t_j)||Z||^2].     (34)

For instance the coefficient of ||r||^2 equals
(u-v)/2=(lambda+1)(t_*-t_j), and ud-z^w=-2lambda A_j(t_*-t_j);
substitution of the displayed C_p,C_z supplies the remaining coefficients.
Thus (34) concerns all original gradients and includes every right aggregate,
bridge and terminal cross term.

The bracket in (34) is positive definite in Y,Z. Its first diagonal is
lambda+1, and its determinant divided by A_j^2 is
(lambda+1)(2+t_*+t_j)-lambda^2>=lambda>0. It vanishes on common gradients
because Y=Z=0. Consequently P_j e_j=0 at every depth, where e_j is the
all-ones gradient vector. This is an exact matrix-kernel identity obtained
by induction from (34), without claiming PSD.

### Signs and the first three levels

Let v_h=a/(c-2). Since c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta_word,
0<v_h<1. Symbolic induction gives

    A_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                       (35)

For our m>=1536, c<5/2<3, hence d_j<A_j. The ratios t_j increase strictly.
The first values are

    A_0=1,d_0=a; A_1=2+a,d_1=beta_word;
    A_2=4+a(2+c),d_2=beta_word c.

Here t_0<t_*, and
lambda d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.
At level2 the exact factorization is

    c(lambda d_2-A_2)
       =epsilon[(lambda+4)-(4lambda+2)epsilon-(5lambda+2)epsilon^2].  (36)

For 2<lambda<3 and epsilon<=1/12, the bracket exceeds
6-14/12-17/144>4>c. Thus lambda d_2-A_2>epsilon, t_2>t_*,
and t_j>t_* for all j>=2. It follows that R_0,R_1 are PSD but R_j is
negative semidefinite for j>=2. This is a statement about the signed join
corrections; the full matrices continue to be the sums in (34).

The weights u_j,v_j^w,z_j^w,k_j are positive. At j=0, p_j^w=4+2a>0.
At j=1, A_1<lambda implies p_j^w>2lambda-2>0. At j>=2,
p_j^w>2lambda. Thus every original finite multiplier produced by (32)
is nonnegative. At epsilon=0 all t_j=t_* and (31)-(32) reduce exactly to
the accepted original-Silver primitive gluing weights u=v=p^w=2lambda,
z^w=2A,k=lambda^2. At positive epsilon the full first two levels also
pass PSD because R_0,R_1>=0. The obstruction below is not a failure of
these starting calibrations.

Complete the finite array to the ORIGINAL objective by setting

    K^0_ij=Lambda^m_ij/D, a_i^0=2h_i/D for i<n,
    a_n^0=2(S+1)/D, b_i^0=0.                             (37)

Its entire quadratic is

    M^0(1/D)=[||x_0-sum_i v_i g_i||^2+P_m(g)]/D.         (38)

To check (38) directly, expand x_n=x_0-sum_(i<n)h_i g_i in

    ||x_0||^2-2D f_n+sum Lambda^m Q
                 +2sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,star)+2(S+1)Q_(n,star).

After using the definition of V_m, its value coefficients cancel and it
is P_m+||x_n-(S+1)g_n||^2. These are exactly the square and weights in
(38). This is also the objective completion in accepted fact042, with no
new primitive inequality assumed. Policy II adds (17) to this full array.

## 8. A rightmost original direction defeats ALL balanced-cycle repairs

Let w be the original gradient vector that equals1 on the first four
queries of the RIGHTMOST H_3 subword and0 elsewhere. Its support is
{n-7,n-6,n-5,n-4}; every one of these is an original physical query.
In that H_3, all left-child gradients including its bridge are1 and all
right-child gradients including its terminal are0. Therefore the two
embedded P_2 values vanish and (34) gives

    P_3(w)=A_2^2-2A_2 d_2-d_2^2=-E_2,
    E_2=(lambda d_2-A_2)(A_2+d_2/lambda)>epsilon.        (39)

At every enclosing join j>=3, the support stays in its right child;
its bridge and terminal gradients are0 and its left block is0. Since
R_j<=0, repeated substitution in the exact identity (34) gives

    P_m(w)<=-E_2 product_(j=3)^(m-1) k_j.                (40)

This is a symbolic inequality, not an executed recurrence. For j>=2,
(31) gives k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j>lambda^2>5.
Also (35) gives D=2A_m-1<2c^m<2(5/2)^m. Consequently

    P_m(w)/D < -2^m/(1000m).                            (41)

Indeed E_2>epsilon=1/(4m), and
5^(m-3)/[2(5/2)^m]=2^m/250. All inequalities retain the fixed enclosing m.

Let T=sum_i v_i w_i=A_2+d_2, since the support contains a complete H_2
and its next physical bridge 1+d_2. Because a<1/2,c<5/2,
A_2<25/4,d_2<5/2, so 0<T<9. Put theta_w=T/D and use the FULL original
assignment

    x_0=0, g=w-theta_w e_m.                              (42)

Here 0<theta_w<1 since D>15. The square in (38) vanishes:
sum_i v_i g_i=T-theta_w D=0. Moreover P_m e_m=0, so P_m(g)=P_m(w).
Increasing only q adds zero on this direction because x_0=0.

It remains to price EVERY balanced cycle, not just the previously displayed
negative direction's diagonal. Define U_i=sum_(t<i)h_t w_t; then 0<=U_i<=T
and the original position at (42) is x_i=theta_w p_i-U_i.
If w_i=0, g_i=-theta_w, and (15) gives

    C_i^opt=theta_w^2(1+p_i)-theta_w U_i
          <=theta_w^2(S+1)<theta_w^2 D=theta_w T<T<9.

If w_i=1, 0<g_i<1 and x_i>=-T, hence
C_i^opt=g_i^2-g_i x_i<=1+T<10. Thus EVERY original cycle, including
the terminal cycle and cycles before or after the support, has value less
than10 on (42).

Now allow ANY nonnegative balanced weights rho_i on the fixed array (37),
at q=(1+eta)/D. If the full matrix were PSD, the exact Huber budget (5)
would impose sum rho_i<=3eta. Equations (38),(41),(42),(15) then give

    M(q)|_(42)<-2^m/(1000m)+30eta.                        (43)

Therefore a necessary condition for a PSD repair in this entire class is

    eta>2^m/(30000m).                                    (44)

This grows without bound, so no bounded whole-word loss repairs this fixed
finite array through balanced optimum cycles, even with arbitrary weights.
The explicit Policy II is included. The proof did not assume exact Huber
contact, and allowed the full positive slack in (4).

For the stated target range, (44) is already incompatible with eta<=1/3
for every m>=1536. The elementary inequality 2^m>m^3 for m>=10 follows
from 2^10>10^3 and (1+1/m)^3<=1331/1000<2. Hence the right side of (44)
is greater than m^2/30000>1/3 at m>=1536.

The mechanism is the amplification of a negative small-block direction in
right copies. A bounded original optimum-outgoing mass can contribute only
a bounded amount on (42). This conclusion is specific to the inherited
finite array (31)-(32); changing those finite coefficients is a substantive
escape route, not ruled out by (44).

## 9. Comparison with the ORIGINAL benchmark, without a new numerical table

The screens are stronger than merely missing the sufficient threshold
250/237. The accepted recurrence in fact042 is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1)Omega(C_k,C_l),
    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(C_k,B_l),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

Using the displayed exact formulas, C_1=lambda, B_1=4 and the single
admissible split k=l=1 at n=3 gives

    B_3>=G(lambda,4)=2lambda^2.

This is a symbolic lower comparison from an admissible split, not a new
benchmark DP or saved-table replay. The accepted ray inequality therefore
gives Z_1>=lambda^(-2)B_3>=2. The exponential series, with
k!>=2*3^(k-2) for k>=2 and strictness from k=4, gives

    exp(1/4)<1+1/4+(1/32)/(1-1/12)=113/88<9/7.

By (2), B_n/D -> Z_1/[2exp(1/4)]>7/9. Policies I and III, including their
larger stated families, would require qD>4/3 at every m>=1536. Thus any
sequence of PSD levels in those classes, on any unbounded subsequence,
would satisfy

    liminf B_n q >= (4/3) Z_1/[2exp(1/4)]>28/27>1.       (45)

Policy II would require the divergent loss (44), so its corresponding
B_n q levels tend to infinity if such PSD levels exist. Consequently none
of these policies can deliver a fixed relative improvement below the
original benchmark by using a sharper scalar B comparison either.

Again, (45) restricts the levels of THESE arrays; it is not a lower bound
on W_n(H_m). No feasibility at a higher level is asserted.

## 10. Precisely unpaid global inequality and what would have to change

The general original-cone problem after allowing optimum-outgoing rows is
now explicit. One must give final a_i,b_i,K_ij>=0, not necessarily obtained
by nonnegative additions to the screened arrays, such that (6) holds and

    C(a,b,K;h)-D aa^T/(4zeta) >=0                       (46)

on ALL n+1 original gradients, with C given by (11). The exact Huber
budget (5) and common-gradient restriction (8) are necessary. A desired
certificate would additionally prove a whole-word bound such as

    limsup_m zeta_m*(237/250)<1,

or prove another strict original-B comparison. One fixed loss at every
recursive level cannot be substituted for such a bound.

Equation (46) is exactly the full Schur condition of the original objective
certificate, by (12); by accepted1252's dual existence, unrestricted
minimization of its level is the original W problem. It is NOT a new easier
primitive lemma, and this draft does not advertise it as a routine bridge.
No surviving policy or new global PSD payment has been obtained.

The screens identify two concrete changes that a further attempt must pay.
For the asymmetric family, the moment identity in (25) leaves the left-common
curvature only 2s+kappa E. Balanced optimum cycles and backward optimum
triangles increase its mixed coefficient at the same time as they add
curvature, forcing (28). A useful finite-row reweighting must alter that
moment relation, not just distribute these same additions differently.
For the source array, retaining the right-copy factors (31) leaves the
rightmost small-block negative direction (42) with the exponential lower
requirement (44). Its inherited finite coefficients or a different full
completion must change; reallocation of a bounded mass of balanced optimum
cycles cannot fix it.

An arbitrary signed deltaK in (13)-(14), with final nonnegative rows, may
alter either restriction. General optimum-row certificates, general finite
circulations, and unrestricted asymmetric transports are not excluded.
Neither do the formal PSD test vectors in (24),(42) constitute actual
interpolation-feasible lower witnesses. The original exact-profile Goal
remains OPEN.

# Clipped scalar gradients: all common curvatures and an independent-curvature obstruction

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED REPORT.** Project gd-exact-profile-20260926,
worker max3, invocation78 after ended77, completed ceiling78 / attempted79,
controller cap1. No assertion newly derived here is a verified fact. The
assignment forbids formal calls; none was made. Every supporting argument
below is part of this one joint candidate, not an independently accepted
premise. There are exactly two analytical mechanisms: the complete common
curvature proof, then the independent-curvature extension and obstruction.

No mathematical program, numerical evaluation, arithmetic/trajectory/
recurrence replay, optimizer, solver, benchmark DP, installation, consult
or subagent was used. Administrative text reads/writes and integrity hashes
are the only local executed operations. No original task or problem was
changed. The original unrestricted minimax target remains OPEN.

## 1. Results and exact scope

Fix an integer m>=1536 and, throughout every child, use

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_m=1+2*S_m,
    n_m=2^m-1, ell=1+a, b=a*beta.

These are local hierarchy symbols. In particular b is a plateau factor,
not either clipping level. d_m is an auxiliary radius, not another step.
The largest actual level is m-1. All original physical steps, including
the final level-zero leaf ell, are retained.

For b_plus,b_minus>0 and kappa_plus,kappa_minus in[0,1], define ONE static
function on the whole line by

    g(x)=min(kappa_plus*x,b_plus), x>=0,
    g(x)=max(kappa_minus*x,-b_minus), x<=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t) dt.

Let Psi_j(s) be its actual full-word GD output from s using H_j,
for0<=j<=m, including the identity Psi_0.
The principal new candidate is the ENTIRE common-curvature law:

    kappa_plus=kappa_minus=kappa in(0,1] implies
    sup_(s!=0) 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2
          = kappa/(1+2*kappa*S_m).                    (1.1)

This is for EVERY fixed positive pair b_plus,b_minus, all starts, and
arbitrary dependence of parameters on m. It includes every effective-step
regime kappa*h<1, =1, between1 and2, =2 and >2. The supremum is attained on
each saturated ray. Taking the supremum also over common kappa gives1/D_m.

For independent positive curvatures put K=max(kappa_plus,kappa_minus), and

    kappa_0=1/(1+d_(m-2)).

A second exact candidate covers the complete low-curvature region:

    0<K<=kappa_0 implies
    sup_(s!=0) 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2
          = K/(1+2*K*S_m).                            (1.2)

Every trajectory here has at most one sign change, since only the root
can cross zero. Section12 proves this using the two different curvatures,
not by replacing the function with a common-curvature function.

When exactly one curvature is zero and the other is kappa>0, the exact
positive-own-distance profile is kappa/(1+2*kappa*S_m), for every
nonnegative physical word, not just H_m. If both vanish the objective is
zero; no quotient by a zero closest distance is taken.

The remaining independent-positive-curvature region is

    K>kappa_0, kappa_plus!=kappa_minus, both positive, (1.3)

with all clipping levels and starts retained. No full profile is asserted
there. Section13 gives a legal genuinely clipped full-word counterexample
to transplanting the common-curvature radial lemma using K. Its exact
objective is below1/D_m, so it refutes that intermediate reduction, not
the desired objective bound or the original minimax statement.

The original normalization remains W_n(h)=sup 2(F(x_n)-F_star)/
(L*||x_0-x_star||^2), with W=2R and the original all-n/all-nonnegative-h
quantifiers. A restricted scalar family upper bound is not an upper bound
on full W. Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 is used only for its unconditional
B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50; its full-W hypothesis17/(16D_m) stays unproved. Prior
unit-core draft987e49ed35eb24e7 was read as awareness; all premises needed
here are derived jointly. No assertion from the unclipped two-ray draft
bf10ce01c54882c8 is imported.

## 2. Static legality, all rows, and closest distance

For a ray with positive curvature kappa_sigma, let
u_sigma=b_sigma/kappa_sigma. With r=|x| on that ray,

    f(x)=kappa_sigma*r^2/2, 0<=r<=u_sigma,
    f(x)=b_sigma*r-b_sigma^2/(2*kappa_sigma), r>=u_sigma.

For zero curvature that entire ray has f=g=0. The derivative g is
continuous and nondecreasing. On every linear piece its slope is in[0,1].
Partitioning any interval at the finite breakpoints proves
|g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Thus f is finite, convex, differentiable and globally
1-smooth. For arbitrary advertised L>0, use F=L*f and physical steps h/L;
the scalar iterates are unchanged and the normalized objective is the
same.

The complete minimum set C is {0} when both curvatures are positive;
(-infinity,0] when only kappa_plus is positive; [0,infinity) when only
kappa_minus is positive; and the whole line when both are zero. The minimum
value is0. When the closest initial distance is positive it is exactly
|s| in the first three cases. Starts already in C are stationary and have
zero gap. In the identically zero case every original admissible pair has
zero payoff, while the closest-distance quotient is left undefined.

For every physical index, including the unqueried terminal, define

    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i),
    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), 0<=i<=n_m.

Every ordered original interpolation row follows directly. For x<y put
Delta=g(y)-g(x), so0<=Delta<=y-x. For x<=t<=y, monotonicity and smoothness
give

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).

Integrating each inequality gives at least Delta^2/2. These are precisely
the two ordered inequalities

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.       (2.1)

Equal locations give equality. This treats every remote pair, every
revisit, all threshold equalities, the terminal, and all minimizers.
The full Gram matrix of the actual scalar displacement and gradients is
positive semidefinite because each quadratic form is a square of a real
linear combination. No formal gradient coordinate or row is removed.

Spatial scaling multiplies both clipping levels and the initial position
by the same factor and leaves curvature unchanged. Removing a common
curvature from g instead changes EVERY effective step. Section3 does that
change explicitly; no word is silently replaced or reset.

## 3. Common curvature: effective word and constants

For common kappa>0 put u=b_plus/kappa and v=b_minus/kappa, reflect if
necessary so u>=v, and write f=kappa*F, where

    G(x)=F'(x)=clip(x,[-v,u]).

The actual update becomes x -> x-alpha_i*G(x), with alpha_i=kappa*h_i
at physical index i. Separately define the following level quantities:

    delta=1-kappa, R_j=kappa*d_j,
    t_j=kappa*(1+d_j)=kappa+R_j,
    e_j=t_j-1=R_j-delta,
    M_j=kappa*S_j, C_j=1+M_j=kappa*A_j+delta.

Here t_j is a level-j effective bridge, not physical time. The effective
word is exactly kappa*H_m. Its masses obey

    C_(j+1)=2*C_j+e_j, M_(j+1)=2*M_j+t_j,
    R_(j+1)=c*R_j.                                  (3.1)

Using epsilon<=1/6144 and sqrt(2)<17/12 gives

    12/5<c<49/20, beta>99/100,
    2/5<a<1/2, 2/5<b<1/2, c*b=beta^2<1.             (3.2)

For the lower bound on b, a>99/245 and therefore
b>9801/24500>2/5. The upper bounds use c>lambda and beta<1.
Let eta=a/(c-2). Since c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1,
0<eta<1. Summing the literal word gives

    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,
    A_j>=d_j/(c-2), A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    d_j/A_j<c-2<1/2.                                (3.3)

The first identity follows from A_0=1 and A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,
using2*eta+a=c*eta. Consequently

    M_j>=R_j/(c-2)-kappa,
    e_j/C_j<=d_j/A_j<1/2 whenever e_j>=0.            (3.4)

Also, with the local constant

    Q=1/(c-2)+1/c,

one has Q>1160/441>5/2>c. No evaluated hierarchy or numerical estimate
is used in these inequalities.

Every level-k occurrence in H_m has a literal H_k immediately before and
after it, so its physical prefix and suffix masses are at least S_k.
After its following H_k, the remaining suffix consists of increasing
ancestor levels, each followed by its full right child:

    H_k, t_(l_1),H_(l_1), ..., t_(l_r),H_(l_r),
    k<l_1<...<l_r<=m-1.                              (3.5)

The H symbols in this display have their effective steps kappa*H. This
is a decomposition of the actual binary tree: ascend past right-child
ancestors until reaching the next left-child ancestor, then take its
bridge and right child. At k=0 the first H_0 is empty. At the final leaf
there is no remaining component.

## 4. Exact signed step images and a monotone-block bound

For common curvature, an interval[-p,q] contains0 and its image under
a step t remains an interval containing0. If0<=t<=1, the map is
nondecreasing, and the exact endpoint magnitudes are

    p'=p-t*min(p,v), q'=q-t*min(q,u).                 (4.1)

If t>=1, writing e=t-1, the exact endpoint magnitudes are

    p'=max(e*min(q,u),p-t*v),
    q'=max(e*min(p,v),q-t*u).                        (4.2)

For(4.2), each ray has slope-e in its core and slope1 on its tail;
its extrema are at a threshold or an endpoint. The first terms in the
maxima are nonnegative. Formulae(4.1)-(4.2) coincide at t=1 and include
all endpoint/threshold equalities. At t=2 the core slope is-1, and for
t>2 it is less than-1; neither case is omitted.

Write Phi_j(p,q) for the TWO nonnegative endpoint magnitudes of the
exact image Psi_j([-p,q]). Continuity makes both endpoints attained.
Interval inclusion makes Phi_j coordinatewise nondecreasing. Composition
with(4.1) or(4.2) is exact for the whole interval. Bounds below therefore
apply to compatible actual child histories, not independently chosen
child maximizers. This interface is used only symbolically.

Consider any block all of whose effective steps lie in[0,1], of total
mass M. Sign is preserved, and on a ray with threshold w its radius map
is a composition of r -> r-t*min(r,w). Each such map is convex and
nondecreasing on r>=0, fixes0, and has slopes1-t,1. The complete map is
therefore convex, nondecreasing and fixes0. In particular

    B(theta*r)<=theta*B(r), 0<=theta<=1.             (4.3)

There is also an explicit upper bound by the clipped continuous flow:

    B(r)<=V_M(r;w),
    V_M(r;w)=r*exp(-M),                       r<=w,
             r-M*w,                         r>=w and M<=r/w-1,
             w*exp(r/w-1-M),                 r>=w and M>=r/w-1. (4.4)

Here(4.4) is an inequality proved from the displayed elementary maps,
not an invocation of an external flow theorem. One step stays linear
on a tail until the threshold. A crossing step has residual radius
w*(1-z)<=w*exp(-z), z>=0. A core step satisfies1-t<=exp(-t).
The displayed maps V form a semigroup by direct substitution and are
nondecreasing in their input. Induction over the block proves(4.4).
Increasing the ray threshold can only decrease the radius map for these
nondecreasing steps, so a smaller comparison threshold is legitimate.

If the terminal radius exceeds w, all queried radii are at least that
terminal radius, and every gradient radius is at least w. Hence

    B(r)<=max(w,r-M*w).                              (4.5)

This last assertion also follows directly from the physical updates.

## 5. All common curvatures: the starting level for plateaus

The plateau factor is the SAME b from(3.2). Define a starting level J.
If kappa*ell>=1, set J=1. If kappa*ell<1, let J be the smallest positive
integer with R_J>=delta, if such an integer is needed within the word.
Then

    delta<=R_J<c*delta,
    every physical step in H_J is less than1.       (5.1)

If no such J occurs before m, the entire word is sign preserving and is
already covered by Section9. Whenever this section is used, J<=m.
For all j>=J the bridges have e_j>=0 and e_j<=R_j.

The base plateau is, for every integer r>=0,

    Phi_J(R_J*w*b^r,R_J*w*b^r)
              <=(w*b^(r+1),w*b^(r+1)).             (5.2)

Here choose w=u for1<=u/v<=2, and w=v for u/v>=2.
We prove both choices directly.

First take kappa*ell<1. Put R=R_J and M=M_J. By(3.4),(5.1),

    M>=R/(c-2)-kappa, delta>R/c,
    M>=R/(c-2)-1+delta.                              (5.3)

For w=v, both ray thresholds are at least v, so(4.4) applies with
comparison threshold v. If R<=1, the output divided by v is at most

    R*exp(-M)<=exp(1)*R*exp(-Q*R)<=1/Q<2/5<b.

The middle function has derivative exp(1-Q*R)*(1-Q*R), so its global
maximum is1/Q at R=1/Q. If R>=1, (5.3) gives M>R-1, and the output
is at most exp(R-1-M)<exp(-(Q-1)*R)<2/5. The last bound follows from
Q>5/2, R>=1, and exp(z)>=1+z. This proves(5.2) at r=0.

For w=u and u/v<=2, both thresholds are at least u/2. Compare with
threshold u/2. If R<=1/2 the same R*exp(-M) bound is below b. If
R>=1/2, (5.3) gives M>2*R-1, and the output divided by u is at most

    (1/2)*exp(2*R-1-M)
       <(1/2)*exp(-(Q-2)*R)
       <=(1/2)/(1+1/4)=2/5<b.

Here Q-2>1/2 and R>=1/2. Convexity(4.3), separately on each ray,
now proves(5.2) for all r>=0 in BOTH cases.

If kappa*ell>=1, put e_0=kappa*ell-1 in[0,a]. Also R_1=kappa*beta<1.
For w=v the entire base interval is in the common core, so both endpoint
magnitudes are e_0*R_1*v*b^r<=a*beta*v*b^r=b*v*b^r.
For w=u and u/v<=2, apply(4.2). The core extremum is at most
e_0*R_1*u*b^r<=b*u*b^r. The only additional tail term is

    R_1*u*b^r-t_0*v
       <=b^r*(R_1*u-t_0*v)
       <=b^r*u*kappa*(beta-ell/2)<=b^(r+1)*u.

Indeed beta-ell/2<=b, since
b-beta+ell/2=(1+a)/2-beta*(1-a)>(3*a-1)/2>0;
if beta-ell/2 is negative the desired upper bound remains immediate.
This proves the base plateau in every case, including kappa*ell=1.

For the large-ratio proof we also need the positive endpoint at the
larger base radius:

    second coordinate of Phi_J(c*R_J*v,c*R_J*v)<=v. (5.4)

In the low-curvature base, negative inputs stay negative. A positive
input has threshold at least2v. If c*R<=2, (4.4) bounds the positive
endpoint divided by v by
exp(1)*c*R*exp(-Q*R)<=c/Q<1. If c*R>=2, it is bounded by

    2*exp(c*R/2-1-M)
       <2*exp(-(Q-c/2)*R)
       <=2*exp(-(2*Q/c-1))<2/exp(1)<1.

The same inequalities show that the threshold is reached before the
end; Q>c is sufficient. In the high-curvature base, a negative input
can return to at most e_0*v<=a*v. A positive core input has nonpositive
output. A positive saturated input at most c*R_1*v has output at most
kappa*(c*beta-2*ell)*v<0, because c<49/20 and2*ell>14/5.
Thus(5.4) holds in this case too.

## 6. Ratio at most two: exact capture and all higher plateaus

Assume1<=u/v<=2. For every j<=m,

    Psi_j([-C_j*v,C_j*u])=[-v,u].                   (6.1)

An exterior start x>=C_j*u stays positive saturated at every query
and ends at x-M_j*u; the negative exterior has the analogous affine
formula. Indeed each physical prefix has mass at most M_j, leaving
radius at least its threshold. This proves the exterior assertion
without any sign assumption on core slopes.

For(6.1), the empty child is the identity. If(6.1) holds for j, the
first child of the parent interval has exact endpoint magnitudes
(C_j+t_j)*v and(C_j+t_j)*u, by the just proved exterior formula and
its central capture interval. If t_j<=1, (4.1) sends these to C_j*v
and C_j*u. If t_j>=1, (4.2) does the same: its cross terms satisfy
e_j*u<=C_j*v and e_j*v<=C_j*u by e_j/C_j<1/2 and u/v<=2. The
second child then gives exactly[-v,u]. This completes the induction.

We extend(5.2) to every j>=J, with w=u. Suppose it holds for j.
For the unscaled parent radius c*R_j*u, (6.1) and its exterior
branches bound the first-child endpoints by

    p<=max(v,c*R_j*u-M_j*v),
    q<=max(u,c*R_j*u-M_j*u).

The bridge has e_j>=0. Its core cross terms are at most R_j*u.
Its negative tail term is at most
max(-e_j*v,c*R_j*u-(M_j+t_j)*v), and its positive tail term is at
most max(-e_j*u,(c*R_j-M_j-t_j)*u). Both are at most R_j*u because

    M_j+t_j=kappa*A_j+R_j
              >=(1+1/(c-2))*R_j,
    (c-1)*R_j*(u/v)<=2*(c-1)*R_j<=M_j+t_j.

The last inequality uses c-2<1/2. Thus the ACTUAL bridge image is
contained in[-R_j*u,R_j*u], and the second child's plateau bounds
both terminal endpoints by b*u.

For r>=1, the parent radius is
c*R_j*u*b^r=(c*b)*R_j*u*b^(r-1)<=R_j*u*b^(r-1).
The first-child plateau bounds its image by u*b^r on both sides.
Since2*b<1, this interval is inside both core thresholds. The bridge
multiplies absolute values by e_j<=R_j. The second child's plateau
at r then gives u*b^(r+1) on both sides. This proves

    Phi_j(R_j*u*b^r,R_j*u*b^r)
                  <=(u*b^(r+1),u*b^(r+1))          (6.2)

for every j>=J and every integer r>=0.

## 7. Ratio at least two: joint drift, returns and plateaus

Assume u>=2v. For every j>=J, prove together

    |Psi_j(s)|<=max(v,|s|-M_j*v/3) for every real s, (7.1)
    Phi_j(R_j*v*b^r,R_j*v*b^r)
                  <=(v*b^(r+1),v*b^(r+1)), r>=0.   (7.2)

The base plateau was proved in Section5. If the base block has steps
less than1, compare both rays with the smaller threshold v, as justified
after(4.4), and apply(4.5). This proves(7.1). For the high-curvature leaf base,
a negative-to-positive output is at most e_0*v<v. A positive output
larger than v must come from a positive saturated input and loses t_0*u.
A negative output-p with p>v, from a negative input, loses t_0*v.
From a positive input s it satisfies p<=e_0*s. If e_0=0 this is
impossible; otherwise e_0<=a<1/2 gives
s-p>[(1-e_0)/e_0]*v>v>t_0*v/3, since t_0<3/2.
These cases prove the base drift.

We first describe a consequence of already proved lower-level plateaus.
Any negative-to-positive crossing at level k>=J lands at most e_k*v
<=R_k*v. Its following actual H_k captures it to b*v. Each later
ancestor in(3.5) sees radius at most b*v<v, has core multiplier
of magnitude e_l<=R_l, and its H_l captures it again to b*v.
If the high-curvature case permits a level-zero crossing, its landing
is at most e_0*v<=a*v<v. If it is the final leaf that is the actual
terminal. Otherwise its next ancestor has level at least1 and the
same plateau argument applies. In the low-curvature case levels k<J
cannot cross at all. Thus whenever the needed proper-child plateaus
are available, a negative-to-positive return implies terminal radius
at most a*v. A zero hit stays zero. This consequence is used only with
levels strictly below the block whose drift or plateau is being proved.

In particular, for a child H_j with j>J, the positive endpoint at input
radius c*R_j*v is at most a*v. A trajectory with a return has that bound.
Without such a return, any positive terminal must come from an entirely
positive trajectory. Its root level j-1 has t_(j-1)>=1. Remaining positive
there forces a saturated root query, and all earlier positive queries
are saturated because positions decrease. Its initial s must satisfy

    s>=(M_(j-1)+t_(j-1))*u
       >=2*(1+1/(c-2))*R_(j-1)*v
       >c^2*R_(j-1)*v=c*R_j*v.                     (7.3)

The strict comparison follows from c^2<25/4 and
2*(1+1/(c-2))>58/9>25/4. This contradicts the input radius.
For j=J the necessary bound by v is(5.4). Therefore in EVERY induction
step the first child's positive endpoint is at most v.

Suppose both(7.1)-(7.2) hold at j. Write R=R_j, M=M_j, t=t_j,
e=e_j. At parent input radius c*R*v, the first child has

    p<=max(v,(c*R-M/3)*v), q<=v.

Also p<=(kappa+2*R)*v. Indeed kappa+2*R>=1 because R>=delta, and

    (c-2)*R<=kappa+M/3

follows from M>=R/(c-2)-kappa and(c-2)^2<1/3. Formula(4.2) now
puts BOTH bridge endpoint magnitudes at most R*v: e*q<=R*v,
p-t*v<=R*v, e*min(p,v)<=R*v, and q-t*u<=0. The second child's
plateau proves the parent plateau at r=0. For r>=1 the geometric
argument of Section6 applies with w=v: c*b<1 places the first child
in its previous radius, its image is in the common core, and e<=R
allows the second plateau. Thus(7.2) is proved at j+1 BEFORE its drift.

For the parent drift write y=Psi_j(s), z=y-t*G(y), w=Psi_j(z).
Only |w|>v needs proof. If w>v, the return consequence, using the
already available proper-child plateaus, excludes any negative-to-
positive crossing. The whole trajectory is then positive. Its gradient
at every query is at least min(w,u)>v, so
s-w>=M_(j+1)*v, stronger than required.

Let w=-p with p>v. Child drift and child plateau give respectively

    |z|>=p+M*v/3, |z|>R*v.                          (7.4)

There are three possible bridge cases.

A. If z>0, a negative y could land at most e*v<=R*v, contradicting
(7.4). Thus y>0 stays positive across the bridge. Because t>=1,
this query is saturated and y=z+t*u>u. The first child's terminal y>u
excludes a return; all of its queries are positive saturated. Hence
s=z+(M+t)*u>=p+M*v/3+(M+t)*v>=p+(2*M+t)*v/3.

B. If z<0 and y<0, a core input would be nonnegative after the bridge.
Thus the query is negative saturated, |y|=|z|+t*v>v, and applying child
drift to the first child yields
|s|>=|y|+M*v/3>=p+2*M*v/3+t*v>=p+(2*M+t)*v/3.
The inequality |y|>v also follows from R+t=kappa+2*R>=1.

C. If z<0 and y>0, then |z|<=e*min(y,u). By(7.4), e<=R implies
y>v and min(y,u)>v. The first-child output y>v excludes returns, so
that entire child is positive and

    s>=y+M*min(y,u).

If y<=u, then |z|=e*y and
s-|z|>=(1+M-e)*y. If y>=u, then |z|=t*u-y and
s-|z|>=2*y+(M-t)*u>=(1+M-e)*u. In either case

    3*(1+M-e)-(M+t)
       =6-4*kappa+2*M-4*R
       >=6*delta+2*R*(1/(c-2)-2)>=0.                (7.5)

In particular1+M-e>0, and s-|z|>=(M+t)*v/3.
Combining with(7.4) gives s>=p+(2*M+t)*v/3.

Zero bridge inputs or outputs cannot occur in these cases. The cases
are exhaustive. Since M_(j+1)=2*M+t, they prove(7.1) at j+1 and
complete the joint induction. All child histories and both endpoints
remain actual. No unit-core positive-tail assertion was used.

For the FULL H_m, the same physical suffix argument now gives

    a negative-to-positive crossing => |Psi_m(s)|<=a*v. (7.6)

The final leaf uses a*v, not b*v; it has not been deleted.

## 8. Radial consequence and exact overlap of curvature ranges

Either plateau(6.2) with w=u or(7.2) with w=v implies

    |Psi_j(s)|<=|s|/R_j for0<|s|<=R_j*w, j>=J.      (8.1)

Choose an integer r>=0 such that
R_j*w*b^(r+1)<|s|<=R_j*w*b^r. The plateau bounds the output by
w*b^(r+1)<|s|/R_j. If an amplitude is a geometric boundary, take it
as the upper boundary of its next cell. Zero maps to zero. This covers
every real amplitude in the specified interval, not a sampled mesh.

We need the final comparison

    R_m^2>=1+2*kappa*S_m.                            (8.2)

It holds for EVERY kappa>=kappa_0=1/(1+d_(m-2)). Here is an explicit
uniform overlap proof. Let d=d_(m-2). By a>2/5,c>2,m>=1536, d>20,
so(d/(1+d))^2>9/10. By A_m<c^m and d_m=c^2*d,

    (kappa_0*d_m)^2>(9/10)*c^4,
    1+2*kappa_0*S_m<1+2*c^2/a<1+5*c^2.

For x=c^2>144/25>23/4, the polynomial(9/10)*x^2-5*x-1 is positive:
it equals1/160 at23/4 and has positive derivative for x>=23/4.
Thus(8.2) holds strictly at kappa_0. For larger kappa the equivalent
inequality d_m^2>=1/kappa^2+2*S_m/kappa only becomes weaker.
This proves(8.2) throughout the advertised range.

Also J exists below m in this range: at kappa_0,
R_(m-1)=c*d/(1+d)>1, and it increases with kappa. Since delta<1,
the low-curvature starting level, if needed, is at most m-1.
No unproved asymptotic overlap or rounding choice is hidden here.

## 9. All no-crossing and single-crossing histories

This section is independent of clipping ratio. Consider a segment of
common-curvature effective mass M, staying on one ray sigma, with initial
radius r and terminal radius w. Write F_sigma(w)=F(w) on the positive
ray and F_sigma(w)=F(-w) on the negative ray. Its radii are nonincreasing and
r>=w+M*min(w,u_sigma). Directly from the objective pieces,

    2*F_sigma(w)<=r^2/(1+2*M).                       (9.1)

If w<=u_sigma, use w<=r/(1+M) and(1+M)^2>=1+2*M.
If w>=u_sigma, use r>=w+M*u_sigma and

    (w+M*u_sigma)^2
       -(1+2*M)*(2*u_sigma*w-u_sigma^2)
          =(w-(1+M)*u_sigma)^2>=0.

These arguments include a zero terminal and equality at the threshold.

Suppose exactly one sign crossing occurs at level k, at a physical
position whose effective prefix and suffix masses are P,T. Let y>0
be its pre-crossing radius, after reflection if necessary. The entire
prefix stayed on its ray, so with q=min(y,u_source),

    |s|>=y+P*q>=(1+P)*q,
    crossing radius r=t_k*q-y<=e_k*q
                         <=e_k*|s|/(1+P).          (9.2)

A crossing requires e_k>0. The suffix stays on the other ray and (9.1)
applies there. It remains to prove the exact positional inequality

    e_k^2/[(1+P)^2*(1+2*T)]<=1/(1+2*M_m).           (9.3)

Put C=C_k>=1, e=e_k>0, and N=M_m-M_(k+1)>=0. For some x in[0,N],
P=M_k+x, T=M_k+N-x. The denominator in(9.3) is

    (C+x)^2*(2*C-1+2*N-2*x).

Its logarithm is concave, so its minimum occurs at an endpoint. The
endpoint x=N minus the endpoint x=0 equals
N*(2*C*(C-1)+N*(2*C-1))>=0. Thus the denominator is at least
C^2*(2*C-1+2*N), while1+2*M_m=4*C+2*e-1+2*N.
By e/C<1/2,

    e^2*(4*C+2*e-1)/(C^2*(2*C-1))
       <(5*C-1)/(4*(2*C-1))<=1,

the last inequality being3*(C-1)>=0. Adding the2*N terms preserves
the inequality because e^2<C^2. This proves(9.3) for every position,
including both endpoint leaves. Combining(9.1)-(9.3) and f=kappa*F
gives score at most kappa/(1+2*kappa*S_m).

If kappa<=kappa_0, every non-root step has effective size at most1.
Such a step preserves sign globally by(4.1). The only possible crossing
is at the root, so this section proves the desired upper bound for the
ENTIRE common-curvature family in that region. Root and threshold
equalities cause no exception; a zero hit remains zero.

## 10. Complete common-curvature proof and attainment

It remains to cover kappa>=kappa_0. Then(8.2) holds and all plateaus
needed at m exist.

For1<=u/v<=2, take a start in the capture rectangle of(6.1). If
|s|<=R_m*u, (8.1) and2*f(x)<=kappa*x^2 give score at most
kappa/R_m^2<=kappa/(1+2*kappa*S_m). If |s|>R_m*u, capture gives
|Psi_m(s)|<=u, leading to the same strict upper bound. Starts outside
the capture rectangle follow their exact saturated affine branches;
(9.1), or its displayed square identity, gives the required bound.

For u/v>=2, if |s|<=R_m*v use(8.1) exactly as just done. If the
initial radius is larger and a negative-to-positive crossing occurs,
(7.6) gives score at most kappa*a^2/R_m^2, also below the desired
bound. Without a negative-to-positive crossing, a nonzero trajectory
has no sign change or has exactly one positive-to-negative crossing.
Section9 covers both. A trajectory hitting zero has zero gap.

The two clipping-ratio domains cover every ratio after reflection. The
low- and high-curvature domains overlap at kappa_0 with the exact
comparison in Section8. Thus every kappa in(0,1], all clips, both signs,
and all amplitudes are included.

For attainment, let D_kappa=1+2*kappa*S_m and choose s=D_kappa*u.
At every actual index i, with physical prefix mass P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t,

    x_i=u*(D_kappa-kappa*P_i)>=u,
    x_(n_m)=u*(1+kappa*S_m),
    2*f(x_(n_m))=kappa*D_kappa*u^2.

The own initial radius is D_kappa*u and the attained score is
kappa/D_kappa. Choosing s=-D_kappa*v gives the reflected attainment.
These are queries of the one fixed function in Section2 and satisfy
all its ordered rows. This proves the joint candidate(1.1).

As a function of kappa, the now-proved profile kappa/(1+2*kappa*S_m)
has positive derivative1/(1+2*kappa*S_m)^2. This is a consequence of
the proof, not an assumed curvature monotonicity of trajectories.
Its maximum for0<kappa<=1 is1/D_m. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 therefore
gives the conditional-on-this-draft restricted-family benchmark deficit
B_(n_m)*sup E<47/50. It gives NO unrestricted full-W upper bound.

## 11. Independent curvatures: exact interval interface

This begins the second analytical mechanism. Return to the original
physical h and the independent curvatures of Section2. For a ray sigma
and radius r>=0 define

    Stay_sigma(r)=max(0,r-h*min(kappa_sigma*r,b_sigma)),
    Cross_sigma(r)=(h*kappa_sigma-1)_+*
                       min(r,b_sigma/kappa_sigma),  if kappa_sigma>0,
    Cross_sigma(r)=0,                               if kappa_sigma=0.

The notation(z)_+ means max(z,0). On a zero-curvature ray Stay_sigma(r)=r.
For a true interval[-p,q], the exact next signed endpoint magnitudes are

    p'=max(Stay_minus(p),Cross_plus(q)),
    q'=max(Stay_plus(q),Cross_minus(p)).              (11.1)

On each ray the slope is1-h*kappa_sigma in the core and1 on the tail.
The largest opposite-sign excursion occurs at the smaller of the input
radius and its true threshold. The largest same-sign output is the
positive part of the endpoint image. This proves(11.1), including
h*kappa_sigma=1 or2, all threshold ties and zero curvatures.

Both endpoints remain essential: the two sign-conditioned switching
levels may interlace, and one side's small-step property says nothing
about the other. Equation(11.1) is an exact interface, not a claimed
solution by a formal recurrence.

## 12. Independent curvatures in the root-only region; flat boundary

Let0<K=max(kappa_plus,kappa_minus)<=kappa_0, with both curvatures positive.
All physical steps except the root satisfy h*kappa_sigma<=1 on BOTH
rays, so they preserve sign. At most one crossing is possible.

For a no-crossing segment of physical mass T on a ray of curvature q>0,
the proof of(9.1), rescaled by that q alone, gives twice terminal objective at
most q*r^2/(1+2*q*T), where r is the segment's starting radius. If a
single crossing has source curvature p>0, destination curvature q>0,
physical prefix mass P, crossing step h, and suffix mass T, then its
landing radius is at most

    (h*p-1)*|s|/(1+p*P),                             (12.1)

and h*p>1 is necessary. Thus its full score is at most

    [q/(1+2*q*T)]*[(h*p-1)/(1+p*P)]^2.              (12.2)

Both factors are increasing in their own positive curvature on the
crossing domain: their unsquared derivatives are respectively
1/(1+2*q*T)^2 and(h+P)/(1+p*P)^2. Replacing p and q in this UPPER
expression by K, not replacing the underlying function, bounds(12.2)
by the common-K single-cut expression from(9.3). It is at most
K/(1+2*K*S_m). The no-crossing expression has the same upper bound.
The saturated ray with curvature K attains it, using the start given
in Section10 on that ray. This proves the complete joint candidate(1.2)
for every clipping pair, start and threshold equality.

If exactly one curvature is zero, an active-side trajectory either stays
on the active ray or crosses into the flat minimum half-line and stays
there forever. The crossing case has zero terminal objective; the other
case is bounded by the no-crossing inequality. Its saturated active ray
attains kappa/(1+2*kappa*S) for ANY nonnegative word of physical mass S,
including empty words, zero steps and arbitrarily large steps. The own
distance is the active initial radius. If both curvatures are zero the
objective is identically zero, as specified in Section2.

No profile beyond(1.3) follows from these arguments.

## 13. Actual clipped counterexample to the max-curvature radial transfer

The common-curvature proof uses(8.1) at radius R_m*w with R_m=kappa*d_m.
A tempting independent-curvature extension substitutes K=max(kappa_plus,
kappa_minus) while retaining the true coordinate thresholds. That
intermediate assertion is FALSE on the complete original H_m.

Write S=S_m and retain the same ell,a,d_m. Define ONE static function by

    kappa_plus=1, kappa_minus=mu=1/(4*S),
    b_plus=1, b_minus=mu*a/2=a/(8*S),
    u_plus=1, u_minus=a/2,
    x_0=1.

Both curvatures are strictly positive and at most1. The minimum set is
exactly{0}; the own radius is1. The first physical step is ell, so the
threshold query g(1)=1 gives x_1=-a. For EVERY later physical query define

    T_i=sum_(t=1)^(i-1) h_t, 1<=i<=n_m,
    x_i=-a+b_minus*T_i.

Since0<=T_i<=S-ell<S, its radius is greater than7*a/8>a/2. Every such
query and the terminal therefore lies on the negative saturated tail of
this SAME function. Its gradient is exactly-b_minus, so the displayed
formula verifies each original update, not just a block endpoint.
All ordered rows, including the first threshold query and minimizer,
follow from the direct proof in Section2. The terminal radius is

    r=a*(7/8+ell/(8*S)).                             (13.1)

The proposed max-K version of(8.1) would apply to s=1 because
K=1, w=min(u_plus,u_minus)=a/2, and d_m*a/2>1, and would give
r<=1/d_m. In fact r>7*a/8>1/d_m: a>2/5 and d_m>20 already imply
both strict comparisons. Thus this is an exact full-word counterexample
to that radial lemma. Its negative clipping is active on the entire
post-leaf suffix; it is not an unclipped-ray reduction.

The terminal objective and normalized payoff are also exact:

    E=2*f(x_(n_m))
       =2*b_minus*r-b_minus^2/mu
       =a^2*(5*S+ell)/(32*S^2).                    (13.2)

This is NOT an improving witness. Since S>=ell>1 and a<1/2,
E<=3*a^2/(16*S), and D_m<3*S gives D_m*E<9/64<1.
The weak curvature pays for the large surviving radius. Ignoring that
signed objective weight is precisely what makes the false reduction
look stronger than it is.

The counterexample lies in(1.3): K=1>kappa_0 and mu!=1. It leaves the
independent-curvature objective conjecture unrefuted. A future proof must
control both actual terminal energies with their own curvatures, not
just a K-based radius. The exact unresolved target for that conjecture is

    2*f(-p_m(R,R))<=K*R^2/(1+2*K*S_m),
    2*f( q_m(R,R))<=K*R^2/(1+2*K*S_m),              (13.3)

for every R>0 and every parameter choice in(1.3), where p_m,q_m are the
exact interval endpoints from(11.1). Continuity makes each endpoint
attained; monotonicity of f away from0 makes these two inequalities
equivalent to the asserted pointwise family upper bound. Tail attainment
would make the profile exact if(13.3) were proved. It is NOT proved here.

## 14. Audit, retrieval and handoff

The common-curvature proof does not assume monotonicity in curvature or
clipping ratio. Its two curvature regions overlap by an exact inequality;
its ratio split is exhaustive at2. All low levels are retained and bounded
by a monotone-block argument, while later core slopes may have either
magnitude below or above1. At a killing step t=1, zeros persist and both
endpoint formulae agree. At t=2 no estimate assumes strict contraction.
The last physical leaf receives its actual a*v allowance.
Textual audit corrected the positive-tail argument's root eligibility
from t>1 to t>=1: at t=1 a core query lands at zero and cannot have a
positive terminal. Thus the saturated-root argument includes that tie.

The plateau/drift induction is not circular. Base plateaus and drift are
proved from literal low-step blocks or one leaf. At each next level,
proper-child plateaus give the return cap and positive endpoint bound;
child drift proves the parent plateau; then that plateau and child facts
prove parent drift. No parent assertion is used to prove itself. The
same-m constants stay fixed throughout.

Mechanism2 does not re-label its exact interval recurrence as an entire
parameter law. It proves the complete root-only independent-curvature
region and gives a physical counterexample to the proposed radial
extension. The precise remaining region and two endpoint objective
inequalities are(1.3),(13.3). The original all-function/all-word target
is farther beyond this restricted interface and remains OPEN.

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving,
construct-counterexamples. Matlas search returned three inapplicable
results (polyhedral shadow descent, stochastic ADMM, and a PL condition
for deep optimization). A second independent-curvature query returned two
inapplicable statements about learning a graph by conjugate gradients.
Web retrieval supplied silver-schedule background,
not a proof premise. The exact known local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur
1502.05666 text was read at Section2.4 and both directions of Lemma2.
No external theorem was imported. All rows and all new estimates above
were proved directly within this joint draft.

This report follows TASK.md and master_guidance5c34172adf354c48. The plan
record is9f7dc514b4fe78b9. Formal calls, fact_submit, new accepted facts,
mathematical programs, numeric or trajectory replays, solvers, benchmark
DP, installations, consults and subagents are all ZERO. There is no
outward publication, finalization, fact revocation, service/worker control,
shutdown, automatic renewal or declaration of partial Goal completion.

**Common-curvature theorem: JOINT UNVERIFIED. Independent large-curvature
region: OPEN. Original minimax target: OPEN. New fact IDs: none.**

## 15. A backward extension strengthens the middle triangle

This is a new joint DRAFT calculation in the seventh launch's final round.
It is not a fact and consumes no verifier or numerical allowance. All four
steps retain their order. Write

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), a,b,c,d>0,
    C=5+a+b+c+d, P=2+a, D=2+d, U=C-P,
    H=U-b=1+c+D, eta=b/H,
    kappa(a)=(1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,
    kappa_plus=max(kappa(a),0),
    R=P^2-kappa_plus, J=P^2-kappa_plus*(1+eta).

The preceding full-domain derivation gives a<1 and the b-cut gives
eta<=P/C, hence a*eta<1 (indeed a*P/C<3/7). Then a stronger necessary
inequality than the middle adjacent triangle is

    b*c*[R+b(P+C)] <= D*J.                           (43)

For a<=sqrt(2)-1 this is exactly(34). For a>sqrt(2)-1 the positive
term subtracted from the old triangle slack is strictly positive.
No relation between c and b, or saturation assumption, is imposed.

Here is the entire new interpolation check. The case D<=b*c is already
excluded by the explicit capped function in Section11. Thus D>b*c. Set

    K=b^2*c+U*(D-b*c)=U*D-b*c*H,
    r=b^2*c/K,
    u=(1,0,0), w=(r,sqrt(r*(1-r)),0),
    v=-(b*u+D*w)/(1+c).

We have 0<r<1, <u,w>=||w||^2=r, and 0 lies in conv{u,v,w}.
These are exactly the middle triangle vectors, but its initial prefix
will now change. Choose t in[-eta,1] and put

    q=(1+(a-1)*t)/a,
    z=t*u+sqrt(q-t^2)*(0,0,1).

The radicand is (1-t)(1+a*t)/a>=0 because a*eta<1. Define the actual
positions and gradients by

    x=(u+(1+a)*z, u, -b*u, D*w, w),
    g=(z,u,v,w,w),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

The four GD equalities hold. Add the optimum jet(0,0,0). Each proximal
point y_i=x_i-g_i must support conv{z,u,v,w} at g_i. This suffices for
all ordered Q because Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.

At i=0, y_0=u+a*z. Since t+a*q=1+a*t and every old vertex y in
{0,u,v,w} has <u,y><=1,

    <y-z,y_0>=(1+a*t)(<u,y>-1)<=0.

At i=1 and i=4 the proximal point is zero. At i=3 it equals(1+d)*w.
Its pairing with z-w is (1+d)*r*(t-1)<=0; pairings with u-w and v-w
are respectively zero and negative, as explicitly computed in Section11.

At i=2 let n=-b*u-v. The two old vertex comparisons are

    <w-v,n>=0, <u-v,n>=-b*(1-r)*c/(1+c)<0.

The remaining comparison with z is exact. Put A_num=D*r-b*c. Then

    <u,n>=A_num/(1+c),
    <v,n>=r*(D-b*c)/(1+c),
    A_num=-b*c*H*(D-b*c)/K<0,
    r*(D-b*c)=-eta*A_num.

The fresh coordinate of z is orthogonal to n. Consequently

    <z-v,n>=(t+eta)*A_num/(1+c)<=0.

The optimum comparisons follow as 0 is already a convex combination
of u,v,w. This checks every original ordered pair, including those
involving the modified initial gradient. Fact1252b8823bb5a290 realizes
the complete array by one global convex1-smooth function with minimizer0.
No gradient Gram from any other schedule has been reused.

The energy is ||x_0||^2-C^2*r by the directly derived identity(3), and

    ||x_0||^2
      =1+2(1+a)t+(1+a)^2*q
      =P^2+kappa(a)*(t-1).

Choose t=1 if kappa(a)<=0 and t=-eta otherwise. The initial squared
norm is exactly J. Nonnegative energy therefore requires

    C^2*b^2*c <= J*K.

Using K=U*D-b*c*H, C-P=U, and J*H=P^2*H-kappa_plus*U gives

    C^2*b^2*c-J*K
      =U*{b*c*[P^2-kappa_plus+b(P+C)]-D*J},

which proves(43). Compared to the original triangle's forbidden-side
expression, the new left-minus-right expression increases by

    kappa_plus*[D*(1+eta)-b*c]
      =kappa_plus*K/H.

This is strictly positive whenever a>sqrt(2)-1. Thus(43) is a genuine
strengthening throughout that region, rather than a relabeling of the
old test. This last algebraic comparison is a strength comparison only;
it does not by itself prove there exists a word passing every old test
and failing the new one.

For rational elimination on the a>sqrt(2)-1 branch, use

    R=a+3+1/a,
    J=R-kappa(a)*b/(C-2-a-b),
    c <= J*(C-3-a-b)/(J+b*[R+b(C+2+a)]),              (44)

where c+d=C-5-a-b and D=2+d. All denominators are positive: H>0,
J=||x_0||^2>0, b>0, R>0. The strict positivity of J follows also
because its fresh coordinate is nonzero at t=-eta. Equations(43),(44)
retain the independent variables C,a,b,d (with c recovered by the sum).
