# Coupled ellipsoid pilot: multiplier drainage, paid radius, and the core-return gap

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED proof draft of the scoped results below.
The complete centered-ellipsoid payoff question is BLOCKED at Section 10.
This is round 47, the one analytical round authorized by TASK.md and
master_guidance eb57bb7a5bebd161. No numerical, solver, machine-algebra,
DP/replay, formal-verifier, consult, or subagent call is used. No fact is
claimed. All new mathematical ingredients are proved jointly here.

## 1. Selected concrete family and result

All norms and inner products are Euclidean. Let A be a symmetric positive
definite matrix on a finite-dimensional real space. Write

    d_min = smallest eigenvalue of A,
    d_max = largest eigenvalue of A,  kappa = d_max/d_min,
    E_A = {g : <g,A g> <= 1},
    F_A(x) = max_(g in E_A) {<x,g> - ||g||^2/2}.

I denotes the identity matrix and ||u||_A^2=<u,A u>. The d_l and x_l in
principal-coordinate formulas denote the eigenvalues of A and the
corresponding coordinates of x. Along a trajectory write g_i=g(x_i),
t_i=t(x_i), v_i=v(x_i), and f_i=F_A(x_i).

The selected family has 1 <= kappa <= 2. Thus the squared ratio of its
longest to shortest semiaxis is at most 2. Dimension, orientation, scale,
individual eigenvalues, and initial point may all depend on the ENTIRE
original word. At least two distinct active eigenvalues are permitted;
no common scalar quadratic orbit is assumed. This family is centered.
It does not include arbitrary translated or more eccentric ellipsoids.

Define t(x)=0 when x belongs to E_A. Otherwise t(x)>0 is the unique root

    sum_l d_l x_l^2/(1+t(x)d_l)^2 = 1,                     (1)

in orthonormal principal coordinates of A. Set v(x)=d_max t(x).
The actual gradient is g(x)=(I+t(x)A)^(-1)x. For every actual step h>=0,

    x_plus = ((1-h)I+t(x)A)g(x),
    v(x_plus) <= max{v(x)-h, kappa(h-2)-v(x), 0}.         (2)

Formula (2) includes zero and arbitrarily large original steps.

For the diagnostic word fix an integer m>=128 and retain its original
enclosing parameters in EVERY child and at EVERY physical query:

    lambda = 1+sqrt(2),  beta = 1-1/m,
    c = lambda(1+1/m),  a = beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n=2^m-1,  S_j=sum H_j,  S=S_m,  M=1+S.

For any input state, run the same static F_A through a complete H_j block.
Its actual multiplier satisfies

    v_exit <= (v_entry-S_j)_+,  0<=j<=m.                 (3)

Here (r)_+=max(r,0). In particular an exterior terminal point x_n outside
E_A forces v(x_0)>S. This is not a statement that a core terminal is zero.

Let x_0!=0, use every step of H_m, and set

    R_0=||x_0||=dist(x_0,argmin F_A),
    P_A=2(F_A(x_n)-F_A(0))/R_0^2.

The following rigorously scoped conclusions are the output of this pilot:

* If v(x_0)>=S, then

      P_A <= kappa/(kappa+2S) <= 1/M.                    (4)

  This covers EVERY exterior terminal trajectory, and also the core
  terminal branch under the stated initial condition.
* If d_min R_0^2>=M, then P_A<=1/M, whether the terminal is exterior or
  inside the core. The sufficient normal-state condition

      v(x_0)>=kappa(sqrt(M)-1)                           (5)

  implies this paid-radius condition.
* If v(x_0)<=1+a, the first original step puts the actual state into E_A
  and the entire remaining physical word stays in E_A. With

      q_m=beta^(2^m-1)c^(-m),

  the exact endpoint is x_n=(-1)^(n-1)(q_m/a)x_1 and

      P_A=(q_m/a)^2 ||x_1||^2/R_0^2
          <= (q_m/a)^2 <= c^(-2(m-1)).                  (6)

  If x_0 already belongs to E_A, the stronger exact value is P_A=q_m^2.

For the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark B_n from accepted
b98bb807d514e80d, each of the covered regimes obeys

    B_n P_A < 14848/15315 < 1,  m>=128,                 (7)

and uniformly over their horizon-dependent parameters,

    limsup_(m->infinity) sup B_n P_A <= lambda/exp(1)<1. (8)

The small shell in (6) has ratio tending to zero and is strictly below
each COMPLETE same-word K, alternating-ray, and full schedule-dependent
complex-polynomial reference specified in Section 8. The large-state
bound is below 2K and 2F_ray and below 8 times the full complex reference;
no dominance by one of those references is claimed in that regime.

The remaining core-ending initial region is explicit:

    v(x_0)>1+a,  d_min R_0^2<M,  v(x_n)=0.              (9)

Every instance not covered by the stated regimes lies here. No uniform
payoff bound on (9), no closure of all centered ellipsoids, and no
all-n/all-nonnegative-word conclusion about W_n are asserted.
Here W_n(h) is the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)
over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth objectives
with a minimizer x_star, x_0!=x_star, and the actual updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), as in accepted 1252b8823bb5a290.

## 2. One legal function, its actual query, and the true minimum

E_A is nonempty, compact and convex. Completing the square in its defining
maximum shows that its unique maximizing g is the Euclidean projection
of x onto E_A. Existence follows from compactness; uniqueness follows from
strict convexity of ||x-g||^2 on a convex set.

For x in E_A this gives g=x. For x outside, the left side of (1) is
continuous, strictly decreasing on [0,infinity), starts above 1, and
tends to zero. Thus its unique root t is positive, even if some x_l
vanish or some eigenvalues coincide. For g=(I+tA)^(-1)x one has
<g,A g>=1 and, for every z in E_A,

    <x-g,z-g> = t(<g,A z>-1) <= 0,

by Cauchy--Schwarz in the A inner product. Expanding squared distances
then shows ||x-z||^2>=||x-g||^2. This proves the query formula without a
black-box Lagrange multiplier assertion. Write x=(I+tA)g throughout,
including t=0 in the core.

For two projection pairs (x,g),(y,p), their two normal inequalities add
to ||g-p||^2<=<g-p,x-y>. In particular ||g-p||<=||x-y||. Comparing the two
defining maxima for F_A gives

    <g,z> <= F_A(x+z)-F_A(x) <= <g(x+z),z>.

The difference between the left expression and the middle expression is
between zero and ||z||^2. Thus F_A is differentiable, its gradient is g,
and that gradient is 1-Lipschitz. F_A is convex as a maximum of affine
functions of x and is finite by compactness.

Since 0 is feasible, F_A>=0, and F_A(0)=0. If x!=0, choose epsilon>0
small enough that epsilon*x belongs to E_A and epsilon<2. Its test value
epsilon(1-epsilon/2)||x||^2 is positive. Hence argmin F_A={0}, proving the
true minimum and the paid initial radius exactly. Multiplication of F_A
by any L>0 and use of steps h_i/L preserves every point and P_A. This is
the project's W=2R normalization.

The actual value is

    F_A(x)=<x,g>-||g||^2/2
          =||g||^2/2+t<g,A g>
          =||g||^2/2+t.                                 (10)

The last equality holds also in the core because t=0. In particular
F_A(x)=||x||^2/2 there. Actual GD gives the first formula of (2), and the
next t is evaluated at that actual x_plus by (1). No state is reset.

For any two actual samples, including the optimum sample with x=g=t=0,
the complete smooth-convex residual in the convention of accepted
1252b8823bb5a290 is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j>
        =t_j(<g_j,A g_i>-<g_j,A g_j>) <= 0.              (11)

If t_j=0 the last expression is zero. If t_j>0, its final inner product
is at most 1 and <g_j,A g_j>=1. This proves every ordered remote,
adjacent, and optimum inequality at once. In particular Q_(i,*)=0 and
Q_(*,i)=-t_i. No finite list of selected interpolation rows is substituted.

When both gradients lie on the ellipsoid boundary, (11) becomes

    Q_ij=-(t_j/2)||g_i-g_j||_A^2.                        (12)

Thus for t_i,t_j>0 the ratio Q_ij/t_j equals Q_ji/t_i. A one-way zero
residual with the reverse strictly negative is impossible among two
strictly exterior queries. For three such queries the products
Q_ij Q_jk Q_ki and Q_ji Q_kj Q_ik coincide. These are scoped structural
restrictions of this centered ellipsoid, not claims about general
smooth-convex interpolation or about floating-point H6 residuals.

## 3. Genuine coupling and a fully exterior nonlinear example

In principal coordinates put

    D_t=sum_l d_l^2 x_l^2/(1+t d_l)^3 > 0

at an exterior point. Implicit differentiation of (1) gives

    partial t/partial x_l = [d_l x_l/(1+t d_l)^2]/D_t.

For distinct k,l,

    partial g_k/partial x_l
      =-[d_k x_k/(1+t d_k)^2]
        [d_l x_l/(1+t d_l)^2]/D_t.                      (13)

This is nonzero when both coordinates are nonzero. In these coordinates
the query is not a direct sum of independent scalar quadratic queries.
Each denominator shares the changing t. No reduction of the entire
family to a fixed quadratic or one common polynomial orbit is made.
Indeed the Hessian is I in the interior core, whereas differentiating
<g(x),A g(x)>=1 at an exterior point gives the nonzero left null vector
(A g(x))^T for the gradient Jacobian. That exterior Jacobian exists by
(1) and is singular. Thus this function cannot be a fixed quadratic in
any orthonormal coordinate system.

The exterior regime is nonempty and supports changed gradient directions
at every physical query. For ANY nonnegative word of sum S, choose

    A=diag(1,2),  g_0=(1/sqrt(2),1/2),
    T=4S+1,       x_0=(I+T A)g_0.

Then <g_0,A g_0>=1 and t(x_0)=T. Because every projected coordinate has
|g_1|<=1 and |g_2|<=1/sqrt(2), the actual recursion gives at every index

    (x_i)_1 >= (1+T)/sqrt(2)-S
              =(2sqrt(2)-1)S+sqrt(2)>1,
    (x_i)_2 >= T+1/2-S/sqrt(2)>0.

Every query is exterior with two positive gradient coordinates. If h_i>0
and g_(i+1) were parallel to g_i, boundary normalization and positivity
would force equality. The actual update would then require both
t_(i+1)=t_i-h_i and t_(i+1)=t_i-h_i/2, a contradiction. Thus, on H_m,
each successive gradient direction changes, while the whole trajectory
still satisfies the obstruction below. Continuous coupled boundary
motion alone does not avoid its paid-radius cost.

## 4. One-step multiplier drainage, including zero and huge steps

Take any x=(I+tA)g, t>=0, g in E_A. For h>=0 define

    u=max{t-h/d_max, (h-2)/d_min-t, 0}.

For every eigenvalue d in [d_min,d_max],

    1+t d-h <= 1+u d.

For the other sign, if h>=2 the definition of u implies
h-1-t d<=1+u d. If 0<=h<=2, the same inequality holds because
h-2<=0<=(t+u)d. Consequently

    |1+t d-h| <= 1+u d.

For y=x-hg, the vector (I+uA)^(-1)y therefore has A norm at most the A
norm of g, hence at most 1. The root characterization in Section 2
implies t(y)<=u. Multiplying by d_max proves (2). When h=0 the bound is
v(y)<=v(x), and the actual map is exactly the identity. No upper bound
on h was imposed.

The right side of (2) is not monotone in v. The block proof next does
NOT iterate it by replacing v with an upper bound inside the negative-v
term; that replacement would have the wrong inequality direction.

## 5. Every inherited H_j block drains its full original mass

The exact mass formulas, obtained from S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a c^j, are

    alpha=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)],
    S_j+1=alpha c^j+(1-alpha)2^j.                        (14)

For m>=128, c>lambda>2, c<5/2, and 0<alpha<1. The last statement follows
from c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta. The upper bound c<5/2 follows, for
example, from sqrt(2)<17/12 and (29/12)(129/128)<5/2.

Put b_j=1+a c^j. If b_j<=2 then kappa(b_j-2)_+=0<=S_j. If b_j>2,

    S_j-kappa(b_j-2)
      =(1-alpha)2^j+[alpha-kappa a]c^j+kappa-1 >= 0,     (15)

because alpha-kappa a=alpha[1-kappa(c-2)]>=0 for kappa<=2, c<5/2.

We now prove (3) by induction for arbitrary actual inputs to each block.
H_0 is empty, so the assertion is equality. Suppose it holds for H_j.
After the left copy, write its actual multiplier as w. It satisfies
w<=(v-S_j)_+. After the middle step b=b_j, (2) and w>=0 imply

    v_middle <= max{(v-S_j)_+-b, kappa(b-2), 0}.

This step bounds the reflected term by kappa(b-2), rather than wrongly
inserting the upper bound on w into its negative occurrence. Apply the
inductive assertion to the actual input of the right copy and subtract
S_j from this maximum. By (15), its second entry becomes nonpositive;
the third is also nonpositive. The first entry is at most
(v-2S_j-b)_+ after taking the positive part, including the case v<S_j.
This proves v_exit<=(v-S_(j+1))_+.

The induction keeps all original child parameters and every physical
query. It neither restarts t nor changes the function between copies.

## 6. Radius payment and the large-state payoff

Suppose v_0>=S. Then x_0 is exterior. Let z=d_min t_0=v_0/kappa and
p=S/kappa. Here p>=1 since S>=2^m-1 and kappa<=2. Write
w_l=d_l(g_0)_l^2, so w_l>=0 and sum_l w_l=1. Exactly,

    R_0^2=sum_l w_l[(1+t_0 d_l)^2/d_l].

For d>=d_min the bracket is 1/d+2t_0+t_0^2 d, whose derivative is
t_0^2-1/d^2>=0 because t_0 d_min=z>=p>=1. Consequently

    R_0^2 >= (1+z)^2/d_min.                             (16)

Every projected g has ||g||^2<=1/d_min. From (3),
t_n<=(v_0-S)/d_max. Thus (10) and (16), whether t_n is zero or positive,
give

    P_A <= [1+2(z-p)]/(1+z)^2 <= 1/(1+2p).             (17)

For the last inequality subtract the middle expression from the right
one. The numerator after multiplication by the positive denominators
is (z-2p)^2. This proves (4), without optimizing any computed orbit.

For any exterior terminal, (3) forces v_0>S, so (17) applies. For a
core terminal, P_A=||x_n||^2/R_0^2<=1/(d_min R_0^2). It follows that
d_min R_0^2>=M gives P_A<=1/M in both terminal branches. For (5),
v_0/kappa>=sqrt(M)-1>=1, so (16) again gives d_min R_0^2>=M.

## 7. The initial shell: exact core absorption and all tail products

All step excesses b_j-1=a c^j are positive. Let q_j be the product of
these excesses over the complete physical word H_j. From q_0=1 and
q_(j+1)=q_j^2 a c^j one obtains

    q_j=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).                             (18)

Every nonempty prefix product in H_j is at most a. For j=1 this is the
single product a. For the induction, a prefix in the left copy has the
claimed bound. At the middle bridge the product is
q_j a c^j=a beta^(2^j-1)<=a. A prefix continuing into the right copy
multiplies this by a prefix product of H_j, including the empty product
1; all those products are at most 1 because a<1. This proves the bound.
Therefore EVERY prefix product of the tail obtained by deleting only
the very first physical step is at most 1, by division by that first a.

If v_0<=1+a, then at the first step h_0=1+a,

    x_1=(t_0 A-aI)g_0.

Each t_0 d_l is in [0,1+a], so |t_0 d_l-a|<=1. Hence x_1 belongs to
E_A. The prefix-product bound now proves inductively that every actual
later point remains in E_A: the next prescribed point is its scalar
quadratic update and stays in that ellipsoid. The endpoint formula in
(6) follows from the tail product q_m/a.

Finally ||x_1||<=||x_0||. Indeed <x_0,g_0>>=||g_0||^2, so

    ||x_1||^2<=||x_0||^2-h_0(2-h_0)||g_0||^2<=||x_0||^2,

since 1<h_0<2. This proves (6). If x_0 is already in E_A, the same
prefix argument applies from the first query and gives P_A=q_m^2.

## 8. Comparisons with the COMPLETE same-word reference channels

The references are those of accepted b98bb807d514e80d,
1fca3e5dd555cb4a, and dfde2fa326c908bf, respectively. Their scopes are
not enlarged. For clarity their full definitions are recorded here.

For a word h of total mass S, put P_j=sum_(i<j)h_i and T_j=sum_(i>j)h_i.
The full single-cut functional is

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),
      max_(j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]}.

For F_ray_n(h), take ALL chronological checkpoint subsets of physical
indices, splitting h as (block_0,b_0,...,b_(k-1),block_k), with s_i the
sum of the actual non-checkpoint block_i. For k>=1 set

    m_0=1/(1+s_0), O_0=0,
    m_(i+1)=((b_i-1)m_i-O_i)/(1+s_(i+1)),
    O_(i+1)=O_i+s_i m_i, 0<=i<=k-2.

Require m_i>0 and m_i>=m_(i-2) for i>=2. Define
z_term=(b_(k-1)-1)m_(k-1)-O_(k-1), and m_old=0 if k=1, otherwise
m_(k-2). Require z_term>=(1+s_k)m_old. Set
e_term=sum_(0<=i<k, i congruent k mod 2)s_i m_i^2. Optimize

    2e_term+2q z_term-(2s_k+1)q^2,
    m_old<=q<=z_term/(1+s_k).

For k=0 use z_term=1, e_term=m_old=0 and q in [0,1/(1+S)]. F_ray_n is
the maximum over ALL feasible checkpoint subsets. The accepted facts
give K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n, including all offset and area payments.

For the complex reference, let Poly_0(z)=1 and
Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z). Put xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z)
and xi_*=gamma_*=0. The inner product on C is Re(conj(u)v). For
z=alpha_c+i beta_c, put kappa_c=alpha_c-|z|^2 and give edge j->i weight

    q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+|gamma_i-gamma_j|^2/2.

For every directed simple cycle C on {*,0,...,n}, including 2-cycles,
let Area_C=sum_(j->i)Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i) and
Energy_C=sum_(j->i)|xi_i-xi_j|^2. The FULL schedule-dependent domain is

    D_h={z:kappa_c Energy_C>=2|beta_c Area_C| for EVERY C}.

For z in D_h let ell_i be the maximum q-weight of a path i->*, with the
empty path at *, and put Gap_h(z)=-2ell_n. Define

    F_cx,n(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z).

This is the full accepted channel, with initial point 1, minimizer 0,
and denominator 1; it is not the universal lens or a selected list of
complex parameters. All definitions retain every original query.

Only rigorous bounds on these COMPLETE references are needed here.
With M=S+1, their accepted statements imply

    K_n>=1/(2M-1),  F_ray_n>=K_n,
    F_cx,n>= M^2/(2M-1)^3 > 1/(8M).                    (19)

For the complex bound, choose the real theta=1/(2S+1) in [0,1]. It
belongs to D_h since beta_c=0 and kappa_c=theta(1-theta)>=0. By the
accepted lower bound, Gap_h(theta)>=theta^2 Poly_n(theta)^2, but we use
the stronger actual scalar-quadratic interpolant: f(x)=theta*x^2/2 has
these positions and gradients and hence maximality of Gap gives
Gap_h(theta)>=theta Poly_n(theta)^2. Each factor is nonnegative, and
product_(i<n)(1-theta h_i)>=1-theta S=M/(2M-1). This product inequality
follows by induction from (1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v for nonnegative u,v with
all partial sums at most 1. This proves (19).

For either large-state regime P_A<=1/M gives

    P_A < 2K_n <= 2F_ray_n,
    P_A < 8F_cx,n.                                     (20)

No contrary ordering or equality is inferred. For the small shell,
(14) gives M<=c^m, and m>=128 implies c^(m-2)>8. Thus

    P_A<=c^(-2(m-1))<1/(8c^m)<=1/(8M)<F_cx,n,

and also P_A<1/(2M-1)<=K_n<=F_ray_n. These comparisons are on the SAME
whole H_m word; no child is evaluated with a different enclosing m.

## 9. Exact all-split benchmark comparison, proved here

Use the benchmark definitions from accepted b98bb807d514e80d:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),
    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(C_k,B_l),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

Every split k,l is retained. We prove B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1), without
assuming a benchmark asymptotic formula or running its recurrence.

Put eta=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda) and
Llin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/eta. Its two coefficients exceed 1, so Llin>=x+y.
Using lambda^2=2lambda+1 gives the explicit square

    eta^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]
      =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda*y)^2 >=0.

Therefore Llin>=Omega(x,y). At z=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial
whose larger root is G(x,lambda y) has value

    z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2
       =x[4x+lambda^2 y-eta Omega(x,y)] >=0.

Also z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, the vertex location. Hence
G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y). Monotonicity of G in its second
argument and induction in the ORIGINAL recurrences give B_n<=lambda C_n.

Put rho=log_2(lambda)>1, Dpow(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, and Lpow(u)=log Dpow(exp u).
For t=exp u>0, direct differentiation gives

    Lpow''(u)=rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
      *[(1+t)^rho-1-rho*t]/Dpow(t)^2 >0.

The bracket vanishes at zero and has positive derivative thereafter.
Thus Dpow(t)Dpow(1/t)>=Dpow(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. For x,y>0 set
Z=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho and t=(y/x)^(1/rho). It follows that
(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy, or Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0. Also Z>=x+y; for this last
inequality, (u+v)^rho-u^rho has derivative in u at least zero and is
at least its value v^rho at u=0. Therefore Omega(x,y)<=Z. Zero arguments
follow directly. Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^rho. Consequently

    B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).                            (21)

For a finite explicit margin write delta=1/m<=1/128. Since

    c(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))delta+(3+2sqrt(2))delta^2
             <1+7delta+6delta^2,

one has alpha>(1-delta)/(1+7delta+6delta^2)>15/16. Indeed the last
inequality is equivalent to 1>121delta+90delta^2; at delta=1/128 the
numerator is 121*128+90=15578<16384, and the polynomial is increasing.
The first four binomial terms give

    (1+1/m)^m >= 8/3-1/m+1/(3m^2)>1021/384.

By (14), M>=alpha c^m, and hence for a large-state regime

    B_n P_A<=lambda/[alpha(1+1/m)^m]
              <(29/12)/(15315/6144)=14848/15315<1.      (22)

For the small shell, (6) and (21) give
B_n P_A<=lambda^(3-m), which is smaller than the constant in (22).
This proves (7).

Finally alpha->1, c->lambda, (1+1/m)^m->exp(1), and (14) implies
M/c^m->1. Equations (21) and P_A<=1/M yield (8); the small-shell bound
tends to zero. For a purely rational strict comparison,
lambda<5/2 and exp(1)>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3 give lambda/exp(1)<15/16<1.
No equality or limiting constant for the actual B_n has been assumed.

## 10. Counterexample checks and the precise unresolved interface

The scalar drainage result loses the terminal core radius. It cannot be
strengthened into a mass-normalized objective bound on arbitrary words
using only that state: on the single step h=(2), take A=diag(1,2) and
any nonzero x_0 in E_A. Then x_1=-x_0 is in E_A, v_0=v_1=0, and P_A=1,
although 1/(1+sum h)=1/3. This is an exact counterexample to that general
shortcut, not a counterexample to an H_m-specific estimate.

There is also no lossless replacement of exterior starts by the unit
quadratic on the actual H_m. In the same anisotropic A, take

    x_0=(0,(2+a)/sqrt(2)),
    g_0=(0,1/sqrt(2)),  t_0=(1+a)/2,  v_0=1+a.

The first step gives x_1=(0,1/sqrt(2)); Section 7 proves every remaining
query is in the core. Its exact payoff is

    P_A=q_m^2/[a^2(2+a)^2] > q_m^2,

because a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1 implies a(2+a)<1. This refutes equality
of the whole ellipsoid family with the unit-quadratic orbit, while
remaining within the proved small-shell obstruction. A fixed-m strict
inequality persists under sufficiently small perturbations giving both
initial coordinates nonzero, by continuity of projection and the finite
GD recursion. No quantitative perturbation is needed or used elsewhere.

Two direct approaches to region (9) were tested. First, multiplier
drainage determines only that the terminal is in E_A, giving
P_A<=1/(d_min R_0^2). Its denominator is too small in (9). Second, the
exact core prefix products control an orbit ONLY after it enters the
core at a time whose remaining prefix products all stay within the
available core radius. Entry at a general internal H_j endpoint does
not supply that radius: the following bridge can multiply it by
a c^j>1 and can send it outside again. The one-step bound deliberately
discards this core radius when it replaces a reflected term by
kappa(h-2). Iterating that upper bound cannot recover the missing size.

For an all-family exclusion, one still needs an H_m-specific bound on

    sup { ||x_n||^2/||x_0||^2 :
          1<=kappa<=2, 1+a<v(x_0), d_min||x_0||^2<M },   (23)

where x_n is generated by the actual common-multiplier map (1)-(2).
These inputs necessarily have v_n=0: if v_n>0, then v_0>S, and (16)
gives d_min||x_0||^2>=(1+S/kappa)^2>=M, a contradiction.
The same calculation also shows the unknown normal state is below
kappa(sqrt(M)-1). The remaining issue is thus a concrete core-return
size estimate, not remote interpolation feasibility or an unknown SDP.

The full-family payoff question is BLOCKED at (23) in this allocation.
No argument here excludes high eccentricity, translated ellipsoids,
other curved bodies, or unrestricted adversaries. In particular accepted
e9a55f57461f253c is a fixed original H6 witness in dimension 66 and is
not an all-m assumption. Neither its data nor any stored float is used.

## 11. Literature and provenance

The required Matlas query for the ellipsoid projection mechanism returned
three statements about other geometric problems; none was used. A
background search led to David Eberly, "Distance from a Point to an
Ellipse, an Ellipsoid, or a Hyperellipsoid", Geometric Tools, created
2013, revised 2020, especially Section 4.1 and equation (42):
https://www.geometrictools.com/Documentation/DistancePointEllipseEllipsoid.pdf

The exact PDF was downloaded to this worker's downloads directory.
Local extraction failed with `pdftotext: command not found`; the web
tool's PDF text extraction supplied the relevant pages. Eberly's object
is the BOUNDARY surface, including interior-to-surface cases with a
negative multiplier. Here the convex body is FILLED, so an interior
query has projection x and multiplier zero. The exterior normal formula
is the useful connection. Section 2 proves our complete filled-body
query characterization independently; no external theorem is a premise.

The exact benchmark and reference definitions are accepted facts cited
above. All further estimates, including the scalar benchmark envelope,
are reproduced in this joint draft. The earlier parabola, power,
polygonal-face, and common-metric findings are awareness only. No
verification service was invoked, and this document is not a fact.

# Ancestor clamping bridge: explicit coefficients and a complete joint proof draft

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Project gd-exact-profile-20260926, worker max4,
invocation80, guidance ecacb7cb9b03f152, plan750b1079379f0b82.
No verifier submission was authorized or made. There is no new fact_id.

The first mechanism closes the requested ancestor-pair bridge at the
level of the self-contained proof draft below. In particular, it pays
the nonnegative-multiplier clamping cost; it does not replace that cost
by positivity of an unconstrained determinant. The coefficient rule is
explicit and has three cases. All depths, actual exterior masses, and
both terminal charges are retained.

The second mechanism, a paid qr-cycle calculation, was started while
the first mechanism remained unresolved. Its complete paid formulas
are recorded in Section12. The first mechanism was subsequently closed
by the boundary comparison in Sections7--9. No third mechanism or
four-sector exploration was then undertaken: TASK.md expressly forbids
a third mechanism after a qr-cycle attempt. A lift to arbitrary queried
gradients remains open. Nothing here establishes the full-class bound
required by accepted931b9653ce0f7201 or resolves the minimax target.

All mathematical work was textual. Mathematical/numerical programs,
trajectory or recurrence replays, solvers, formal calls, fact_submit,
subagents, installations, consultations, and automatic renewal: ZERO.

## 1. Exact scope, normalization, and physical masses

Fix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. Throughout this entire report set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon_h=1/(4m), beta_h=1-epsilon_h,
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon_h), a=beta_h/c,
    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l),
    S_l=sum H_l, X_l=1+S_l, n=2^m-1,
    Z=D_m=1+2S_m, d0=1+a.

Every child retains these same enclosing-m parameters. H_l is a
physical word. There is no reset of m, a, c, or the benchmark.
For each actual step index i, its dyadic level is l_i=nu_2(i+1), and
its complete rooted subtree occupies

    I_i=[i-(2^(l_i)-1), i+(2^(l_i)-1)].

These identities follow by induction on the literal concatenation:
the new middle has level l, and translation of a child by 2^l
preserves all its lower valuations. Its step is 1+a*c^(l_i).

Choose any actual indices 0<=j<k<=n-2 such that j is an ancestor
of k. Thus k lies in the right child of I_j. Set l=l_j>=1,
X=X_l, d=a*c^l, and e=a*c^(l_k). Write the WHOLE original word as

    (block0,1+d,block1,1+e,block2),
    P=sum block0, M=sum block1, T=sum block2,
    u=P+1, v=M+1, A=u+d, B=v+e, U=u+v+d.

Let z_loc be one plus the mass after k within j's right child and
O the entire mass after I_j. The literal physical relations are

    u>=X, v+e+z_loc=X, O>=0, T+1=O+z_loc.             (1.1)

The entire left child of k lies in the middle block, and its entire
right child lies in z_loc. Consequently, with tau=5/12,

    0<d<tau*X, 0<e<tau*min(v,z_loc).                 (1.2)

Proofs of the uniform constants used here are in Section4.

Normalize a static convex differentiable objective to smoothness1,
minimum0, and an arbitrary one of its OWN minimizers x_star=0.
Consider its actual GD transcript whose queried gradients satisfy

    g_i=p, 0<=i<=j;
    g_i=q, j+1<=i<=k;
    g_i=r, k+1<=i<n.                                (1.3)

The real vectors p,q,r are arbitrary, may coincide, and may live in
any finite dimension. Family E requires g_n=r. Family Q permits
every legal terminal gradient and every static completion. The
normalized payoff is V=2f_n/||x_0||^2. For general smoothness L>0,
translation and division of the objective by L give precisely
2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), so W=2R is unchanged.

The draft conclusion is, for EVERY such ancestor pair,

    sup_E V=1/Z,
    1/Z <= sup_Q V <=1/(Z-d0^2).                    (1.4)

No restriction (1.3) is shown lossless for the original W. The
outside-subtree pairs were the subject of the previous separate
JOINT UNVERIFIED report a79d801952174a0a. That draft is not a
correctness premise here: all algebra needed for (1.4) is derived below.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used only for the necessary original
smooth-convex interpolation rows and its normalization scope.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 is used only for the stated hierarchy and
the unconditional benchmark comparison in Section11. The scope of
acceptedb98bb807d514e80d was checked; no claim identifying W with its
single-cut channel is used. No external paper theorem is a premise.

## 2. Literal original rows and all terminal charges

Let F_p,F_q,F_r be the values at the first point of each sector.
Equal gradients and the two convexity inequalities force values to
decrease by the cumulative step mass times the squared sector
gradient. Put

    Z_p=x_0, Z_q=x_0-Ap, Z_r=x_0-Ap-Bq.

An actual sample at sector mass s has position Z_p-sp and value
F_p-s||p||^2, or the corresponding q or r expression. In particular
the actual named indices are

    L_p=j, L_q=k, R_0=k+1, N_-=n-1.

Use the ORIGINAL ordered rows

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.    (2.1)

This is exactly the orientation in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Direct
substitution, without dropping any physical mass, gives

    Q_(L_p,star)=F_p-<p,x_0>+||p||^2/2,
    Q_(L_q,star)=F_q-<q,x_0>+A<p,q>+||q||^2/2,
    Q_(R_0,star)=F_r-<r,x_0>+A<p,r>+B<q,r>+||r||^2/2,
    Q_(L_q,L_p)=F_q-F_p+(P+1/2)||p||^2+||q||^2/2+d<p,q>,
    Q_(R_0,L_q)=F_r-F_q+(M+1/2)||q||^2+||r||^2/2+e<q,r>,
    Q_(R_0,L_p)=F_r-F_p+(P+1/2)||p||^2+||r||^2/2
                                      +d<p,r>+B<q,r>.

The PAID pq cycle is the sum of both original rows:

    C_pq=Q_(L_p,L_q)+Q_(L_q,L_p)
         =-d||p||^2+(d-v)<p,q>+v||q||^2.             (2.2)

The negative p-square in this cycle is retained throughout.

For E put t_pay=T, epsilon=1/2, and add the actual row Q_(n,R_0)
with multiplier1. Its expression is f_n-F_r+T||r||^2.
For Q put t_pay=T-d0^2/2, epsilon=(1+d0^2)/2, and add
Q_(N_-,R_0) and Q_(n,N_-) with multiplier1 each, omitting the first
if N_-=R_0. Since the final actual step is d0 and g_(N_-)=r,
their sum is exactly

    f_n-F_r+(T-d0^2/2)||r||^2
                   +||g_n+(d0-1)r||^2/2.           (2.3)

Indeed Q_(n,N_-)=f_n-f_(N_-)+d0<g_n,r>+||g_n-r||^2/2,
and f_(N_-)=F_r-(T-d0)||r||^2. This proves (2.3) even when
the queried r sector is a singleton. Define for either family

    w=t_pay+1/2=T+1-epsilon=O+z_loc-epsilon,
    D=U+e+w=u+d+X+O-epsilon=X_m-epsilon.             (2.4)

Thus 2D=Z for E and 2D=Z-d0^2 for Q. These are the actual
terminal charges, not altered suffix masses.

## 3. The xi=0 original multipliers and entire quadratic identity

For now the qr-cycle multiplier xi is zero. The core multipliers mu
are literally as follows; all unlisted core ordered rows have weight0:

| Original row | mu |
|---|---:|
| Q_(L_p,star) | alpha |
| Q_(L_q,star) | beta |
| Q_(R_0,star) | gamma |
| Q_(L_q,L_p) | ell+eta |
| Q_(L_p,L_q) | eta |
| Q_(R_0,L_q) | beta+ell |
| Q_(R_0,L_p) | R=alpha-ell |

Require alpha+beta+gamma=1, alpha,beta,gamma,eta>=0, and
0<=R<=alpha. The two finite intermediate nodes balance: the incoming
flow to L_p is ell+eta+R=alpha+eta, and the incoming flow to L_q
is beta+ell+eta. The core emits1 at R_0 and the optimum absorbs1.
The terminal rows in Section2 transfer that emission to the original
index n. For the final normalized identity use lambda_ij=2mu_ij,
including the terminal rows; their contributions add if labels coincide.

Introduce abbreviations

    Lc=d+v, Ec=2D-u, Kc=2D-U, C=U+e,
    hc=e+2w, theta=alpha+beta, sigma=gamma=1-theta,
    gbar=alpha*p+beta*q+gamma*r.

Expanding the weighted rows plus t_pay||r||^2 gives the quadratic

    Q=(alpha*u-eta*d)||p||^2+(beta+ell+eta)*v||q||^2+w||r||^2
      +[ell*d+beta*A+eta*(d-v)]<p,q>
      +[R*d+gamma*A]<p,r>
      +[(beta+ell)*e+(1-beta-ell)*B]<q,r>.            (3.1)

The weighted value/initial part is F_r-<gbar,x_0>. At p=q=r,
Q=D||r||^2. The p row sum and the cumulative p,q row sum show that
Q-D||gbar||^2 has zero common-gradient cross terms precisely when

    alpha*Ec=A-eta*Lc,
    theta*Kc=C-R*Lc.                                (3.2)

These equations will be imposed explicitly, not assumed from a
numerical optimizer. Write s=p-q and tvec=q-r. Under (3.2) the
entire remaining quadratic is J11||s||^2+2J12<s,tvec>+J22||tvec||^2,
where direct collection of the p^2 and cumulative p,q coefficients gives

    J11=alpha*u-eta*d-D*alpha^2,
    J22=theta*U-R*Lc-D*theta^2,
    J12=[sigma*(2D*alpha-A)-R*d]/2.                  (3.3)

For example the mixed coefficient before subtracting the common
square is 2(alpha*u-eta*d)+ell*d+beta*A+eta*(d-v).
Inserting ell=alpha-R, beta=theta-alpha, and
eta*Lc=A-alpha*Ec reduces it to the last expression in (3.3).
Thus no transverse cross term has been left unchecked.

The COMPLETE identity, in all real vectors x_0,p,q,r and the free
g_n for Q, is

    ||x_0||^2/(2D)-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij
     =2D||gbar-x_0/(2D)||^2
        +2[J11||s||^2+2J12<s,tvec>+J22||tvec||^2]
        +terminal_square,                          (3.4)

where terminal_square is0 for E and ||g_n+(d0-1)r||^2 for Q.
This follows by completing the square in x_0 after (2.3),(3.1).
All original intermediate sample values cancel through the stated
sector equalities and the literal flow. PSD here is on the ENTIRE
equal-sector formal space, not just selected Gram directions or
diagonal matrix entries. It is not claimed on the space in which
gradients inside a sector are independent.

Nonnegative row weights, D>0, and J>=0 therefore imply
2f_n<=||x_0||^2/(2D), because every original Q_ij is nonpositive.
Unused rows still remain necessary constraints of a legal transcript.

## 4. Uniform physical inequalities, including shallow subtrees

Literal summation yields

    X_(l+1)=2X_l+a*c^l, X_0=1,
    X_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a/(c-2).           (4.1)

The closed expression has the stated initial value and satisfies the
identity because v_h(c-2)=a; no recurrence was run. The elementary
bounds 7/5<sqrt(2)<99/70, obtained by squaring, imply

    12/5<c<29/12,
    2/5<a<c-2<5/12,
    0<v_h<1, 2^l<=X_l<=c^l,
    2/5<a<=a*c^l/X_l<c-2<5/12.                     (4.2)

For c<29/12 use c<(169/70)(6145/6144) and cross-multiply;
the remaining strict comparison is 2028<12288. For a>2/5 use
beta_h>99/100 and c<29/12. The inequality a<c-2 follows from
c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta_h. Division of (4.1) by c^l
gives the last ratio bound. These prove (1.2) for both selected
checkpoints using their actual children.

Since k<=n-2, the actual suffix includes d0. Therefore

    0<epsilon<13/8,
    w>=1-a^2/2>9/10,
    e/w<(5/12)(1+epsilon/w)<505/432<6/5.             (4.3)

For the last inequality use the right child of k: if Y=X_(l_k),
then e<(5/12)Y and T+1>=Y. Hence Y<=w+epsilon, and
epsilon/w<65/36. The first lower bound for w is the worse, free
terminal charge; the E charge is larger.

Two single-checkpoint expressions will be needed. The first is

    U*w>e*(e+w).                                   (4.4)

If l_k>=2, Y>=4, U>=Y, and w>19Y/32. Thus
(U-e)w>(7/12)(19/32)Y^2>25Y^2/144>e^2.
If l_k=1, Y=2+a>12/5 and e=ac=beta_h<1; consequently
(U-e)w>(7/5)(9/10)>1>e^2. If l_k=0, U>=1 and e=a<5/12,
so (U-e)w>(7/12)(9/10)>25/144>e^2. This proves (4.4).

The second is specific to the first, ancestor checkpoint:

    Fp=D*(u-d)-u^2>0.                              (4.5)

Indeed by (1.1),(2.4),

    Fp=(u-d)*(X+O-epsilon)-d^2
       >=(X-d)*(X-epsilon)-d^2.

Here X-epsilon is positive. If l>=2, X>=4 and the last expression
is greater than
[(7/12)(19/32)-25/144]X^2=(199/1152)X^2>0.
If l=1, d=ac<1, X=2+a>12/5, and
X-epsilon>=1-a^2/2>9/10. The expression is then greater than
63/50-1>0. This includes shallow ancestors embedded in the huge
original word without discarding their exterior prefix or suffix.

In particular all denominators below are positive, alpha_max=A/Ec
lies strictly between0 and1, and

    j_cap=alpha_max*(u-D*alpha_max)=A*Fp/Ec^2>0.      (4.6)

## 5. Strict determinant resource at every ancestor pair

Define

    Delta=u^2*v^2*w-d^2*e^2*D.

We prove Delta>0 jointly here. Put tau=5/12. For l>=4, X>=16.
First suppose z_loc>=13/4. At fixed u,d,v,e,z_loc the ratio
D/w=1+(u+d+v+e)/w decreases as O increases. At O=0,
w=z_loc-epsilon>z_loc/2, and

    D/u^2 <=(2+tau)/X,
    e^2/(v^2*w)
      <2tau^2*min(v,z_loc)^2/(v^2*z_loc)
      <=2tau^2/max(v,z_loc)
      <=2tau^2*(2+tau)/X.

The middle estimate separates v<=z_loc and v>=z_loc; the last
uses X=v+e+z_loc<=(2+tau)max(v,z_loc). Since d<tau X,

    d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)
       <2tau^4(2+tau)^2<25/32<1.

This is monotonicity of this explicit scalar ratio only, not a
monotonicity assertion about the endpoint optimization problem.

Still with l>=4, suppose z_loc<13/4. Then e<65/48<3/2,
v>X-5, e^2<3, and e^2/w<2 by (4.3). Moreover
d+v+e<(1+tau)X<3X/2. Therefore

    d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)
      <tau^2*X^2/(u^2*v^2)*(3+2u+3X)
      <=tau^2*(3+5X)/v^2
      <(3+5X)/(4(X-5)^2)<1.

For the final inequality, writing X=16+x gives
4(X-5)^2-(3+5X)=4x^2+83x+401>0.

Finally l<=3 implies X<c^3<(5/2)^3<16 and d,e<7, while
the ACTUAL D=X_m-epsilon>2^20. If u>=D/4, then v>=1,w>9/10 give

    d^2*e^2*D/(u^2*v^2*w)<384160/(9D)<1.

If u<D/4, the actual outer mass is
O=D-u-d-X+epsilon>3D/4-23>D/2, and w>O-13/8>D/3.
Using d/u<tau and e/v<tau gives the ratio <3tau^4<3/16<1.
All cases are exhausted. Hence

                         Delta>0.                  (5.1)

## 6. Exact ellipse, without deleting the multiplier constraints

Set

    sigma0=hc/Kc, alpha_max=A/Ec,
    a0=(u*v+2D*d)/(2D*Lc), b0=d*hc/(2w*Lc),
    alpha_opt(sigma)=a0-b0*sigma,
    R(sigma)=(Kc*sigma-hc)/Lc,
    eta=(A-Ec*alpha)/Lc,
    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha-R(sigma).

These satisfy both contact equations (3.2) identically. All original
multiplier signs are equivalent to

    sigma>=sigma0,
    R(sigma)<=alpha<=min(alpha_max,1-sigma).         (6.1)

Here sigma>=0 and sigma<=1 follow from (6.1). In particular this
is exactly the multiplier polygon, not an unspecified feasible matrix.
Equation (4.4) implies

    0<sigma0<sqrt(w/D)<1,
    w-D*sigma0^2
      =(U+e)[U*w-e*(e+w)]/Kc^2>0.                  (6.2)

Let y=2D*alpha-A-d*Kc/Lc and

    M2=u^2*v^2+4D*d^2*(e+w).

Using A*Lc-d*U=u*v in (3.3) gives

    J11=M2/(4D*Lc^2)-y^2/(4D),
    J22=w-D*sigma^2,
    J12=(sigma*y+d*hc/Lc)/2.                       (6.3)

For example substituting eta in J11 yields
-D*alpha^2+(u*v+2D*d)*alpha/Lc-d*A/Lc;
completing this square proves its displayed constant. Completing
the determinant square, and using
4w(e+w)-hc^2=-e^2, gives exactly

    det J=(w-D*sigma^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2)
                      -D*w*(alpha-alpha_opt(sigma))^2.          (6.4)

In particular det J>=0 is an ellipse. Positivity of Delta alone
does not establish that it meets (6.1).

The center of its horizontal section at sigma0 is

    alpha_c=alpha_opt(sigma0)
      =[u*v/(2D)+d*(2w*U-e^2)/(2w*Kc)]/Lc.          (6.5)

It satisfies alpha_c>0 and alpha_c<1-sigma0. For positivity use
2wU>e^2 from (4.4). For the second inequality multiply by
2D*w*Kc*Lc: the difference has numerator

    v*w*(2D*U-u*Kc)+2D*w*Lc*e+D*d*e^2>0,

because 2DU-uKc=2D(U-u)+uU>0. Thus this point satisfies EVERY
multiplier half-plane except possibly alpha<=alpha_max.

If alpha_c>alpha_max, feasibility can be tested entirely on the
eta=0 edge alpha=alpha_max. Indeed (alpha_c,sigma0) lies strictly
inside the ellipse and satisfies all the other half-planes. For any
feasible point, the line segment to this center stays in the ellipse
and those other half-planes and meets alpha=alpha_max. This also
explains why an unconstrained-center argument alone was incomplete.
The explicit proof below supplies a point on that edge.

## 7. Entire eta=0 section and its true upper endpoint

Put alpha=alpha_max, eta=0, j_cap as in (4.6), and define

    Hc=d*Kc-alpha_max*u*Lc,
    sigma_star=d*hc*Hc/M2,
    sigma_A=(hc+Lc*alpha_max)/Kc.

On this edge, beta and ell can equivalently be parameterized by

    beta=(B+ell*Lc)/Kc,
    sigma=1-alpha_max-beta.

Hence sigma ranges over exactly [sigma0,sigma_A] as ell decreases
from alpha_max to0. Notice the STRICT identity

    1-alpha_max-sigma_A=B/Kc>0.                    (7.1)

Thus beta>=0 imposes no extra restriction on this edge. The full
determinant reduces to the literal concave quadratic

    det J=j_cap*(w-D*sigma^2)
                       -(d*hc-Hc*sigma)^2/(4Lc^2)
         =j_cap*Delta/M2
                       -M2*(sigma-sigma_star)^2/(4Lc^2).        (7.2)

To check the constant and curvature, use
M2=Hc^2+4D*Lc^2*j_cap and w*M2-D*d^2*hc^2=Delta.
In particular its unconstrained maximum is strictly positive.

The following sign calculation removes the upper clamping penalty
whenever alpha_c>alpha_max. In that case Hc>0, since
Hc=2D*Lc*(a0-alpha_max) and a0>alpha_c. We claim

                  v*Ec*Kc-2D*B*Lc>0.              (7.3)

Write Y=e+w>0 and q_ratio=e/v. We have d<5u/12, v<u,
Lc<17u/12, q_ratio<5/12, and by (1.1),(1.2),

    e< (5/17)*(u-v).

Expanding after division of (7.3) by v gives

    Ec*Kc-2D*Lc*(1+q_ratio)
      =u^2+(1-2q_ratio)*u*Lc-2q_ratio*Lc^2
        +4uY+(4-2q_ratio)*Lc*Y+4Y^2.               (7.4)

If Lc<=6u/5, replace q_ratio by5/12 to lower-bound the first
three terms by (u+Lc)*(u-5Lc/6)>=0; the Y terms are positive.
If Lc>6u/5, then v>47u/60 and
q_ratio<(5/17)*(13/47)=65/799<1/12. Replacing q_ratio by1/12
instead bounds those terms below by (u+Lc)*(u-Lc/6)>0 because
Lc<17u/12<6u. Again the Y terms are positive. This proves (7.3)
at every depth with the ACTUAL u and w; no parameter was reset.

For completeness, derive the needed derivative using increments
p-r and q-r, an invertible change from (p-q,q-r) with determinant1.
With alpha=alpha_max and eta=0 the entries in these coordinates are

    j_cap,
    (beta+ell)*v-D*beta^2,
    (ell*d-beta*alpha_max*u)/2.

At ell=0, beta=B/Kc. The derivative of their determinant with
respect to ell is exactly

    [j_cap*(v*Ec*Kc-2D*B*Lc)
                 +B*alpha_max*u*Hc/2]/Kc^2>0.       (7.5)

Since d sigma/d ell=-Lc/Kc<0, (7.5) says that the derivative
in sigma at sigma_A is negative. The strictly concave quadratic
(7.2) therefore has

                 0<sigma_star<sigma_A.             (7.6)

Moreover sigma_star<sqrt(w/D): (7.2) has positive value there,
and j_cap>0 in the first expression of (7.2) forces
w-D*sigma_star^2>0. This proves that only the lower clamp at
sigma0 can still matter.

## 8. The lower clamping cost is paid explicitly

The diagonal-cycle case u*v>=d*U will be handled separately in
Section9. Assume here that

    u*v<d*U, alpha_c>alpha_max.                     (8.1)

If sigma_star>=sigma0, the positive maximum in (7.2) is admissible
by (7.6). Suppose instead that sigma_star<sigma0. We show that
the lower-clamped choice sigma=sigma0 still has positive determinant.

The left root of det J=0 on the horizontal line sigma=sigma0 is

    alpha_left=alpha_c
             -sqrt((w-D*sigma0^2)*Delta)/(2D*w*Lc).

Define the explicit tangency threshold

    alpha_tan=a0-M2/(2D*Lc*d*Kc).

Because Hc=2D*Lc*(a0-alpha_max), the inequality
sigma_star<sigma0 is equivalent to alpha_max>alpha_tan.
On the other hand, direct subtraction gives

    alpha_left-alpha_tan
      =sqrt(Delta)/(2D*w*Lc*Kc)
          *[sqrt(Delta)/d-sqrt(w*Kc^2-D*hc^2)].       (8.2)

All square roots are real and strictly positive by (5.1),(6.2).
The exact identities

    w*Kc^2-D*hc^2=w*U^2-D*e^2,
    Delta/d^2=w*(u*v/d)^2-D*e^2

and u*v<d*U prove that the bracket in (8.2) is strictly negative.
Consequently

    alpha_left<alpha_tan<alpha_max<alpha_c.

The determinant on that horizontal line is the concave quadratic
in (6.4), positive strictly between its two roots. Its right root
is larger than alpha_c. Thus det J(alpha_max,sigma0)>0.

This is the previously missing clamping estimate. It does not infer
the clamped value from Delta>0: (8.2) compares the actual boundary
penalty against the ellipse radius using the physical case distinction.

## 9. Explicit uniform coefficient rule and full PSD

Here is a literal choice for every ancestor pair and each charge.
All quantities are closed expressions in the original physical masses.

CASE I: u*v>=d*U.

    alpha=A/(2D), eta=alpha*u/Lc,
    theta=C/Kc, sigma=1-theta=sigma0,
    beta=theta-alpha, gamma=sigma, R=0, ell=alpha.

Both contact equations hold, because eta*Lc=alpha*u and
2D*alpha=A. The signs are explicit: alpha,eta,ell>0,
0<theta<1, and theta>alpha because C>A and Kc<2D.
The entire increment matrix is diagonal:

    J12=0,
    J11=alpha*(u*v-d*U)/(2Lc)>=0,
    J22=(U+e)[U*w-e*(e+w)]/Kc^2>0.                 (9.1)

This includes equality u*v=d*U. The mixed coefficient is exactly0,
so (9.1) proves full PSD, including that boundary case.

CASE II: u*v<d*U and alpha_c<=alpha_max.

    sigma=sigma0, alpha=alpha_c,
    R=0, eta=(A-Ec*alpha)/Lc,
    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha.

Section6 proves alpha>0,beta>0,gamma>0; the assumed comparison
gives eta>=0. All row signs and both contact equations therefore
hold. Equations (5.1),(6.2),(6.4) give

    J22>0,
    det J=(w-D*sigma0^2)*Delta/(4D*w*Lc^2)>0.        (9.2)

CASE III: u*v<d*U and alpha_c>alpha_max.

    alpha=alpha_max, eta=0,
    sigma=max(sigma0,sigma_star),
    R=(Kc*sigma-hc)/Lc,
    beta=1-sigma-alpha, gamma=sigma, ell=alpha-R.

Sections7--8 prove sigma0<=sigma<sigma_A and
sigma<sqrt(w/D). Thus 0<=R<alpha, ell>0, beta>B/Kc>0,
and gamma>0. Every final multiplier in Section3 is nonnegative.
Both contact equations hold by their defining formulas. If
sigma=sigma_star, (7.2) gives det J=j_cap*Delta/M2>0.
If sigma=sigma0>sigma_star, Section8 gives det J>0.
Also J22=w-D*sigma^2>0 in either subcase.

For Cases II and III, the ENTIRE increment quadratic has the explicit
square representation

    J22*||tvec+(J12/J22)*s||^2+(det J/J22)*||s||^2.   (9.3)

Together with (9.1) in Case I and all squares in (3.4), this proves
full PSD on every real equal-sector vector assignment in every case.
There is no numerical pair list, unknown matrix, optimization oracle,
or assumption of favorable angles.

Finally these choices solve precisely the former scalar interface
(10.2)--(10.5) of a79d801952174a0a. They have

    sigma0<=sigma<sqrt(w/D),
    sigma<=1,
    sigma<=(hc+Lc*alpha_max)/Kc,
    sigma<=(hc+Lc)/(Kc+Lc),

because their alpha obeys (6.1). At this sigma our explicit alpha
has det J>=0. The nearest-point alpha_clamp of alpha_opt to the
same permitted alpha interval can only increase the determinant in
(6.4). Therefore the literal clamped inequality (10.5) holds too.
In Cases II and III the determinant is strictly positive; Case I
may be semidefinite exactly at u*v=d*U.

## 10. A failed tempting choice, on the SAME physical hierarchy

This diagnosis is included to distinguish the proof above from the
invalid shortcut of ignoring the alpha upper bound. For every
m>=1536 choose

    j=2^(m-2)-1, k=j+1,
    X=X_(m-2), u=X, v=1, d=a*c^(m-2), e=a,
    D=4X+(2+c)d-epsilon.

The actual suffix is T=3X+(1+c)d-2-a, so this is a genuine ancestor
pair, with its full exterior mass. It has X>=2048,
5X<D<6X, d>2X/5, d<X/2, U>X, Lc>d, w>3X.

The sign of alpha_c-alpha_max, after multiplication by the positive
denominator 2D*Kc*Ec*Lc, is the sign of

    N=2D*d*U*Lc-u^2*v*Kc-D*d*e^2*Ec/w.

For the displayed pair the positive term is greater than
(8/5)X^4. The next term is smaller than12X^3, and the last is
smaller than3X^2, using e<1/2, Ec<12X, and w>3X. Thus

    N>(8/5)X^4-12X^3-3X^2>0

for X>=2048; the last sign follows already from X>=16 by dividing
by X^2 and observing (8/5)X^2-12X-3 is increasing there and positive
at16. Hence the unconstrained center at sigma0 has eta<0 at every
one of these actual pairs. It is NOT a legal certificate. The rule
in Section9 repairs this failure by the explicit eta=0 boundary.
This is a counterexample to that coefficient choice only, not a
primal escape or a failure of the endpoint upper bound.

## 11. Objective consequence and exact scope boundary

Applying (3.4),(9.1)--(9.3) to any legal static transcript proves
the upper inequalities (1.4). This works after translation to ANY
minimizer of that very function, and therefore also its own closest
minimizer. There is no substituted radius or inherited minimum set.

The matching member for E is the single scalar Huber function

    kappa=1/Z,
    f_H(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=kappa,
    f_H(x)=kappa*|x|-kappa^2/2 otherwise, x_0=1.

Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and1-Lipschitz, and its
complete minimum set is {0}. At every actual cumulative mass s<=S_m,
x=1-kappa*s>kappa, so the gradient is kappa. Every original step
is executed, with p=q=r=g_n=kappa and own radius1. Direct substitution
gives 2f_H(x_n)=2/Z-Z/Z^2=1/Z. This also belongs to Q.

The original benchmark B_n is the all-integer-split benchmark in
accepted931b9653ce0f7201, with U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Its unconditional
comparison B_n/Z<47/50 applies to precisely this unchanged H_m.
Since d0^2<9/4 and Z>=2^(m+1)-1>1000*(9/4), (1.4) gives

    B_n sup_E V<47/50,
    B_n sup_Q V<940/999<19/20.

These are ancestor-pair, three-constant-sector statements only.
Even combining this joint draft with the previous outside-subtree
draft would still concern three constant queried-gradient sectors.
The function-class supremum W has independent possible gradients
inside each block. Neither this report nor the unverified sparse
simplex finding a753ad89e79ede74 supplies a lossless restriction to
three or four sector values. The full-class hypothesis
W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) in accepted931b9653ce0f7201 is still absent.
The original unrestricted asymptotic minimax problem remains OPEN.

## 12. Second mechanism: fully paid qr cycle, retained but unnecessary

This fallback was explored before Sections7--8 closed the first
mechanism. It is not needed as a premise for Sections1--11.
Let R_1=n for E and R_1=n-1 for Q, the last available r endpoint.
Its actual sector mass is t_r=T or T-d0. Put z_q=t_r+1.
Adding BOTH original rows Q_(L_q,R_1), Q_(R_1,L_q) with multiplier
xi>=0 adds exactly

    xi*C_qr=xi*[-e||q||^2+(e-z_q)<q,r>+z_q||r||^2]. (12.1)

The term -xi*e||q||^2 is a real cost. In particular

    z_q=w+1/2 for E,
    z_q=w+a^2/2 for Q.                             (12.2)

For Q one may NOT use the terminal equality g_n=r to replace this
endpoint. The same original terminal rows and square (2.3) remain.
Core value flow is unchanged by (12.1). The full quadratic (3.1)
acquires -xi*e on its q-square, xi*z_q on its r-square, and
xi*(e-z_q) on its qr coefficient. Its PAID contacts are

    alpha*Ec=A-eta*Lc,
    theta*Kc=C-R*Lc-xi*(e+z_q).                    (12.3)

Thus R=[Kc*sigma-hc-xi*(e+z_q)]/Lc. The complete increment entries
are still J11 in (3.3), and

    J22=w+xi*z_q-D*sigma^2,
    J12=[sigma*(2D*alpha-A)-R*d]/2.                 (12.4)

The full identity (3.4) holds with these entries and the additional
literal original row weights xi, all ultimately doubled to lambda.

At fixed alpha,sigma, the exact sign interval for xi is

    xi_low=max(0,(Kc*sigma-hc-Lc*alpha)/(e+z_q)),
    xi_high=(Kc*sigma-hc)/(e+z_q),                  (12.5)

provided sigma>=sigma0, 0<=alpha<=alpha_max, and alpha+sigma<=1.
These conditions are equivalent to all signs: (12.5) enforces
0<=R<=alpha, the first contact gives eta>=0, and beta,gamma>=0
are explicit. All other table entries are sums of these weights.

For further exact elimination set J12_0 to the xi=0 expression,
j11=J11, and b_xi=d*(e+z_q)/(2Lc)>0. Then

    det J=det J_at_0
             +xi*(z_q*j11-2b_xi*J12_0)-b_xi^2*xi^2.             (12.6)

For j11>0, the candidate maximizing this determinant on its entire
sign interval is the explicit clipping of

    xi_stat=(z_q*j11-2b_xi*J12_0)/(2b_xi^2)

to [xi_low,xi_high]. This follows by completing the square in the
strictly concave quadratic (12.6), not by invoking an optimizer.
Nonnegative determinant then implies the entire J is PSD because
j11>0; no diagonal-only check is used.

There is also a useful audit of the paid cost. Define

    w_xi=w+xi*z_q, h_xi=hc+xi*(e+z_q),
    Delta_xi=w_xi*M2-D*d^2*h_xi^2,
    alpha_opt_xi=a0-d*h_xi*sigma/(2w_xi*Lc).

The same square completion as Section6 gives

    det J=(w_xi-D*sigma^2)*Delta_xi/(4D*w_xi*Lc^2)
                      -D*w_xi*(alpha-alpha_opt_xi)^2,

    Delta_xi=Delta
       +xi*[z_q*u^2*v^2+2D*d^2*e*(z_q-e-2w)]
       -D*d^2*(e+z_q)^2*xi^2.                      (12.7)

The strictly negative xi^2 term shows why simply increasing the
backward cycle is not a monotone repair. At sigma=sigma0, (12.5)
even forces xi=0, and the first contact in (12.3) is independent
of xi. Thus adding this cycle while freezing the illegal center
from Section10 cannot repair its negative eta. No failure of the
full endpoint cone or legal primal counterexample follows from that
observation. The first mechanism already supplies a legal xi=0 choice.

## 13. Handoff, verification status, and work accounting

The concrete next correctness check is the joint draft in Sections1--11,
especially the upper-edge derivative sign (7.3)--(7.6) and the
lower-clamp comparison (8.2). Those two steps close the previous
inequality (10.5). All quantities, physical hypotheses, original
multipliers, and the full PSD square are printed in this report.

No verifier has accepted this proof. Shared publication is awareness
only. No new result may be treated as a fact-graph premise. There
was no fact_submit allowance, no new fact_id, and no finalization,
revocation, shutdown, outward publication, or Goal-completion action.
The invocation returns at cap1, without renewal or TASK modification.

The invoked skills were query-memory, direct-proving, and
construct-counterexamples. The one Matlas query returned two unrelated
statements (arXiv:2102.07922 Lemma6 and arXiv:2105.12062 Lemma1);
neither was used. A background web query located Taylor--Hendrickx--
Glineur, Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case
Performance of First-order Methods, arXiv:1502.05666,
https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666. The existing own extracted text was
read around its interpolation construction; it supplies no proof
premise beyond accepted1252b8823bb5a290 here. Broad initial recall
output was truncated, so the needed prior-report sections and accepted
scope passages were reread separately. No truncated passage was used
as a mathematical premise.

# A two-dimensional exact counterexample to the asymmetric eight-step seed

Status: complete UNVERIFIED counterexample, worker max, 2026-09-26.
No verifier or numerical solver call. This attacks the precise schedule
in finding 29a1f4a5269032b1, not a generic three-block rule and not the
unrestricted minimax target. Every needed calculation is included below.

## 1. Exact candidate and elementary parameter bounds

Define

    u=10/7, C=1+2u=27/7,
    D=(165+sqrt(65641))/42,
    s=D-55/7,
    T=(4+D+sqrt((4+D)^2+16D))/2,
    r=T-4-D.

The actual prescribed schedule is

    h=(3/2,3/2,1+r,3/2,3/2,1+s,10/7,10/7).           (1)

Its physical capacity is T=1+sum h_i=83/7+r+s. Directly from the
two quadratic equations,

    D s=196/9, rT=4D, Trs=784/9.                       (2)

For later exact estimates,

    10<D<21/2, 12/5<r<5/2, 0<s<98/45.                 (3)

Indeed P(D)=D^2-(55/7)D-196/9 is negative at10, with P(10)=-22/63,
and positive at21/2, with P(21/2)=215/36. Its positive root lies
between those values. The positive root r of
r^2+(4+D)r-4D=0 exceeds12/5 because the polynomial there is
384/25-(8/5)D<0. It is less than5/2 because its value there is
65/4-(3/2)D>1/2. The s bounds follow from (2) and D>10.

Put

    kappa=s/C=7s/27, so 0<kappa<686/1215<3/5,
    v=sqrt((5r-8)/800)>0,
    g_A=(1/4,0),
    g_B=(-1/10,v),
    g_C=-kappa g_B.                                    (4)

In particular

    ||g_B||^2=r/160,
    <g_A,g_B>=-1/40,
    <g_A,g_C>=kappa/40.                                (5)

## 2. One globally fixed function and the actual GD trajectory

On R^2 define, before running GD,

    J(z)=max(0,<g_A,z>,<g_B,z>,<g_C,z>),
    f(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                       (6)

This is one finite convex polyhedral function and its unit Moreau
envelope. The quadratic proximal objective is coercive and strictly
convex, so its minimizer is unique. Subgradient monotonicity gives
||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>;
the minimizing inequalities identify grad f=x-prox_J(x). Thus f is
globally convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth. It is nonnegative and
minimized at origin. The two gradient directions g_A and g_B are not
collinear because v>0.

Start at x_0=(1,0)=4g_A. The displayed queries and gradients are

    x_0=(1,0),                         grad f(x_0)=g_A,
    x_1=(5/8,0),                       grad f(x_1)=g_A,
    x_2=(1/4,0),                       grad f(x_2)=g_A,
    x_3=(-r/4,0),                      grad f(x_3)=g_B,
    x_4=x_3-(3/2)g_B,                  grad f(x_4)=g_B,
    x_5=x_3-3g_B,                      grad f(x_5)=g_B,
    x_6=x_3-(4+s)g_B,                  grad f(x_6)=g_C,
    x_7=x_6-u g_C,                     grad f(x_7)=g_C,
    x_8=x_6-2u g_C,                    grad f(x_8)=g_C.    (7)

Define the residual point

    Z=x_3-4g_B=(-r/4+2/5,-4v).

From (5), <g_B,Z>=r/40-4r/160=0, hence also <g_C,Z>=0.
Since Ckappa=s and C=1+2u,

    x_6=Z+Cg_C, x_8=Z+g_C.                             (8)

The arithmetic in (7) proves the GD updates for (1); the next paragraph
checks that all these gradients are the actual gradients of (6).

### Every old and future affine plane, on all three blocks

If g is an active plane normal in J at x-g, its subgradient inequality
proves that x-g uniquely minimizes the proximal objective. Therefore
it suffices to compare all four planes there.

On the first pair and its following checkpoint query, write the query
as (4-t)g_A, with ordinary cumulative t in [0,3]. Its proposed
proximal point is d g_A, d=3-t>=0. The current plane has value d/16.
The B plane has value -d/40<=0. The C plane has value d kappa/40,
whose ratio to the current value when d>0 is 2kappa/5<1. The zero
plane is also dominated, including the case d=0. This checks both
future planes at ALL first-block queries.

On the middle pair and its following checkpoint query, write the query
as x_3-tg_B, t in [0,3]. Its proposed proximal point is Z+d g_B,
d=3-t>=0. The current B value is d r/160. The future C value is
-kappa d r/160<=0. The old A value is

    <g_A,Z>+d<g_A,g_B>=-(5r-8)/80-d/40<0.

Thus every plane, including zero, is dominated there too.

On the last pair and at the final point, the proposed proximal point is
Z+d g_C with 0<=d<=C-1=20/7. The selected C value is
d kappa^2 r/160, the B value is its negative multiple -1/kappa,
and zero is dominated. The old A comparison is exactly

    2(5r-8)>=d kappa(4-r kappa).                        (9)

Its left side is greater than8 by (3). If 4-r kappa were negative
the right side would be nonpositive. Otherwise, using (3)--(4), it is
at most

    (20/7)*(3/5)*4=48/7<8.

Hence (9) holds strictly. This checks the potentially harmful positive
correlation with the remote first-block plane. It is the constraint
omitted by a purely scalar block gluing or a quadratic-product test.

All gradients in (7) are therefore actual gradients of the same fixed
function (6). The zero set is nonempty and the true minimum is zero.
Moreover origin is closest to x_0: any minimizing z satisfies
<g_A,z><=0, hence its first coordinate is nonpositive and
||(1,0)-z||^2>=1. The actual initial distance is exactly one.

## 3. Exact negative primitive energy

The proposed saturated primitive inequality, with physical capacity
T=1+sum_(i=0)^7 h_i and S=T-1, requires nonnegativity of

    V_h=2 sum_(i=0)^7 h_i(f_i-f_8)
       +sum_(i=0)^7 h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
       -S T ||g_8||^2,                                (10)

for every convex 1-smooth GD trajectory. This is the precise primitive
energy being tested; no nonsaturated objective certificate is rejected
by merely giving it this name.

Every sampled gradient here is one of the active homogeneous plane
normals, so its actual value satisfies

    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2, including i=8.             (11)

In particular every optimum interpolation residual is zero. Substituting
(11) into (10), and using x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, gives the complete telescope

    2sum h_i f_i+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      =sum[2h_i<g_i,x_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2]
      =||x_0||^2-||x_8||^2,

    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_8+Sg_8||^2.                      (12)

By (8), g_8=g_C, x_8=Z+g_C and <Z,g_C>=0. A direct calculation gives

    ||Z||^2=r(5r-8)/80,
    T^2||g_C||^2=T^2 r s^2/(160 C^2)
                 =941192/(295245 r),                  (13)

where (2) was used in the second equality. Hence the EXACT energy is

    V_h=1-r(5r-8)/80-941192/(295245 r).                  (14)

This is strictly negative with a rational margin. Since r<5/2,

    T^2||g_C||^2 >1882384/1476225>1,
    V_h < -406159/1476225 <0.                           (15)

The additional term ||Z||^2 is positive and strengthens the rejection.
No numerical residual, optimizer output, or limiting argument is needed.

## 4. Scope

Equations (6)--(15) give a legal two-dimensional counterexample to the
exact eight-step schedule (1) being primitive at its proposed physical
capacity T. It therefore cannot be used as a primitive seed for the
conditional amplification route in that form. Passing all original
single-cut tests and full-curvature equality does not save that seed.

This does not rule out a weaker objective certificate for the same
schedule, different bridge parameters, or any unrestricted minimax
statement. It is a new unverified shared counterexample; no correctness
claim is being promoted into the fact graph without a verifier allocation.

# Max47: recurrent signed reset directions, and the next original row

Status: complete joint analytical draft, UNVERIFIED. No fact_id; no formal
submission is authorized. This is the final allocated round after the
completed45 baseline. The original all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile
goal remains OPEN. Every new argument used below is reproduced here.
Earlier unverified closures are motivation, not correctness premises.

## 1. What changed, and what the evidence does and does not say

The second and final declared template is T2_recurrent_signed_carrier
(plan4af8cb8007ee35f8). It changes the dependence law: a persistent
carrier direction returns after an intervening excursion, while two
reused branch directions have negative inner product. The scale-indexed
supports can shrink and grow. The dimension grows with the number of
distinct scales, and a coordinate can change at arbitrarily many cells.
This is not a positive min kernel, a chronological fresh-innovation law,
a one-future tail, or a common-gradient augmentation.

The motivation is the ORIGINAL frozen H6 Gram, not a numerical fit or
an assertion about its exact rank. For example, its saved inner products
and squared norms include

    <g15,g23>=0.0006937998381333206,
    <g15,g31>=0.0005003025123186063,
    ||g15||^2=0.0016594229178722502,
    ||g31||^2=0.0009516573920766777;
    <g23,g31>=0.0008700101349075905>0,
    <g31,g39>=0.0005328988769998643>0,
    <g23,g39>=-0.0027718891731691783<0.

These suggest both recurrence of a previous direction and a triangle of
correlations whose signs cannot be generated by multiplying one positive
kernel by scalar signs. In T2, the three corresponding successive roles
are q_minus,p,q_plus: their adjacent inner products are positive and their
remote inner product is negative. Unlike the previous positive min kernel,
T2 retains this sign pattern and the carrier recurrence explicitly.
It does NOT reproduce every saved correlation. In particular no exact
pattern is asserted of the raw data or the accepted H6 witness.

Constant gradients inside H2 and the local maximal-energy policy remain
restrictions. The substantial internal H2 changes in the frozen outputs
are NOT represented by this template. The point of this second test is
to determine whether changed signed dependence alone repairs the previous
failed row and all the other rows. It repairs that row, but fails another.

Provenance: authorized broker job4 max47_internal_changes, completed exit0
at2026-09-30T20:43:55.274662+00:00, child wall time
0.11879143099940848 seconds. Script SHA256:
8f87943a26f836a3df07d6721db1f5ed183634f532f2f2256f24d1c0d6ba71e7.
