<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001195 -->
# Outward curvature kink: exact tail, comparison region, and capture gap

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED REPORT.** Project gd-exact-profile-20260926,
worker max3, invocation81 after ended80, completed ceiling81 / attempted82,
controller cap1. Assignment TASK.md and master_guidance72c9dd786e3c6ae3;
plan ee74db1ddd7f5bef. No fact_submit was made. Nothing new in this report
is an accepted fact. All arguments below belong to this single joint draft;
no earlier unverified lemma is imported as a premise.

The requested WHOLE-family all-start bound is **NOT established**.
There is no improving complete-word witness. Mechanism1 yields an exact
all-saturated subprofile, an all-start comparison theorem on a substantial
parameter region, a uniform core-start theorem, and a quantitative all-depth
capture statement for the entire odd family. The remaining normalization
gap is stated precisely below. Mechanism2 is not activated because the first
law has not received the decisive whole-family treatment required by TASK.

No mathematical program, numerical/arithmetic replay, executed recurrence,
solver, optimizer, benchmark DP, formal call, consult, or subagent was used.
All displayed inductions and inequalities are symbolic arguments.

## 1. Problem, normalization, and statements actually claimed

Fix any integer m>=1536, and keep the SAME parameters at every child:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, S=S_m,
    D=1+2S, n=2^m-1, ell=1+a, theta=a*beta.

The symbols h_i without a superscript mean the chronological physical
entries of H_m. The auxiliary d_m is not an additional physical step.
The actual last physical step is ell.

Fix arbitrary 0<kappa<=1 and finite B>=kappa. Define

    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,
    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.

Write Psi_j(s) for the actual final iterate of the complete H_j from s on
this ONE fixed function. Let

    M_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R) |Psi_j(s)|, R>=0.

The normalized payoff is 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 for s!=0. The denominator is
the true squared distance to the unique minimizer, not a radius of a child.

The following are the scoped conclusions of this joint candidate:

(A) Exact maximum over trajectories remaining on one saturated ray through
the terminal:

    C_tail=B^2+v*(2B-kappa),
    V_tail=B^2/(2*B^2*S+C_tail).                       (1.1)

It is attained by an explicit complete real itinerary in Section4.
For kappa<1 this is STRICTLY less than 1/D. At kappa=1 it equals 1/D.
For B=kappa the expression reduces to kappa/(1+2*kappa*S).

(B) Define

    kappa_0=(1-a)/(1+a), kappa_1=1/(1+2a).

If

    kappa>=kappa_0 AND ell*kappa+a*B>=1,              (1.2)

then EVERY nonzero start on the current fixed outward-kink function has

    2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2<=1/D.                           (1.3)

In particular this covers EVERY B>=kappa whenever kappa>=kappa_1,
and covers B>=1 whenever kappa>=kappa_0. These are whole all-start
regions, including genuine increasing-curvature middle intervals.

(C) For EVERY allowed kappa,B and EVERY 0<|s|<=1, every physical prefix
stays inside the core. Put

    P_j(kappa)=product_(h in H_j) |1-kappa*h|, P_0=1.

The exact payoff for these starts is kappa*P_m(kappa)^2, and

    kappa*P_m(kappa)^2<3/(4D).                        (1.4)

(D) For EVERY allowed kappa,B and EVERY j>=1,

    G(M_j(d_j*B))<=a*B.                              (1.5)

Moreover, any trajectory of a complete H_j containing a strict sign
crossing has terminal gradient magnitude at most a*B. All subsequent
returns and every actual ancestor suffix are included in this statement.
It controls an absolute gradient cap; it does not by itself prove (1.3).

(E) For EVERY allowed kappa,B, every full-word trajectory with at most
one strict crossing satisfies (1.3). So does every trajectory each of
whose updates is either a strict crossing or a same-side stay queried
on a saturated tail. In particular the latter includes every trajectory
whose queried AND terminal points all have radius at least1.

The remaining parameter/trajectory region can therefore be confined to
the complement of (1.2), |s|>1, with at least two strict crossings and
either a core query before the terminal or a final positive middle-to-core
entry. No all-start bound for that region is claimed. The original unrestricted minimax problem remains OPEN.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation and terminal
conventions; accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies B_n/D<47/50 and the
conditional799/800 consequence only AFTER a full-function-class bound
W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D). Neither supplies the missing bound here. Even complete
closure of this odd family would not establish that full-class premise.

## 2. Static function, actual integrals, and all ordered rows

The exact radial objective F(r)=f(r)=f(-r), r>=0, is

    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                        0<=r<=1,
    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                  1<=r<=u,
    2F(r)=2B*r-C_tail,                      r>=u.    (2.1)

At r=1, the first two values are kappa. At r=u, r-v=B and
2F(u)=B^2+kappa*v=2B*u-C_tail. Thus values and first derivatives
match at both knots. The middle interval disappears at B=kappa.
At kappa=1, v=0 and the function is the usual unit-curvature Huber
function, irrespective of whether B is greater than1.

The derivative g is continuous and nondecreasing, with slopes kappa,
1, and0 on the relevant intervals. Partitioning any interval at its
finitely many knots proves |g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Hence f is finite,
convex, differentiable, and globally1-smooth. When a piece disappears
the same assertions remain valid. Since G(r)>0 for every r>0,
the unique minimum is0 at x=0. The true initial distance is |s|.

For every physical index including the terminal, define

    x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i),
    g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i).

Also include x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For x<y set Delta=g(y)-g(x).
Monotonicity and the Lipschitz bound give 0<=Delta<=y-x and, on [x,y],

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).

Integrating gives both directed convexity remainders at least Delta^2/2.
Consequently all ordered rows, not just adjacent or outgoing terminal
rows, obey

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.      (2.2)

Equal positions give equality. These are actual samples of one fixed
function, so repeated visits cannot assign different derivatives or
values. The full Gram matrix is PSD because each of its quadratic forms
is a square of a real linear combination of actual scalar vectors.
No formal gradient or zero-step convention is deleted.

For arbitrary advertised L>0 use L*f with physical step sizes h_i/L.
The iterates remain x_i and the payoff remains 2*f(x_n)/s^2.
In particular, when the actual Lipschitz constant is smaller than1,
it is NOT used to rescale the physical word or the performance.

## 3. Same-m geometry and constants

The following elementary bounds hold throughout this report:

    12/5<c<17/7<5/2, beta>99/100,
    2/5<a<1/2, 2/5<theta<1/2,
    c*a=beta<1, c*theta=beta^2<1.                   (3.1)

For example sqrt(2)>7/5 gives the lower c bound.
Using epsilon<1/1000 and sqrt(2)<17/12 gives
c<(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7. The lower a bound follows from
a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5; the theta bound follows in the same way.

Set eta=a/(c-2). The strict inequality c(c-2)>1>beta gives 0<eta<1.
The exact mass identity and its solution are

    A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,
    d_j/A_j<c-2<3/7<1/2,
    A_j<c^j for j>=1.                              (3.2)

These follow by substitution into the literal word recursion.
Also

    S_j/d_j>7/5, j>=1.                             (3.3)

The base is S_1/d_1=ell/beta>7/5. If L_j=S_j/d_j>7/5, then
L_(j+1)=(2L_j+1+1/d_j)/c>(19/5)/(17/7)>7/5.
No recurrence was executed to obtain these assertions.

At any physical occurrence of a level-k bridge in H_j, let P,T be
the actual masses before and after that occurrence within H_j.
Its two full adjacent H_k children imply

    P>=S_k, T>=S_k, P+T=S_j-h_k^lev.                (3.4)

Its actual remaining suffix is

    H_k,h_(l_1)^lev,H_(l_1),...,h_(l_t)^lev,H_(l_t),
    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                            (3.5)

To obtain this list, ascend the binary tree after the immediate right H_k,
skip ancestors entered through a right child, and retain each remaining
bridge and full right child. This includes the real last leaf; after a
final leaf the list is empty.

## 4. Exact saturated itinerary and the changed tail intercept

Choose

    R_star=2B*S+C_tail/B,
    r_star=R_star-B*S=B*S+C_tail/B.                 (4.1)

The complete chronological trajectory is explicitly

    x_i=R_star-B*P_i,
    P_i=sum_(0<=t<i) h_t, 0<=i<=n.                 (4.2)

Indeed

    r_star-u=B*S+v*(1-kappa/B)>=0.

Thus every x_i>=r_star>=u, every gradient equals B, every physical
update in (4.2) is valid, and x_n=r_star after the actual final leaf.
All data inherit every ordered row from Section2.

For any positive saturated start whose terminal is still saturated,
write R>=B*S+u. Its payoff is

    [2B*R-2B^2*S-C_tail]/R^2.

Differentiation in R, or completing a square in 1/R, gives its unique
maximum at (4.1), with value (1.1). Reflection gives the negative ray.
Because C_tail>=B^2,

    V_tail<=1/(1+2S).

If kappa<1, v>0 and 2B-kappa>=kappa>0, so the inequality is strict.
This is not a full profile formula: a terminal in the middle/core or
a crossing history has not been optimized by this calculation.
In particular, substituting the ordinary Huber intercept B^2 into the
current tail would give an incorrect score.

## 5. Exact interval update and the outer crossing term

For a physical step h>=1, put d=h-1 and q_h=|1-h*kappa|.
The exact radius of the image of [-R,R] is

    E_h(R)=max{
       q_h*min(R,1),
       [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,
       [R-h*B]_+
    }.                                             (5.1)

Here [z]_+=max(z,0). In the core the map is (1-h*kappa)x.
In the middle its positive-ray expression is -d*r+h*v.
In the tail it is r-h*B. The extrema occur at0, a knot, or the input
endpoint; including a formal term from an unavailable piece does not
increase the maximum, since that term is then dominated by an available
endpoint. These observations establish (5.1) also at R=1,u.

The signed step is continuous and odd and fixes0, so its interval image
is the entire symmetric interval with this radius. Therefore successive
composition of E_h is exact, rather than a choice of incompatible starts.
In particular M_(j+1)=M_j composed with E_(h_j^lev) composed with M_j.

When both the source and the destination of a strict crossing are in the
middle, put z=G(r)=r-v. The destination gradient is

    z_new=(h-1)*z-2v.                               (5.2)

This displays the lost fixed-curvature assumption. The term -2v is
retained; a child cannot be treated as a homogeneous quadratic in z.

Equation (5.1) alone is NOT the claimed result. Sections6-10 give the
quantitative deductions obtained from it, and Section11 states what is
still missing.

## 6. Complete unit-Huber reference, rederived jointly

Let J_B have derivative sign(x)*min(|x|,B), and write U_j for its interval
radius map under the same H_j. Scaling x by B reduces this subsection to
B=1. Its exact single-step map is

    U_h(R)=max(d*min(R,1),R-h).

The maximum is nonnegative because its first term is. Direct induction
gives exact capture and the exterior branch:

    U_j(A_j)=1,
    U_j(R)=R-S_j for R>=A_j,
    U_j(R)<=max(1,R-S_j) for all R>=0.              (6.1)

For the induction, start at A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j.
The first child gives A_j+d_j+1. The bridge's staying endpoint is A_j,
and its crossing endpoint is at most d_j<A_j. The second child gives1.
An exterior start stays on the unit tail at every actual query.

For j>=1 and every integer r>=0,

    U_j(d_j*theta^r)<=theta^(r+1).                  (6.2)

The leaf base is a*beta*theta^r=theta^(r+1).
At the unscaled parent input c*d_j, (6.1) bounds the first child by
max(1,c*d_j-S_j). The bridge sends this to at most d_j, since

    c*d_j-S_j-h_j^lev<=d_j
    iff (c-2)*d_j<=A_j,

which follows from (3.2). The second child uses (6.2) at r=0.
For r>=1, c*theta<1 gives
c*d_j*theta^r<=d_j*theta^(r-1). The first child gives at most theta^r,
the bridge then gives at most d_j*theta^r, and the second child gives
theta^(r+1). This proves the full geometric family simultaneously.

For 0<R<=d_m, choose r with
d_m*theta^(r+1)<R<=d_m*theta^r. Then

    U_m(R)<=R/d_m.                                 (6.3)

Every unit-Huber history containing a strict crossing has terminal
radius at most a. At a level-k crossing the landing is at most d_k.
For k>=1 the full following H_k gives at most theta<a. For k=0 the
landing is at most a. At every actual later ancestor l>=1, the input
is at most a<1, the bridge lands at most a*d_l<=d_l, and the full H_l
again gives at most theta. This proves the assertion with the exact
suffix (3.5), including all returns.

For R<=d_m, 2J_1(U_m(R))<=U_m(R)^2<=R^2/d_m^2.
For R>=d_m and a crossing, it is at most a^2/R^2<=a^2/d_m^2.
For sufficiently large m as here, d_m^2>D: by (3.2), D<2d_m/a,
while d_m>2/a. Hence both crossing estimates are below1/D.
A no-crossing path has terminal radius t with
R>=t+S*min(t,1). For t<=1 use t^2<=R^2/(1+S)^2<=R^2/D.
For t>=1 use

    (t+S)^2-D*(2t-1)=(t-S-1)^2>=0.

Thus the ENTIRE unit-Huber all-start profile is at most1/D.
The saturated start R=D attains1/D. Rescaling proves the same exact
profile for every B>0. This is a self-contained joint reference proof,
not an invocation of an earlier unverified report.

## 7. Whole all-start outward-kink comparison region

Always G(r)<=min(r,B), so F(r)<=J_B(r). A strict crossing at radius r
has landing magnitude

    h*G(r)-r <=(h-1)*G(r)<=d*min(r,B).              (7.1)

For an interior noncrossing query, its largest possible staying output
is [1-h*kappa]_+*min(R,1). In the middle this follows because
r-h*(r-v)=1-h*kappa-d*(r-1), which decreases with r.

Under (1.2), for every physical h>=ell,

    [1-h*kappa]_+<=d,
    [1-h*kappa]_+<=d*B.                            (7.2)

The first follows at h=ell from kappa>=kappa_0, and the second at
h=ell from ell*kappa+aB>=1; increasing h preserves both.
For R<=B use the first inequality and min(R,1)<=R.
For R>=B use the second inequality.
Together with (7.1) and the actual tail stay, this gives

    E_h(R)<=max(d*min(R,B),R-hB)=U_h,B(R).          (7.3)

The unit-Huber radius maps are nondecreasing. Chronological composition
therefore gives M_m(R)<=U_m,B(R). Since F<=J_B, Section6 proves (1.3)
for every start, every allowed B in (1.2), and every enclosing m>=1536.

If kappa>=kappa_1, then
1-ell*kappa<=a*kappa<=aB; also kappa_1>kappa_0, because the
difference in the cross-multiplied numerators is2a^2>0.
If B>=1 and kappa>=kappa_0, then
ell*kappa+aB>=1-a+a=1. This proves the two stated convenient regions.
For example, kappa>=3/5 is an m-independent sufficient curvature range,
since a>2/5 gives kappa_1<5/9<3/5.

## 8. All core starts: literal prefix invariance and objective margin

This subsection is uniform in ALL 0<kappa<=1. Define P_j as in Section1.
We prove jointly

    P_j<=1,
    kappa*d_j*P_j<=theta for j>=1,                 (8.1)

and that every prefix product of factors |1-kappa*h| in H_j is at most1.

At j=1, kappa*|1-ell*kappa|<=a.
On the nonnegative-factor interval the quadratic kappa*(1-ell*kappa)
has maximum1/(4ell)<a. On the negative-factor interval
kappa*(ell*kappa-1) increases up to a at kappa=1.
Since d_1=beta, the second bound in (8.1) follows.

Suppose the level-j bridge is a reversal, kappa*(1+d_j)>=1.
Put t=kappa*d_j. Then |1-kappa*(1+d_j)|<=t, and

    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)
       <=c*(t*P_j)^2<=c*theta^2=theta*beta^2<theta.

The bridge applied after the first child has absolute multiplier
at most t*P_j<=theta<1. Inductively, all prefixes of the second
child multiply this by at most1, proving prefix invariance.

If the level-j bridge is nonreversing, EVERY effective step in
H_(j+1) lies in [0,1]. Thus

    P_(j+1)<=exp(-kappa*S_(j+1)),
    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)
      <=1/(e*(S_(j+1)/d_(j+1)))<5/14<theta,

using (3.3) and e>2. Prefix products are plainly at most1 in this case.
The j=0 bridge has multiplier at most1 directly.
These arguments prove all assertions, including equality/zero factors.

Starting with |s|<=1, every resulting quadratic prefix has radius<=1;
induction on actual physical indices therefore shows that it is also
the trajectory of the current kink function. Hence the payoff is exactly
kappa*P_m^2, with no radius reset or altered child parameter.

If kappa*h_(m-1)^lev<=1, then

    kappa*P_m^2<=kappa*exp(-2kappa*S)<=1/(2eS),
    D*kappa*P_m^2<(1+1/(2S))/2<3/4.

If kappa*h_(m-1)^lev>1, (8.1) and D<2d_m/a give

    D*kappa*P_m^2
       <2a*beta^2*(1/d_m+1/c)<31/60<3/4.

Here d_m>10 for m>=1536, a<1/2 and c>12/5 suffice.
This proves (1.4), uniformly even when kappa depends arbitrarily on m.
It also proves that a complete H_j begun inside the core never increases
radius or gradient magnitude and remains core at every physical prefix.

## 9. Entire odd family: exact cap capture and unscaled child plateau

There is an exact cap-level interval capture valid for every kappa,B:

    M_j(u+B*S_j)=u,
    M_j(R)=R-B*S_j for R>=u+B*S_j,
    M_j(R)<=max(u,R-B*S_j) for every R>=0.          (9.1)

An exterior start stays saturated at all queries and gives the exterior
formula. For the inductive capture input u+B*(2S_j+h_j^lev), the first
child gives u+B*(S_j+h_j^lev). The bridge's staying endpoint is
u+B*S_j. Its largest crossing is at most d_j*B, which is smaller
than (1+S_j)*B<=u+B*S_j by u>=B and (3.2). An interior same-side
endpoint is at most1<=u. The second child therefore gives exactly u.

We now prove (1.5) by induction, together with the following capture
consequence using proper-child plateaus:

    a crossing in a complete H_j
       => terminal gradient magnitude <=a*B.      (9.2)

At any level-k crossing, (7.1) bounds its landing radius by d_k*B.
If k>=1, the complete following H_k and its plateau give gradient<=aB.
If k=0 the landing radius is at most aB, so G of the landing is <=aB.

At each later ancestor l>=1, suppose the input gradient is <=aB<B.
The input cannot be a saturated tail point. If its bridge crosses,
(7.1) bounds its landing by a*d_l*B<=d_l*B; the full H_l plateau
gives gradient<=aB. If its bridge does not cross, the resulting point
is inside the core: this is immediate for a core source, while a middle
source r>=1 has staying value
1-h_l^lev*kappa-d_l*(r-1)<=1. Its gradient has decreased.
Section8 shows the full following H_l remains core and does not increase
that gradient. A zero hit stays zero. This proves (9.2) along (3.5).
When used for H_j it invokes plateaus only at levels <=j-1.

For the base j=1, input d_1 B=beta B has no positive tail stay.
Every crossing lands at radius<=a*beta*B. A positive interior stay
has output gradient at most
kappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+*beta*B<=a*beta*B.
The core negative case uses the base inequality in Section8.
Thus G(M_1(d_1B))<=theta B<aB.

Suppose the plateau is known through j>=1 and start the parent at any
radius R<=c*d_j*B. The first-child image is bounded by (9.1).
At the parent bridge:

* A crossing lands within d_jB.
* A saturated same-side stay has radius at most
  c*d_jB-(S_j+h_j^lev)B<=d_jB, by (3.2).
* An interior same-side stay lies inside the core.

For the saturated case, the first child cannot have contained a crossing:
(9.2), using only proper-child plateaus, would make its terminal gradient
at most aB<B. Its first child is therefore noncrossing and ends at radius
y>=u. Every earlier radius is at least y, so the ENTIRE first child is
saturated and y=R-S_jB exactly. This justifies the displayed bound even
at y=u; the max(u,R-S_jB) estimate alone would not suffice.

The first two cases are handled by the complete second child's plateau.
For the third case, if the first child already contained a crossing,
(9.2) gives gradient<=aB before the bridge; the bridge and the full
core second child cannot increase it.

It remains to handle a first child with no crossing followed by an
interior noncrossing bridge. Let y be the first-child radius and z
its post-bridge radius. Since all first-child radii decrease,

    R>=y+S_j*G(y)>=(1+kappa*S_j)*y,
    z=y-h_j^lev*G(y)<=(1-kappa*h_j^lev)*y.

For z>0 this forces kappa*h_j^lev<1, so every core factor in H_j
is nonnegative and P_j<=exp(-kappa*S_j). Put
t=kappa*d_j and L_j=S_j/d_j. The terminal gradient is bounded by

    kappa*P_j*z
     <= c*t*(1-kappa-t)*exp(-L_j*t)/(1+L_j*t)*B
     <= c*t*(1-t)/(1+L_j*t)^2*B
     <= c/[4*(1+L_j)]*B
     <85/336*B<2/5*B<aB.                           (9.3)

The penultimate inequality follows from the exact square identity

    (1+L*t)^2-4*(1+L)*t*(1-t)=(1-(L+2)*t)^2.

This completes the plateau induction for EVERY j and EVERY positive
kappa,B, with all repeated returns included. It is a quantitative
all-depth result, not just the interval recurrence.

To see its limitation, let r_a=G^(-1)(aB), the unique pre-cap radius
having gradient aB. Its true objective is

    2F(r_a)=a^2*B^2/kappa,                  aB<=kappa,
    2F(r_a)=a^2*B^2+kappa*(1-kappa),        aB>=kappa. (9.4)

Consequently (9.2) gives at most 2F(r_a)/R^2 for a start of radius R.
This does NOT control all small R relative to B. The additive middle
area in (9.4) must not be discarded.

## 10. Additional complete trajectory regions

A same-side noncrossing segment of mass T from radius R to terminal
radius r, with terminal gradient p=G(r), satisfies

    R>=r+T*p,
    2F(r)<=2r*p-p^2.

The second inequality is the actual optimum row from Section2.
Hence 2F(r)<=2R*p-(1+2T)*p^2<=R^2/(1+2T).
This proves the full-word no-crossing claim for every kappa,B.

For exactly one strict crossing, let it be a level-k step h=1+d_k,
with actual prefix/suffix masses P,T, source radius y and gradient p.
The noncrossing prefix gives R>=y+Pp>=(1+P)p.
Its crossing lands at z=h*p-y<=d_k*p, and the noncrossing suffix gives

    2F(terminal)/R^2
      <=d_k^2/[(1+P)^2*(1+2T)].                    (10.1)

Set C=A_k, N=S_m-S_(k+1), x=P-S_k, so 0<=x<=N and T=S_k+N-x.
The denominator is

    (C+x)^2*(2C-1+2N-2x).

Its derivative is 2(C+x)*(C-1+2N-3x); its minimum on [0,N]
is at an endpoint. The endpoint difference, value at N minus value
at0, is N*(2C(C-1)+N(2C-1))>=0.
Thus its minimum is C^2*(2C-1+2N). Since
D=4C+2d_k-1+2N and d_k<C/2, at N=0,

    d_k^2*(4C+2d_k-1)/(C^2*(2C-1))
       <(5C-1)/(4*(2C-1))<=1.

Adding the matching 2N terms preserves the comparison because d_k^2<C^2.
This proves (10.1)<=1/D for every physical cut, including k=0.

Finally consider a trajectory every update of which is either a strict
crossing or a same-side stay queried on a saturated tail. Inequality(7.1)
and the exact tail stay bound each actual radius by the unit-Huber
interval step from its preceding radius. Chronological composition and
F<=J_B therefore give payoff<=1/D.

This includes every history whose queried AND terminal points all lie
outside the open core: a middle query r>=1 has positive noncrossing
output at most1-h*kappa<1, so such a stay cannot occur in that history.
It also includes exterior queries followed by a final crossing into
the core. Knot equalities are handled by either adjacent piece.

A final SAME-SIDE middle-to-core entry is a separate case. Its output
radius is at most1-ell*kappa, but this fact does not imply unit-Huber
domination at low curvature. No bound is claimed here for that case
unless another proved region, such as at most one crossing, applies.

## 11. Direct-proof screen, concrete failures, and the remaining target

One-step unit-Huber domination is FALSE on the entire parameter family.
For the single fixed legal law kappa=1/4, B=1 and any 0<R<=1, the
physical leaf produces

    E_ell(R)=(3-a)*R/4 > aR=U_ell,1(R),

because a<1/2. This is a counterexample to a LOCAL comparison lemma,
not a complete-word improving witness. Section8 in fact bounds every
one of these core-start complete-word histories strictly below1/D.

Nor can the outward middle simply be replaced by the clipped kappa
quadratic. For that same law, at R=u=7/4 and any h>=8 the actual
crossing radius is h-7/4. The clipped-kappa comparison with derivative
min(r/4,1) has interval output (h/4-1)*7/4 there, smaller by9h/16.
A level with h>=8 exists in every specified H_m (level4 already suffices).
Again this refutes a step comparison, not the full-word target.

The first direct attempt was to propagate unit-Huber domination.
It succeeds exactly as a sufficient argument on (1.2), and fails as
a universal argument by the first local example.

The second direct attempt was to propagate a cap-based state through
the kink using the physical interval maps. It proves (1.5) and (9.2)
for the ENTIRE family, without assuming fixed curvature in a child.
Its unresolved extension would need small input radii, not just d_jB.
A tempting statement is

    G(M_j(d_j*w))<=a*w for every 0<w<=B.             (11.1)

This report neither proves nor refutes (11.1).
It does NOT follow by rescaling (1.5): the kink remains at radius1,
and replacing B by w changes the function on the entire actual trajectory.
In an attempted geometric induction, a bridge that stays on its current
side lands in the core. The incoming gradient bound w only gives
a physical radius w/kappa. For kappa*h_j^lev<=1 the bridge need not
contract it by an extra factor a. The complete core child is legitimate,
but its factor can be near1 at these low effective levels. The proof
of (9.3) only handles the no-crossing first-child alternative; its
crossing alternative currently carries aB rather than aw.
No theorem-strength small-radius claim is hidden in that case split.

The exact remaining WHOLE-family question is

    sup_(s!=0) 2F(|Psi_m(s)|)/s^2 <=17/(16D)
    for every m>=1536 and every allowed kappa,B,    (11.2)

or the stronger1/D version, or a complete all-m legal counter-witness.
This is an unresolved target, not a technical lemma being assumed.
After removing the proved regions one may restrict attention to:
parameters outside (1.2); |s|>1; at least two strict crossings; and
either a core query before the terminal, or a final positive middle-to-core
entry. The last-entry exception is part of the remaining region; it is not
silently included in the exterior crossing/tail comparison.

The original unrestricted all-dimension/all-function minimax problem
remains open irrespective of whether (11.2) ultimately holds.
The optional asymmetric family is not entered: no decisive full first-law
result authorizes that escalation in this invocation.

## 12. Literature and administrative scope

The memory search identified the current guidance and the preceding
independent-clipped-family draft13022dab8fa067fe. That draft was used for
awareness of closed directions; every argument needed above is rederived
jointly. The accepted fact statements1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201
were read directly in the shared fact graph.

The Matlas query about exact interval bounds returned:
Corollary3.9 of arXiv:2003.04201 (steps <=1/L or backtracking),
Proposition8 of arXiv:2303.03534 (sufficiently small steps),
and Proposition3 of arXiv:1405.7741 (steps <2/L).
None applies to the large fixed steps here; no external theorem is imported.
A web fallback returned silver-schedule/Huber background but no repair of
the kink state. The exact local Taylor-Hendrickx-Glineur arXiv:1502.05666
text, Section2.4 and the two directions of Lemma2 in Section2.5, was read.
It confirms the already audited interpolation context; the actual rows
in this report are independently proved by the integral argument.
The new shape has been reduced to explicit three-piece scalar maps, so
no additional external machinery is being invoked or rejected unseen.

Initial oversized recall output was truncated. The assignment, problem,
current guidance, relevant accepted statements, and relevant portions of
the previous own report were then read in focused calls. No truncated
output is represented as a fully read unrelated finding.

Before final handoff, a self-audit supplied the explicit justification of
the saturated bridge case in Section9: first-child crossing capture rules
out a tail endpoint, forcing the entire first child to be saturated when
the bridge queries the tail. No additional hypothesis or formal call is
used. This amended report supersedes the incomplete exposition in initial
shared proof_attempt0b0daa4d6847279f.

This is the end of the ONE authorized analytical invocation.
No automatic renewal, live TASK overwrite, worker/service control,
outward publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown, or goal completion
was performed. The deliverables are this joint unverified report, its
handoff, glossary, local notes, and shared findings. No new fact_id exists.

## 10. A missing partial-tail clipped-return test rejects this entire family

The previous sections prove survival of the PRE-EXISTING scalar system.
They do not imply a seed, and the following new actual function now
proves that every displayed word is INFEASIBLE in the full original cone.
The original global decision remains open.

Here is a new necessary inequality for every four-step full seed with
h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), all a,b,c,d>0:

                 C b d <= (2+a)(2+c).                 (9)

No ordering, fixed capacity or cut saturation is assumed. Define
alpha=b/(2+c)>0 and the scalar function

 f(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1,
 f(x)=x^2/2 for -alpha<=x<=1,
 f(x)=-alpha*x-alpha^2/2 for x<=-alpha.

Its derivative clamp(x,[-alpha,1]) is continuous, nondecreasing and
1-Lipschitz, so f is one globally convex differentiable1-smooth
function with unique minimizer0. Start at x0=P=2+a. The first four
queries and gradients are exactly

 x0=P,      g0=1,
 x1=1,      g1=1,
 x2=-b,     g2=-alpha,
 x3=-alpha, g3=-alpha.

Indeed x2=-(2+c)alpha and the next step has length1+c.
The last point is x4=d*alpha>0, with g4=min(d*alpha,1).
Every sample of this function satisfies f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2.
Telescoping the actual GD recursion therefore gives the exact primitive
energy V=x0^2-(x4+s*g4)^2, s=C-1.

If d*alpha<=1, then x4=g4 and V=P^2-C^2 d^2 alpha^2.
If d*alpha>1, then g4=1 and x4+s>C>P, giving V<0 directly.
Thus a full original nonnegative flow/PSD certificate necessarily
forces (9). Conversely, if (9) is violated, this exact function has
negative V in either case. Accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10,
then contradicts the full certificate identity, retaining every formal
coordinate and every original multiplier. This argument requires no
unproved prefix or tail heredity.

For h(t) from Section1, d*alpha<15/32<1. The difference between the
two sides of (9) is

 C b d-P(2+c)=3339/2000-(117/80)t>0
                                         for0<=t<=1/100.

Hence the entire family passing the old scalar tests, including C82
and C83 on0<=t<=10^(-12), fails the ORIGINAL full certificate cone.
At t=0 all data are rational:

 alpha=125/232,
 x=(12/5,1,-5/4,-125/232,375/928),
 g=(1,1,-125/232,-125/232,375/928),
 V=(12/5)^2-(2895/928)^2
   =-(3339*25611)/(25*928^2)<0.

Every original interpolation pair is legal because these are the
actual samples of the single explicit smooth convex function above.
This is a concrete missing constraint, not a violation inferred from
independently chosen jets or a numerical Gram.

The old system's global-coverage implication is still refuted by
Sections1--8. The enlarged system including (9) is a new question:
its whole-domain emptiness is NOT proved, and no surviving full seed
has been constructed. In particular this witness must not be turned
into an unsupported statement that every four-step seed is impossible.

## 11. Exact elimination of the entire lower-clipping parameter

The new inequality is not an isolated parameter choice. The whole
one-parameter scalar family can be eliminated exactly. Let
h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), with all shifts positive, P=2+a,
H=3+c+d, C=P+b+H. Assume cd<1; the earlier legal last-triangle
condition forces this on any candidate. For every alpha>0, take the
same function with derivative clamp(x,[-alpha,1]), start at x0=P,
and perform all four original steps. Then its energy is nonnegative
for EVERY alpha>0 if and only if the following conditions all hold:

 C b/H <=P,
 C b d/(2+c)<=P,
 C |bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P,
 and, when bc>=1, C d<=P.                              (10)

This equivalence concerns only this particular one-dimensional function
family; it is not a characterization of full primitive feasibility.
Every strict violation gives a finite positive alpha and one actual
negative-energy function. Equality and zero limits are retained.

Proof. Every sample of every function in this family has saturated
value f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. Thus V=P^2-Y(alpha)^2, where
Y(alpha)=x4+s g4, s=C-1. The first two steps reach x1=1 and x2=-b.
For alpha>=b the remainder is independent of alpha: x3=bc and
x4=bc-(1+d)min(bc,1). The assumption cd<1 guarantees x4>=-b:
if bc<=1 use x4=-bcd; if bc>=1, then b>=1/c>d and x4>=-d>-b.
Consequently the final lower clipping is inactive. If x4>1 then
Y=x4+s>C>P and the third condition in (10) fails too. Otherwise
Y=C x4, exactly the third condition.

For 0<alpha<=b, g2=-alpha and x3=-b+(1+c)alpha. Split at

 alpha_A=b/H, alpha_B=b/(2+c),
 alpha_C=(b+1)/(1+c) when bc>=1.

Their relevant order is 0<alpha_A<alpha_B<=b, and, when defined,
alpha_B<alpha_C<=b. For alpha<=alpha_B the third query remains
on the lower flat part, so g3=-alpha and x4=-b+(2+c+d)alpha.
For alpha<=alpha_A one has g4=-alpha and
Y=-b-(P+b)alpha, a decreasing function, reaching -C b/H.
From alpha_A to alpha_B, x4 increases and g4 follows x4 until
possibly reaching the upper cap1. Thus Y increases throughout that
interval. Its endpoint at alpha_B has x4=d b/(2+c), with
Y=C d b/(2+c) if x4<=1, and Y=x4+s otherwise. If x4>1 the
second condition in (10) and nonnegative energy both fail. The
limit |Y(0)|=b is smaller than C b/H since C>H.

For alpha between alpha_B and min(b,alpha_C), with alpha_C omitted
when bc<1, the third query is on the quadratic part: g3=x3 and
x4=-d x3 decreases with alpha. On each final clipping branch,
Y is one of x4+s, C x4, or x4-s alpha. Each is decreasing, and
they agree at their boundaries. Thus |Y| attains its maximum at
an endpoint of this interval. At its lower endpoint we already
have the second condition. If its upper endpoint is b, we already
have the third condition. Otherwise x3=1 at alpha_C, and cd<1
implies alpha_C>=1/c>d; hence x4=g4=-d and Y=-C d.

For alpha_C<=alpha<=b when bc>=1, g3=1, and x4=x3-(1+d)
increases with alpha. Also x4+alpha>0 at alpha_C and increases,
so the final lower cap stays inactive. Y increases, first as C x4
and then, if x4>1, as x4+s. Again its absolute maximum is at an
endpoint, already covered by the last and third conditions.
These regimes exhaust every alpha, and prove (10).

For the compact candidate box b<1552559/1081453<2<2+c, the second
condition is implied by Cd<=P when bc>=1. On bc<=1, the third
condition is Cbcd<=P, so the two new/old return constraints are
precisely

 C b d <= P min(2+c,1/c).                              (11)

The max over c>0 of min(2+c,1/c) is1+sqrt(2), attained only at
c=sqrt(2)-1. This follows from the increasing/decreasing branches
and their unique positive intersection. With the separate full
quadratic bound Cabcd<=1 one can strengthen (11) to

 C b d <= P min(2+c,1/(m c)), m=max(1,a P),
 C b d <= P [1+sqrt(1+1/m)].                           (12)

These are necessary inequalities only; no end of the capacity range
is fixed. The first inequality in (12) retains the actual c and is
stronger than replacing it by its maximum in the second.

## 12. Remaining global endpoint/slack problem

The actual enlarged necessary region consists of every prior scalar
condition, all C82/C83 branches, and now (10), with all four variables
C,a,b,d free and c=C-5-a-b-d. No interval of that enlarged region
has been discarded merely because an extra inequality is strict.
The scalar-system survivor from Sections1--8 is explicitly rejected by
(9), so it must not be submitted as an actual full seed.

For the branch bc<=1 one useful exact remaining reduction is as follows.
Put kappa=max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0), R=P^2-kappa,
J=P^2-kappa*(1+b/(3+c+d)), Ksum=c+d,
A=b[R+b(P+C)]/J, and y=Cb/P. The existing middle lift, and the
new partial-tail return, respectively say

 d >= (A Ksum-2)/(A+1),
 d <= (Ksum+2)/(y+1).                                 (13)

All denominators are positive: J is the squared initial norm of the
explicit backward-lift witness, and P,b>0. In particular interval
nonemptiness implies

 Ksum(A y-1)<=2(A+y+2).                               (14)

The clipped return and unit quadratic also give

 d(Ksum-d)<=1/(max(1,aP)*y).                           (15)

Equations(13)--(15) keep both interval endpoints, including the case
where the lower bound in(13) is negative. Product concavity may be used
only after intersecting(13) with the original positive endpoint bounds;
one cannot minimize at an inadmissible endpoint or assume saturation.
The branch bc>=1 retains Cd<=P and the other original inequalities.
No proof that this enlarged four-variable system is empty was obtained
in this round. No actual Lambda/M certificate was obtained either.

# Exact absorption of the actual two-Huber ruler tail

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit was
allocated or called; there is no new fact_id. A mathematical statement in
this document is a candidate with its full proof, not verifier-accepted
truth. The original all-word/all-horizon minimax problem remains OPEN.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; one allocated round after
completed56, completed ceiling57 / attempted ceiling58. Assignment TASK.md;
guidance5f75454952cf10c5; plan8ad15c87bcebe740. No automatic renewal.
Latest guidance reread7b252271b54d4ab9 leaves this assignment unchanged.

Frozen source candidate: shared finding4c4a21a484564bdc, complete public
text research/shared_evidence/4c4a21a484564bdc.md, 39298 bytes,
SHA25607140ce4efba7c8237ae2dfff447fe2afbadbbe94154a82db82d504640f2b79b.
It was read in full. It is UNVERIFIED and is not an accepted predecessor.
All construction, smoothness, trajectory, local-bound and radius ingredients
used here are proved jointly below. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 is used
ONLY for the original benchmark ray limit and its stated enclosure.

## 1. The resulting theorem and its exact scope

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), d=lambda-2=1/lambda. For integer m>=32 and

    1/5 <= tau <= 1/4,       8/m <= beta <= 1/4,

use the SAME enclosing parameters throughout the physical word:

    eta_m=1-tau/m, c_m=lambda(1+tau/m), a_m=eta_m/c_m,
    b_j=1+a_m c_m^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1, J=m-2, L=m-1.             (1.1)

Here beta is beta_arc, not eta_m. Select ONLY the first occurrences of
levels m-2 and m-1. The entire physical word is exactly

    H_m=(H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1)).                (1.2)

Use the direct circular construction in Sections2--4, unchanged from the
frozen candidate. This is an actual convex1-smooth function F on R^3,
initialized at e_1, and all steps are physical GD steps x^+=x-h grad F.
Write E_m(tau,beta)=2F(x_(n_m))/dist(e_1,argmin F)^2.

There is one finite integer m_0 such that for EVERY m>=m_0 and EVERY
(tau,beta) in the displayed rectangle, the two old circular components
are both exactly zero at the actual FINAL iterate. In fact the old pair
enters its moving flat interval by the end of H_(m-1) and thereafter is
stationary. No tail step, old component or radius term is discarded.
Consequently its full actual payoff is exactly

    E_m(tau,beta)=D_3^2/[2(1+S_(m-1)) R_true^2],      (1.3)

with D_3 and R_true defined in Sections3--4.

Let B_n be the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark in Section8, and let the
positive finite Z_1=lim_m B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m be supplied by accepted
fact04219554e41d9750. Set C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta). Uniformly on the
same moving parameter rectangle,

    B_(n_m) E_m(tau,beta)
       - Z_1 exp(-tau)/(2 lambda C_beta^4) -> 0.      (1.4)

In particular, for every FIXED beta in(0,1/4],

    lim_m B_(n_m) E_m(1/4,beta)
       =Z_1 exp(-1/4)/(2 lambda C_beta^4)<7/10.       (1.5)

The same argument controls the explicitly specified nonfixed scaling
beta_m=8/m. More precisely,

    lim_m sup_(8/m<=beta<=1/4) B_(n_m) E_m(1/4,beta)
       =Z_1 exp(-1/4)/(2 lambda)<7/10.               (1.6)

Thus the supremum in(1.6) is below7/10 for every sufficiently large m.
Over the full tau rectangle the corresponding limit of the supremum is
Z_1 exp(-1/5)/(2lambda)<3/4, also giving an eventual uniform deficit.

These are upper bounds on this ONE unchanged three-coordinate witness
family. They are NOT upper bounds on W_n, and do not settle the original
all-n/all-nonnegative-word minimax question. They do not control beta<8/m
(for example beta=1/m), other checkpoint selections or counts, or other
static functions. The smaller-beta region remains open in this report.
No positive pre-tail limit has been added to the terminal payoff.

## 2. The actual circular body and finite local construction

Fix beta in(0,1/4]. Define

    R_circ=1+beta, C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta),
    p(q)=beta+sqrt(R_circ^2-(q+beta)^2), 0<=q<=1,
    v(q)=(p(q),-q),
    K_ctr=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).           (2.1)

The circular center is(beta,beta); its disk contains0 because
2beta^2<=(1+beta)^2. Thus its radial normals support this convex hull
on the displayed arc. The endpoints are(C_beta,0) and(beta,-1).
For every(p,-q) in K_ctr,

    0<=p<=C_beta, 0<=q<=1, p>=beta*q,
    (p,0) belongs to K_ctr.                         (2.2)

For any compact convex K containing0 put

    E_K(r)=max_(g in K){<g,r>-||g||^2/2}.             (2.3)

Completing the square identifies its unique maximizer as the Euclidean
projection Pi_K(r). Its characterization is

    <r-Pi_K(r),g-Pi_K(r)> <=0 for every g in K.       (2.4)

Adding this inequality at two points shows
||Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s)||^2<=<Pi_K(r)-Pi_K(s),r-s>.
The projection is therefore1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing
expressions in(2.3) gives the derivative Pi_K(r) and a remainder bounded
by ||s-r||^2. Hence E_K is convex, nonnegative, differentiable and1-smooth.
Its zero set is the polar cone of K: positive support in a direction
produces a positive value using a sufficiently small multiple of that
supporting vector, while nonpositive support makes0 the maximizer.
The cone of K_ctr is generated by its two nonzero endpoints, since

    (p,-q)=((p-beta*q)/C_beta)(C_beta,0)+q(beta,-1).

For sigma>0 it follows that

    E_(sigma K_ctr)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U<=V.   (2.5)

Use the one-sided Huber function

    Hub_delta(z)=0 for z<=0;
                 z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta;
                 delta*z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta.    (2.6)

It is convex and1-smooth, with derivative min(delta,max(z,0)).

Here is a finite construction for the ACTUAL ordinary block H_J, with
its ordered steps h_1,...,h_N, N=2^J-1. Set alpha_i=h_i/2. Construct
r_i=(u_i,-y_i) backward from

    r_N=(beta,-1), q_N=1.

For each i from N down to1 choose the unique q_(i-1) in[0,1) satisfying

    (q_(i-1)+beta)/(p(q_(i-1))-beta)
       =max{ beta/(C_beta-beta),
               [y_i+(1-alpha_i)beta]
                    /[u_i-(1-alpha_i)beta] },      (2.7)

and set

    r_(i-1)=r_i+alpha_i v(q_(i-1)).                 (2.8)

All our physical steps are positive. The denominator in(2.7) is positive
because u_i>=beta and alpha_i>0. Its left side is continuous and strictly
increasing from beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity. Thus every term is defined.
The symbols specify a finite mathematical recursion; it was not executed.

For clarity, the projection checks underlying this construction are as
follows. Inductively u_i>=beta and y_i>=1, and the updates increase both
when read backward. At an interior projected arc point the centered ray
has ratio(y_i+beta)/(u_i-beta). Subtracting alpha_i*beta from its numerator
and adding alpha_i*beta to its denominator can only decrease that ratio.
Thus(2.7) gives q_(i-1)<=q_i. At q_i=1 this upper bound is immediate. At
the capped endpoint q_i=0 the supporting condition is

    y_i<=beta(u_i-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta).             (2.9)

It makes the second entry in the maximum in(2.7) no greater than the first,
so q_(i-1)=0 as well. At an uncapped new point, (2.7)--(2.8) place r_(i-1)
on the outward radial ray of v(q_(i-1)): the cross-product condition is
exactly(2.7), and y_(i-1)>=1>=q_(i-1) gives a radial factor at least1.
The circular normal then proves(2.4). At the capped new point, the maximum
condition instead gives(2.9) for i-1. The displacement from(C_beta,0) is a
nonnegative combination of the circular endpoint normal and(0,1), which
also proves(2.4). The terminal r_N is on K_ctr. We have proved

    Pi_(K_ctr)(r_i)=v(q_i),
    u_i>=beta, 1<=y_i<=y_0, 0<=q_i<=1.             (2.10)

In particular, with u=u_0, y=y_0 and S=S_J,

    u=beta+sum_i alpha_i p(q_(i-1)),
    y=1+sum_i alpha_i q_(i-1),
    u>=beta*y, M=u+y/beta>0.                       (2.11)

The inequality is termwise p>=beta*q. For a general incoming threshold
ell>=0 and amplitude D>0 define

    sigma=D/M, ell_out=sigma*y,
    A=ell+ell_out/beta,
    Phi(X,Y)=E_(sigma K_ctr)(X-A,Y-ell_out).        (2.12)

This is one fixed finite smooth convex function, zero whenever X<=ell
and Y>=0 by(2.5). Also its OUTPUT derivative is zero whenever Y>=ell_out,
for every X. Indeed replacing a gradient(p,-q) by(p,0) increases the
maximized expression by q(Y-ell_out)+q^2/2 if q>0.

The physical ordinary states are

    (X_i,Y_i)=(A+sigma*u_i,ell_out-sigma*y_i).       (2.13)

They start at(ell+D,0), and(2.8)--(2.10) show that each physical step h_i
updates them by -(h_i/2)grad Phi. Each query has X_i>ell and
0<=Y_i<=ell_out-sigma<ell_out. The final local gradient is
sigma(beta,-1). A physical checkpoint b>2, with T=b/2-1, sends it to

    (X^+,Y^+)=(A-beta*sigma*T,ell_out+sigma*T).      (2.14)

This is an exact half-step identity for the original physical b. It is
in the zero set(2.5). Nothing here substitutes a shortened physical word.

## 3. One static global function, every step, and its radius

For our identical ordinary blocks H_J, use the SAME u,y,M in both.
Put

    T_1=b_J/2-1, T_2=b_(J+1)/2-1,
    sigma_1=1/M, D_2=T_1/M,
    sigma_2=D_2/M, D_3=T_1*T_2/M^2,
    ell_2=y/M, ell_3=D_2*y/M,
    A_1=ell_2/beta, A_2=ell_2+ell_3/beta,
    delta=D_3/(1+S_L).                            (3.1)

For sufficiently large m the two checkpoints exceed2; all the parameters
are then positive. Define, before starting GD,

    Phi_1(X,Y)=E_(sigma_1 K_ctr)(X-A_1,Y-ell_2),
    Phi_2(Y,Z)=E_(sigma_2 K_ctr)(Y-A_2,Z-ell_3),
    F(X,Y,Z)=(Phi_1(X,Y)+Phi_2(Y,Z)
                                      +Hub_delta(Z-ell_3))/2. (3.2)

The function is convex and1-smooth. To check the constant, the linear map
stacking(X,Y),(Y,Z),Z has norm sqrt(2), since its squared output norm is
X^2+2Y^2+2Z^2. The separable sum of the three1-smooth functions has gradient
Lipschitz constant1 on the product space. Composition and the factor1/2
therefore give at most(1/2)*2=1. Every summand is nonnegative and F(0)=0.

At any point write the two projections as(P_1,-Q_1),(P_2,-Q_2), and put
P_3=min(delta,max(Z-ell_3,0)). The exact full update is

    X^+=X-h P_1/2,
    Y^+=Y-h(P_2-Q_1)/2,
    Z^+=Z-h(P_3-Q_2)/2.                            (3.3)

All components in this formula belong to the SAME static function.
During the first H_J and b_J the first pair follows(2.13)--(2.14);
its output is at most ell_2 before the checkpoint. The next component
and final Huber are zero there, so they do not change these updates.
The first checkpoint gives

    X=A_1-beta*D_2, Y=ell_2+D_2, Z=0.

During the next H_J and its root checkpoint b_(J+1), the second pair
follows(2.13)--(2.14), with ell=ell_2,D=D_2. The first component stays
zero in the second ordinary block: its input is fixed at or below A_1,
and its output is at least ell_2. In particular Q_1=0 at every active
query. The final Huber is inactive while the third coordinate is below
ell_3. Therefore the actual state just after the root checkpoint is

    v_in=A_1-beta*D_2,
    w_in=A_2-beta*D_3,
    Z_in=ell_3+D_3.                                (3.4)

This derives the incoming data without importing an unverified trajectory.

The ENTIRE minimum set is the simultaneous zero set of the summands:

    X<=A_1, Y>=beta*X,
    Y<=A_2, Z>=beta*(Y-ell_2),
    Z<=ell_3.                                     (3.5)

Here(2.5) and beta*A_1=ell_2 were used. From(2.11),

    1-A_1=sigma_1*u, ell_2=sigma_1*y,
    0<A_1<=1/(1+beta^2).                           (3.6)

The point x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0) belongs to(3.5). For any point of(3.5),
if X<0 its squared distance from e_1 is at least1. For 0<=X<=A_1,
its squared distance is at least(1-X)^2+beta^2 X^2, because Y>=beta X.
This quadratic decreases until X=1/(1+beta^2), so it is minimized on
[0,A_1] at A_1. The displayed x_star attains this bound, which is at most1.
Thus the TRUE closest-minimizer squared radius is exactly

    R_true^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2
            =sigma_1^2(u^2+y^2)>0.                 (3.7)

This uses every constraint in the actual minimum set. The radius has not
been normalized using a freely chosen more distant minimizer.

Every smooth-convex interpolation row follows from this actual function.
For completeness, subtract <grad F(y),z> from F(z). Its minimum is attained
at y. The1-smooth descent bound at x with displacement
-(grad F(x)-grad F(y)) then yields
F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y> >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
This holds for every ordered pair, including x_star and the terminal sample.
Actual Euclidean Gram matrices are positive semidefinite.
For general smoothness L_smooth>0, take f=L_smooth F and physical updates
